# Game Companion: State Extraction Tooling

Status: signed off 2026-08-09 21:40, content: 0a7fe7de Date: 2026-08-07

## 1. Purpose and context

The Game Companion lets Claude follow a Disco Elysium playthrough and give spoiler-safe guidance. This spec covers the state-extraction layer only: turning raw save files into a game-neutral JSON digest plus a "what changed since you last asked" diff, both consumed by future Companion chat sessions. Guidance behavior itself is out of scope except for the standing rules recorded in `companion/AGENTS.md`.

Version terminology for numbered persisted-document and envelope fields is closed: every such version names its namespace explicitly as `schemaVersion`, `adapterVersion`, or `turnContextVersion`, or as one of the 7.6 fields `stateEventVersion`, `consumerVersion`, and `outboxLedgerVersion`. Versioned framing tags independently namespace evolvable hash recipes, including `companion-source-domain-v2` and `companion-walk-policy-v1`. Delivery boundaries use terms such as "current design" or "current adapter scope"; unqualified numeric shorthand labels are not used.

The user is mid-playthrough. All tooling and documentation must obey spoiler discipline: no dialogue text, no plot-revealing variable names in anything the tooling prints during normal operation or in the docs themselves. Structural and mechanical examples only.

## Operating context

Rigor profile inputs (judgment boundaries recorded with their basis; derivation per the deterministic rule in the nightshift revise skill's `rigor.js`):

- Deployment environment and operational criticality: local, single-machine tooling invoked by the companion assistant during live play sessions; no external deployment, no service surface. Criticality is session-level (a broken pipeline blocks guidance for the current sitting, nothing beyond it). Uplift judged NOT fired.
- Audience: personal use (one player, one machine). Baseline tier: low.
- Failure consequence and data or security sensitivity: a lost or duplicated diff breaks the never-loss/duplicate-show guidance contract that the whole design exists to uphold, and the input saves are sacred read-only originals; data is local and personal with no security exposure. Uplift judged FIRED on failure consequence.
- Concurrency and compatibility risk: watcher, one-shot, and consuming readers interleave under `cycle.lock`/`consume.lock`; schema and adapter versions evolve in lockstep across core and mappers. Uplift judged FIRED.
- Reversibility and recovery cost: state files self-heal from the save history (the pipeline can always re-derive current state); only baseline continuity is at stake and is protected by the typed reset rules. Uplift judged NOT fired.
- Expected feature lifetime: playthrough-scoped bridge components (the turn-context wrapper is explicitly temporary) over a durable game-agnostic core. Uplift judged NOT fired.

Derived profile (`rigor.js "personal use" 2`): tier HIGH; validation, recovery, compatibility, observability, and proof effort all high. This matches the engineered shape of the design (typed failure taxonomies, atomic replacement, lock protocols, exhaustive verification claims in section 11).

## 2. Hard constraints

- The Disco Elysium directory (`C:\Users\asten\AppData\LocalLow\ZAUM Studio\Disco Elysium`) is read-only for this project. Save files are NEVER modified, moved, or deleted.
- Saves are copied into `C:\Git\disco-elysium-companion\.tmp\` before being read (unzipped or parsed). Nothing ever opens a file under `SaveGames\` for write.
- The dump tool adapts the existing Go reference parser from github.com/ig4e/disco-elysium-editor (decision made with the user 2026-08-07: the C# parser assumed by the original brief does not exist anywhere in that repo's ecosystem; the fork is Tauri/Rust plus Go, the upstream Adversarian repo is Python). The binary format is not reimplemented in another language; the vendored parser is extended only where the reference implementation is demonstrably incomplete or conflicts with this tool's fail-closed contract (complete deviation list in section 5.2).
- Glue (watching, diffing, mapping) is PowerShell 7.5 or later (`pwsh -NoProfile`), using the `?` and `%` aliases per user style. Everything downstream of the JSON dump operates on JSON only. The shared module-owned literal `CompanionJsonMaxDepth` is 64. Container depth counts a root object or array as level 1; a primitive root has depth 0. `Read-CompanionJson`, its duplicate-property prepass, `JsonDocument`, `ConvertFrom-Json -Depth 64`, and `ConvertTo-CompanionJson` enforce that same generic document limit. A valid digest has a structural maximum depth of 62, a standalone diff has a maximum depth of 63, and a turn-context envelope may use the generic maximum of 64. The two-level digest reserve closes the embedding chain: an emitted child below `/extra` moves one level deeper when placed under `extraChanges[].from` or `.to`, then the diff may sit under the envelope. A document that exceeds its applicable limit fails instead of truncating.

  `ConvertTo-CompanionJson` writes through `System.Text.Json.Utf8JsonWriter` with `MaxDepth = 64`; production code never calls `ConvertTo-Json`. Materialized known values serialize normally. An embedded owned `JsonElement` writes through `JsonElement.WriteTo`, preserving its exact JSON value without a PowerShell numeric round trip. Adapter mappers written in PowerShell import that helper via the relative path `..\..\core\DigestDiff.psm1` from their companion-shaped adapter subtree. The authoring tree and each trusted release package preserve that shape, so the authoring mapper finds companion `core\` while a packaged mapper finds the release-local `core\`. An adapter depending on core is fine (the seam constrains only what CORE may know about adapters, never the reverse), and non-PowerShell adapter tools are outside this rule.

  On the read side, the materialized view uses `ConvertFrom-Json -AsHashtable -DateKind String -Depth 64`. `-AsHashtable` preserves the real variable store's key pair that differs only in letter case (present in both known saves, so game-data constant); plain `ConvertFrom-Json` throws even though the emitted JSON is legal. `-DateKind String` preserves ISO-looking strings exactly until an explicit RoundtripKind parse is required; automatic `DateTime` conversion followed by string parameter binding can discard fractional ticks and UTC kind (probed in section 11). Before that lossy materialization, `Read-CompanionJson` runs a duplicate-property prepass over the raw UTF-8 JSON with `System.Text.Json.Utf8JsonReader`. For every object within the applicable depth limit it compares decoded property names ordinally and rejects a repeated name, including escape-equivalent spellings such as `"x"` and `"\u0078"`; case-distinct names remain legal. The prepass covers objects beneath `extra` and values embedded in `extraChanges`. Without it, PowerShell silently keeps only the last same-case property and an exact-field validator cannot detect the discarded occurrence.
- The core layer is game-agnostic. No Disco Elysium concepts in `core\` or in the digest schema outside the `extra` field. Game-specific tuning that core behavior needs (for example diff-noise exclusions) reaches core only as adapter.json data, never as core code.

## 3. Directory layout

```
companion/
  AGENTS.md                      canonical standing rules for future Companion sessions
  CLAUDE.md                      one-line @AGENTS.md bridge for Claude Code
  core/                          game-agnostic
    digest-schema.md             digest + diff schema documentation
    Watch-Saves.ps1              poll loop; invokes adapter, then differ
    Update-CompanionState.ps1    one-shot: process newest save now
    Get-Diff.ps1                 computes and prints the diff; consuming mode resets baseline
    Get-ValidatedTurnContext.ps1 host-neutral capture, validation, and admission entry point (7.5)
    Get-TurnContext.ps1          freshness-gated consuming transaction child (7.5)
    Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1    idle walk-back: most recent eventful save pair (7.4)
    Publish-StateEvent.ps1       runtime-role consuming transaction; commits a state-event outbox record (7.6)
    Set-StateEventSettlement.ps1 accepts the runtime's settlement of one outbox record (7.6)
    Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1    switches consumer ownership between bridge and runtime (7.6)
    DigestDiff.psm1              digest differ, source ordering, consumption, and JSON helpers
  adapters/
    disco_elysium/
      adapter.json               save dir, save glob, dump/digest command templates, diff exclusions
      dump-tool/                 Go module: save copy in, complete state JSON out (+ build.ps1)
      Build-Digest.ps1           dump JSON -> game-neutral digest JSON
      Validate-Digest.psm1       adapter-owned semantic validator for admitted digests
      game_data/                 vendored reference DBs (variables_reputation.json, skill_key_map.json)
      knowledge.md               game structure notes (spoiler-safe)
      releases/                  trusted, immutable installed adapter packages (section 8)
        <adapterVersion>/
          schema-<schemaVersion>/
            core/                release-local core modules used by packaged adapters
            adapters/
              disco_elysium/     packaged mapper, digest validator, dump executable, and game_data
  state/
    current.json                 latest digest (authoritative)
    baseline.json                digest snapshot at last consuming read (authoritative)
    diff-since-last-read.json    derived cache of diff(baseline, current); see 7.1
    last-processed.json          internal: pipeline bookkeeping (save file name + write time)
    consumer.json                consumer-ownership marker; absent means bridge (7.6)
    outbox/                      state-event outbox: pending records, ledger.json, quarantine/ (7.6)
    consume.lock                 persistent path whose exclusive open serializes consuming readers
    cycle.lock                   persistent path whose exclusive open serializes save-processing cycles
  .tmp/                          work area; pipeline files use the work- prefix (section 8) and may sit beside long-lived scratch (repo clones) the pipeline never touches
  .nightshift/features/          durable feature designs, including state-extraction.md
```

Core ensures `state\` and `.tmp\` exist at watcher/one-shot startup AND at the start of every cycle (`.tmp` is declared disposable, so mid-session deletion must be routine, not a wedge). `Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1` ensures `.tmp\` (only) exists at startup and runs the reaper there, like the one-shot; it never creates or touches `state\` (7.4). `Get-Diff.ps1` touches `state\` and adapter.json. Its state-compatibility configuration profile validates the configured save-directory path as identity input but does not test current directory existence. It does not enumerate or read save files, create directories, run the reaper, or depend on `.tmp\`. `Get-TurnContext.ps1` ensures both directories and runs the reaper because its freshness path may invoke the save pipeline, and 7.6's `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` does the same for the same reason. 7.6's `Set-StateEventSettlement.ps1` and `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1` touch only `state\`: they create `state\` before taking `consume.lock` and `state\outbox\` when needed, never depend on `.tmp\` beyond the entry fence's own lock file, and never run the reaper.

## 4. Source material: what a save contains (established by exploration)

A save is `<Name>.ntwtf.zip` in `SaveGames\` holding five files:

| File | Content | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `*.1st.ntwtf.json` | area id, party state, fog-of-war status cache | digest (session, extra) |
| `*.2nd.ntwtf.json` | character sheet, journal tasks, inventory, thoughts, clock, checks | digest (most fields) |
| `*.ntwtf.lua` | binary TLV: 5 top-level Lua tables; one is the dialogue variable store (~12k vars) | dump; digest (extra) |
| `*.states.lua` | text Lua: `AreaState[...]` and `ShownOrbs[...]` record assignments | dump only (current adapter scope) |
| `*.FOW.json` | `{}` until populated | ignored (current adapter scope) |

Facts the design relies on, verified against the user's real Day 4 save:

- `sunshineClockTimeHolder.time` gives `dayCounter` and `dayMinutes` (minutes since 00:00). The record also carries `realDayCounter` and `seconds`; this design uses `dayCounter` and `dayMinutes` exclusively wherever a day/time pair is built (the two day counters disagree on many resolution records, so mixing them would produce inconsistent output).
- `playerCharacter` gives `Money` (in cents of reAl), `Level`, `XpAmount`, `SkillPoints`, and `healingPools.ENDURANCE` / `.VOLITION`. The healing-pool fields count consumable charges, not current Health or Morale. `XpAmount` is progress within the current level, not cumulative XP: it resets on level-up (observed: level 25 with XpAmount 53), so its delta legitimately goes negative across a level-up.
- `characterSheet` has 4 ability + 24 skill objects (camelCase keys; `skill_key_map.json` maps them to display names). The Endurance and Volition skill records also carry the effective values shown by the current Health and Morale bars. Before a live Morale hit, Endurance was value/max/damage 4/4/0 and Volition was 5/5/0; after the hit, Volition was 4/5/-1 while both healing-pool charge counts remained 2. The digest therefore uses each record's `value`, while `maximumValue`, `damageValue`, and charge counts stay unexposed. `characterSheet.gainedItems` is the player's owned-item id list (65 in the Day 4 save) and `equippedItems` its subset (9). By contrast, `inventoryState.itemListState` is the per-definition state list for ALL 206 items in the game, owned or not; its `isFresh` flag is a not-yet-inspected badge that defaults to true for every catalog entry. Any player-facing item output MUST therefore be filtered through `gainedItems`; `itemListState` alone reveals unowned items.
- The same catalog-vs-owned distinction holds for thoughts: `thoughtCabinetState.thoughtListState` covers all 53 thoughts including never-acquired ones, while the `characterSheet` arrays (`gainedThoughts`, `cookingThoughts`, `fixedThoughts`, `forgottenThoughts`) contain only what the player has encountered. The game removes forgotten ids from `gainedThoughts` (the two sets are disjoint in the real save). Player-facing thought output derives ONLY from the characterSheet arrays.
- `aquiredJournalTasks.TaskAquisitions` maps `TASK.*` ids to acquisition timestamps. `TaskResolutions` maps the same ids to a resolution timestamp when resolved and to JSON `null` when not (real Day 4 save: 69 keys, 51 timestamped, 18 null). The editor repo's SCHEMA_REFERENCE.md documents unresolved entries as empty objects `{}` instead; real saves on this machine uniformly use null, so the mapper treats "no `dayCounter` value present" as unresolved, which covers both encodings. `SubtaskAquisitions` maps task id to a map of subtask id -> timestamp, and every such parent id is a key in `TaskAquisitions`. `TaskNewStates` is only the journal "NEW" badge; it is not a status source.
- Journal task ids and their TLV counterparts use the uppercase prefixes `TASK.` and `XP.`; location-scoped TLV variables use lowercase prefixes (for example `whirling.`).
- `states.lua` begins with two blank lines in every save inspected (both real saves and the editor repo's example, read from the raw zip bytes); content lines are LF-ended Lua record assignments, not bare integers. Observed field shapes in the real save's `AreaState` entries: `{LocationState=<int>}` (89), `{emptied=<bool>}` (57), `{HasEntered=<bool>}` (56), `{disappear=<bool>}` (44). `ShownOrbs` values are `{OrbSeen=<int>}` records. The dump preserves records as parsed field-value objects rather than assuming one shape.
- The TLV file parses into 5 top-level tables; counts in the real save: 440 entries (actor-shaped), 259 entries (item and thought definitions: 206 + 53), empty, 12153 (variable store), 1501 (conversation metadata). The repo's example Day 1 save parses to the same layout (12145 variables). Exact identities of the non-variable tables are recorded in knowledge.md during implementation; only the variable store is consumed by the digest.
- The editor repo ships reference mappings with distinct provenance. Its extractor emits `variables_tasks.json` and `variables_reputation.json` from the game's dialogue bundle. The former has 1131 `TASK.*` entries, matching the save's `TASK.*` variable count exactly; the latter has 21 reputation axes with their store variable names and initial values. `skill_key_map.json` was committed separately, and the scraper scripts do not document its derivation. It contains an `abilities` array of 4 and a `skills` array of 24, flattened for lookup, plus unrelated enum sections. Two hazards these mappings carry: the task DB's `description` strings are designer AUTHORING NOTES lifted from the dialogue database, not player-facing journal text (655 of 1131 are empty, and several state intended outcomes of in-progress tasks), so they are spoiler-unsafe and are never used for display (6.2). The reputation extractor output carried the same authoring-note class in `description`; the vendored copy strips those unconsumed fields. The digest's reputation keys are NOT the store variable names, so an explicit mapping is required (6.3). Only `variables_reputation.json` and `skill_key_map.json` are vendored: the task DB became unconsumed with the humanized-title decision and stays in the cloned repo as reference, and the `auto.daychange_*` initial values (from the repo's `variables_auto.json`) are pinned as literals in 6.3 rather than vendoring a third DB.

## 5. Component: dump tool (Go)

### 5.1 Contract

```
dedump <path-to-save.ntwtf.zip> [-o out.json] [-variables-only]
```

- Input: a COPY of a save zip (the tool never sees the original; glue guarantees this). The tool reads the zip directly via archive/zip; it does not extract to disk. Before `zip.OpenReader`, one bounded `ReaderAt` preflight validates the end-of-central-directory and, when present, ZIP64 locator and record without materializing archive members. The archive has inclusive ceilings of 1,073,741,824 total bytes, 65,536 central-directory records, and 67,108,864 central-directory bytes. The preflight rejects a declared directory range outside the archive, any count or size above those ceilings, and any archive whose actual byte length exceeds its ceiling. It then scans exactly the declared central-directory byte range with fixed-size header storage and at most one entry's bounded variable fields in memory, verifies every directory-header length against the remaining range, counts actual records and cumulative name bytes, and requires the scan to end exactly at both the declared record count and directory end. UTF-8 and backslash name checks occur in this scan and are repeated defensively while building `source.entries`. These checks precede `archive/zip` because a check of `Reader.File` after open is too late: the standard library has already allocated one file record and its name, extra data, and comment for every directory entry. The constants leave substantial headroom over known saves while bounding ignored-member amplification independently of required-member sizes. The zip must contain exactly one member matching each of `*.1st.ntwtf.json`, `*.2nd.ntwtf.json`, `*.ntwtf.lua`, and `*.states.lua`; a missing or duplicate match is exit 1 (a renamed member must not yield a silently empty output section). Matching is
an ordinal, case-sensitive suffix comparison against the complete `archive/zip.File.Name`. Forward-slash path components do not prevent a nested member from matching. Root-only matching is rejected for compatibility with archives that place the same logical save members beneath forward-slash directories. Every central-directory match has a logical prefix equal to its complete validated name with the known suffix removed. After all four unique matches are identified and before any is opened, the four prefixes must be ordinally identical and the shared prefix's final forward-slash component must be nonempty. This preserves a shared nested prefix while rejecting suffix-only names and hybrid archives assembled from unrelated logical saves. The prefix is not compared with `source.zipName`, because pipeline work copies may be renamed. Every central-directory member name must pass `utf8.ValidString` before retention, backslash validation, or suffix matching. This rejects invalid name bytes that `encoding/json` would otherwise replace with U+FFFD and prevents distinct names from collapsing in `source.entries`; `File.NonUTF8` alone is not a rejection predicate because it can also describe a valid UTF-8 name whose ZIP flag is absent. A name containing a backslash is rejected before matching, independent of the process `GODEBUG` setting. The cumulative UTF-8 byte length of all retained member names has an inclusive ceiling of 16,777,216 bytes, charged during the preflight and again in archive order before a name is appended to `source.entries`; the next byte over fails before `zip.OpenReader` allocation or output. Each matched required member has an inclusive uncompressed-size ceiling of 268,435,456 bytes (256 MiB). A larger declared `UncompressedSize64` fails before content is opened. The streamed read is independently limited to the ceiling plus one byte and fails if that extra byte is observed, so a false smaller declaration cannot bypass the bound. A member exactly at the ceiling is permitted. The ceiling applies separately to each required member; ignored and unmatched members are not opened under this rule. `*.FOW.json` is ignored (current adapter scope).
- Argument parsing accepts the grammar as written, including flags after the positional path (the implementation parses argv itself or configures its parser accordingly; a parser that stops at the first positional silently ignores the flags).
- Output is serialized completely in memory only after every required member has parsed successfully. Without `-o`, the tool writes one JSON document to stdout, verifies that the write accepted every byte and returned no error, and exits 0 only after that successful write. A failed stdout write exits 1. Bytes already accepted by a stream cannot be withdrawn, so a transport failure may leave an unusable prefix at the receiver. Every stdout consumer discards all received bytes unless it observes EOF, process exit 0, exactly one complete JSON document, and successful output-shape validation.
- With `-o`, stdout remains empty and the destination must be absent. The tool creates a unique new sibling temporary file named `<destination>.<32-lowercase-hex>.tmp` with no-overwrite creation in the destination directory, writes every serialized byte, flushes it, closes it, and atomically moves it into the absent destination without overwrite. The move either publishes the complete document or leaves the destination absent. A destination that already exists is an error and remains byte-for-byte unchanged. Any create, write, flush, close, or replacement failure exits 1 and removes the temporary file on a best-effort basis. Before replacement, failure leaves an absent destination absent and an existing destination unchanged. Exit 0 guarantees that the destination contains exactly the complete serialized document. Ordinary process failures attempt to remove the exact sibling temp. Hard-kill recovery is guaranteed only when the pipeline supplied a direct-child destination in the companion `.tmp\` root whose name begins lowercase `work-`; that sibling remains inside the work-file reaper's authority. An arbitrary ad-hoc `-o` destination may retain its sibling temp after a hard kill and the caller or operator owns its removal. Scanning arbitrary destination directories is an explicit anti-goal because it would expand the companion's deletion authority beyond its own roots.
- `-variables-only` omits `tlv.allTables` from the output (see below). The pipeline uses this flag; the default (complete) form exists to honor the "complete parsed state" contract for ad-hoc inspection.

Output shape (illustrative values):

```jsonc
{
  "source": {
    "zipName": "...",
    "byteLength": 1048576,
    "contentSha256": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef",
    "entries": ["..."]
  },
  "header": { /* .1st.ntwtf.json verbatim */ },
  "world":  { /* .2nd.ntwtf.json verbatim */ },
  "states": {
    "areaStates": { "SOME_AREA": { "LocationState": 1 }, "OTHER": { "emptied": true } },
    "shownOrbs":  { "SOME ORB": { "OrbSeen": 1 } }
  },
  "tlv": {
    "variables": { /* the dialogue variable store, name -> value */ },
    "allTables": [ /* all 5 top-level tables in file order; omitted with -variables-only */ ]
  }
}
```

The output schema is normative despite the illustrative values. The root contains exactly object `source`, object `header`, object `world`, object `states`, and object `tlv`. `source` contains exactly string `zipName`, positive Int64 `byteLength`, lowercase 64-hex string `contentSha256`, and array-of-string `entries`. `zipName` is the positional input path exactly as supplied, without normalization. `byteLength` and `contentSha256` are computed by the dump tool from the exact archive path it opens for parsing, independently of core and mapper arguments. `entries` contains every central-directory member name, including ignored and unmatched members, in archive order; duplicate names remain separate entries. `header` and `world` are the validated UTF-8 JSON object values from their required members. The complete member byte sequence must pass `utf8.Valid` before JSON validation. Each standalone member must have container depth at most 63 under the shared counting rule and is rejected before retention when it reaches 64: embedding beneath the dump root adds one container level, so 63 produces a depth-64 dump while 64 would produce a document every required consumer rejects. Before retention, a streaming JSON scan also compares every decoded property name ordinally within its containing object and rejects exact or escape-equivalent duplicates at any depth; case-distinct names remain legal. This is the same syntax-level uniqueness rule as `Read-CompanionJson`, so a successful dump cannot emit bytes its first required consumer rejects before mapper semantics begin. They are embedded as `json.RawMessage`, preserving property order and numeric lexemes without semantically remarshalizing the value. Parent `json.Marshal` compacts insignificant whitespace and applies its default HTML escaping to literal `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028, and U+2029; byte-for-byte whitespace and escape-spelling preservation is not promised. Retaining property order and numeric lexemes keeps opaque source evidence available for format diagnostics without exposing it as a mapped gameplay contract.

`states` contains exactly object `areaStates` and object `shownOrbs`. Each child maps an ordinally unique record name to an object whose ordinally unique fields contain only Boolean or signed Int64 values under the grammar below. `tlv` contains exactly object `variables` plus, without `-variables-only`, array `allTables`; with that flag, `allTables` is absent. `allTables` contains the five parsed top-level values in file order, each a table object. General Lua table property names use the type-preserving projection in 5.2. After the exactly-one probe identifies the variable store, `variables` reprojects that table as its semantic name-to-value map: its array count must be zero and every hash key must be a string, so its property names are the exact decoded strings without the general `s:` prefix. TLV values are JSON null, Boolean, string, finite number, or object; Lua tables always use an object projection, never a JSON array. A stdout or file consumer validates this exact field set, flag-dependent presence, types, counts, and applicable depth limit before accepting the dump.

The aggregate materialization budgets in 5.2 also cover `states.lua`: every state field value charges one decoded value; every record and every field charges one projected object entry; and every record name and field name charges its UTF-8 byte length against the retained-text budget. The parser reserves a complete logical line's record, field, value, and text charges before allocating its inner field map or inserting anything into either namespace. A charge that would exceed a remaining inclusive budget is an ordinary parse error before allocation. Sharing these budgets with TLV bounds total dump materialization rather than merely one input member's projection.

The variable store is identified by a closed two-part content signature: the top-level table contains key `character.books_read_counter` with an integer value (a stable mechanical variable), exactly 1,131 total keys whose names begin ordinally with literal `TASK.`, and Boolean values for every one of those keys. Those independent discriminators match both known saves and the vendored task database. A later game version that changes the task namespace requires an explicit dump and adapter version update; accepting a mismatched count would reduce identity back to the unsafe one-key probe. A content signature is used instead of a fixed table position so the tool survives table reordering across game versions. EXACTLY ONE table must satisfy the complete signature; zero or multiple matches exit 1 (fail closed; first-match-wins on a multi-match could silently publish the wrong store in a future game version, mirroring the exactly-one rule the zip members already follow). Publishing the store under the named key `tlv.variables` (rather than an index into a table array) removes any ambiguity about positions when `-variables-only` omits the array.

`states.lua` must be valid UTF-8 and is parsed line-wise. Logical lines split on LF, with at most one terminal CR removed from each resulting line; the final line may omit LF. A line is blank when its remaining Unicode scalar values are all whitespace, and blank lines are skipped. Every non-blank line must match this grammar exactly, with no unshown whitespace:

```text
record      = namespace "[\"" record-name "\"]={" field *("," field) "};"
namespace   = "AreaState" / "ShownOrbs"
field       = field-name "=" value
field-name  = ASCII identifier matching [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*
value       = "true" / "false" / integer
integer     = [ "+" / "-" ] 1*DIGIT
```

`record-name` is one or more Unicode scalar values other than U+0000 through U+001F, U+007F through U+009F, `"`, or `\`; no Lua escape syntax is recognized. Booleans are lowercase and case-sensitive. Integers are base-10 signed Int64 values, including both endpoints; a leading sign and leading zeroes are accepted. State integers are value-bearing rather than lexeme-bearing: the dump emits normalized Int64 JSON numbers, so unambiguous base-10 sign and zero padding remain accepted for compatibility while whitespace and broader Lua expressions remain outside the closed grammar. Values outside the Int64 range fail. Record names and field names use ordinal, case-sensitive equality. A record name may occur once in `AreaState` and once in `ShownOrbs`, because those are separate output namespaces, but a duplicate within one namespace fails. A field name may occur only once within a record. Empty records, empty names, malformed fields, extra whitespace, unknown value spellings, duplicate records, and duplicate fields all fail before output.

Failure selection is deterministic after whole-input UTF-8 validation. The parser uses this complete ordered grammar-to-reason mapping for each nonblank logical line:

1. Failure of the namespace, literal brackets and quotes, record-name grammar, braces, or terminal semicolon is `invalid-record`.
2. After a valid record envelope, an empty field body, a leading, trailing, or doubled comma, or any field token without exactly one equals sign and a legal field name is `invalid-field`. An extra equals sign is therefore `invalid-field`.
3. After valid field syntax, a value token that is neither a lowercase Boolean nor the integer grammar is `invalid-value`; an integer-grammar token outside Int64 is `integer-out-of-range`.
4. After syntax and value validation, a repeated field name is `duplicate-field`.
5. After the complete record validates, a repeated record name in the same namespace is `duplicate-record`.
6. After syntax, range, and uniqueness validation, failure to reserve the complete logical line against any remaining decoded-value, projected-entry, or retained-text budget is `materialization-budget-exceeded`. This check occurs before allocation or mutation and is last in precedence because the line must otherwise be valid and unique before its aggregate charge is meaningful.

Fields are visited left to right, so the first failing field decides the line. For example, `AreaState["A"]={x=1,x=oops};` reports `invalid-value`, not `duplicate-field`. An empty record reports `invalid-field`.

Every `states.lua` parse failure is rendered exactly as `dedump: states.lua: line <positive-decimal-line-number>: <reason-token>`, followed by one newline. The reason token is exactly `invalid-utf8`, `invalid-record`, `invalid-field`, `invalid-value`, `integer-out-of-range`, `duplicate-record`, or `duplicate-field`, or `materialization-budget-exceeded`. For invalid UTF-8, the line number identifies the line containing the first invalid byte. No record name, field name, value, complete source line, or other member text may appear in the diagnostic.

`states.lua` remains a required, strict, dump-only surface. The dump tool is the save-format boundary, so complete parsing prevents corruption or game-format drift from silently discarding evidence needed for ad hoc inspection, research backfill, and later mappings. The state records are not copied wholesale into the digest because their internal identifiers have no current game-neutral consumer, have not been established as spoiler-safe, and would turn low-level state churn into ordinary `extraChanges`. An individual field may cross this boundary only after it gains an explicit consumer, established spoiler-safe meaning, stable source semantics, a deliberate diff-noise policy, and corresponding versioned schema, mapping, and fixture updates. Until then, parse failure rejects the dump while successfully parsed values remain under `states` only.

### 5.2 Parser adaptation (complete deviation list)

The vendored reference parser (`lua_parser.go`) is adapted with exactly these deviations; everything else, including naming and type tags, is kept verbatim:

1. **Array-part header fix.** The reference treats the first int32 of a table header as padding. In reality the header is `T <int32 arrayCount> <arrayCount values> <int32 hashCount> <hashCount key-value pairs>` (a Lua table's array part then hash part). The reference only ever met hash-only tables (arrayCount 0), so the bug was invisible; on a real save it aborts at the first array-part table with 5.3 MB unparsed and, because its ReadAll converts that abort into success once any data was read, returns a plausible-looking result with the trailing table(s) silently dropped. In both known saves the variable store happens to parse BEFORE the abort point (it is table 4 of 5; the first array-part table is in the conversations region), but nothing guarantees that ordering across game versions, and a silently truncated parse is unacceptable under this tool's contract regardless of which table is lost. Array elements and hash keys use one canonical type-preserving property-name encoding in the emitted JSON object. A decoded string key is `s:` plus its exact UTF-8 string, a Boolean key is `b:0` or `b:1`, and a numeric key is `n:` plus exactly 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits of its canonical binary64 bits. Synthesized Lua 1-based array indices use the same numeric-key rule. Numeric key encoding retains the raw binary64 value instead of first applying the value-side Int64 conversion, rejects NaN and both infinities, normalizes both signed zeroes to positive-zero bits, then formats `math.Float64bits` as fixed-width `%016x`. Every signed Int32 array index is exactly representable as binary64. The permitted hash-key domain is string, finite binary64, or Boolean. A table-valued key is rejected at its key type tag before recursive decoding or allocation; null and unknown key tags are also rejected. The array and hash parts are storage partitions of one logical Lua keyspace, so one JSON object preserves that lookup surface. A JSON array cannot carry mixed hash keys, while separate `arrayPart` and `hashPart` wrappers would expose storage layout and create a second table shape. Duplicate detection occurs after canonical key encoding: an array index and an equal numeric hash key collide, while numeric 1, string `"1"`, and Boolean true remain distinct. A projected collision fails closed instead of discarding either value. The semantic `tlv.variables` projection is the sole untagged exception and first requires array count zero plus string-only hash keys. Validated 2026-08-07 against the user's real save: full parse, 0 bytes remaining, variable store found with 12153 entries and correct spot-check values; same result on the repo's example save.
2. **Top-level values are kept separate.** The reference `ReadAll` merges all top-level tables into one flat map, which destroys the per-table identity that `tlv.allTables` and the variable-store probe need. The adapted reader returns the ordered list of top-level values instead. After parsing reaches clean EOF and before the variable probe or flag-dependent projection, both output modes require exactly five top-level table values. Any other count is an ordinary parse error, including a clean EOF after four complete tables. The count is an outer-format drift sentinel: the content signature tolerates reordering within the known layout, but does not make added or removed top-level tables compatible. Accepting an extra fully parsed table would silently accept an unreviewed format expansion, so changing the count requires an explicit dump and adapter version update.
3. **Strict errors.** The reference `ReadAll` skips stray null bytes and silently stops on a mid-file parse error once some data was read, returning success. The adapted reader propagates every `ReadValue` error and treats leftover bytes after the final top-level value as an error; either causes exit 1. The same strictness covers key collisions within one table: a hash key equal after canonical type-preserving encoding to an already-emitted array-part key, or a repeated encoded hash key, is an error rather than a silent overwrite (the reference lineage silently drops one value). The validated array-part fix parses both known saves to exactly 0 remaining bytes with no null-skipping, so the tolerance is dead weight that would mask real corruption (violating this tool's no-partial-output contract).
4. **Bounded 7-bit string lengths.** Decode into a fixed-width unsigned accumulator. Accept at most five bytes; on the fifth, reject a continuation bit or payload above `0x0f` before shifting. Convert to an allocation index only after the decoded value is no greater than the bytes remaining. Terminated noncanonical encodings within those bounds remain accepted to preserve the reference format's permissive spelling; EOF, excess width, overflow payload, and an out-of-bounds decoded length are ordinary parse errors.
5. **Bounded table recursion.** `ReadValue` carries a table-nesting counter and rejects before descending beyond 61 table objects. The outer table counts as one. This limit keeps the default complete output within the shared JSON depth of 64: root, `tlv`, and `allTables` consume three surrounding container levels. It also prevents crafted inputs below the member-size ceiling from terminating the Go process through a fatal stack overflow.
6. **Exact integral-number boundary.** A finite binary64 value with no fractional part becomes Int64 only when it is at least `-2^63` and strictly less than `2^63`. `-2^63` is representable and converts; `2^63` remains binary64. The strict upper bound avoids the rounded `math.MaxInt64` comparison accepting `2^63` and converting it to `Int64.MinValue` on the target runtime.
7. **Strict string encoding.** Every TLV `TypeString` payload must pass `utf8.Valid` before conversion to a Go string, whether it becomes a value or a projected object key. Invalid bytes are an ordinary parse error; they are never allowed to become replacement characters during JSON serialization. A literal, valid U+FFFD remains valid data.
8. **Aggregate materialization budgets.** One parser invocation shares inclusive limits across all five top-level tables: at most 2,000,000 decoded values, 1,000,000 final projected object entries, and 67,108,864 retained UTF-8 bytes across decoded string bodies plus generated and decoded projected keys, including every two-byte type prefix and every 16-byte numeric payload. Decrement the value budget at the entry to every `ReadValue`. Immediately after decoding each signed Int32 array or hash count, reject a negative value before budget arithmetic, allocation, or use as a loop bound. Reserve nonnegative declared array and hash entries before allocating or growing a table; and charge string or generated-key bytes before allocation or insertion. A declaration or value that would exceed a remaining budget fails before allocation as an ordinary parse error. These limits bound decoded amplification that the compressed member ceiling, string-length checks, and nesting depth do not; the repository fixture uses at most 667,568 decoded values, 275,461 projected entries, and 6,082,480 retained string and key bytes.

A comment at the top of the vendored file records origin (repo URL, commit) and this deviation list. The pinned upstream source is MIT-licensed. The companion root's `THIRD_PARTY-NOTICES.md` records the exact source revision and preserves the upstream copyright and permission notice.

### 5.3 Build

`dump-tool\` ships the Go sources plus a `build.ps1` that runs `go build` (Go 1.26 toolchain already installed) producing `dump-tool\dedump.exe`. Building is a setup action, never a pipeline action: core executes adapter commands exactly as configured (section 8 seam contract) and never builds. `build.ps1` produces an authoring-tree binary for package preparation; it does not repair an installed immutable release. When the packaged `dedump.exe` is missing, section 8's game-neutral command preparation classifies the absent artifact-root member directly as `adapter-release-corrupt` before spawn. The cycle carries that typed reason unchanged. The companion host's fixed remediation mapping in section 10 directs the user to repair or reinstall the active adapter package without carrying command paths through the diagnostic protocol.

## 6. Component: digest mapper (PowerShell)

`Build-Digest.ps1 -DumpPath <dump.json> -SourceDomainId <lowercase SHA-256 hex> -SourceName <original save file name> -SavedAt <original save LastWriteTimeUtc, ISO 8601 'o' format> -SourceLength <bytes> -SourceSha256 <lowercase hex> -AdapterVersion <int> -OutPath <digest.json>`

Reads the dump JSON plus the vendored reference DBs and writes the digest. Game-neutral schema; every Disco Elysium specific goes under `extra`. The core-owned acquisition domain and original save's verified receipt cross the seam as explicit parameters (`-SourceDomainId`, `-SourceName`, `-SavedAt`, `-SourceLength`, `-SourceSha256`) because the dump is made from a work-area copy whose name and timestamps say nothing about the configured source or original. The mapper stamps `schemaVersion` (the core schema version it targets, a mapper constant verified against the running core at commit, section 8), `adapterVersion` (NOT a mapper constant: the value arrives via the `-AdapterVersion` parameter, which core substitutes from adapter.json's declared value, making adapter.json the single source of truth; this is what lets a version bump heal via one reprocessing run), and `game` (a mapper constant; core verifies it equals adapter.json's `game` at commit, catching a digest command wired to the wrong adapter). Core still validates the mapper's emitted `adapterVersion` before commit: argument substitution does not prove that an external or miswired mapper honored the argument.

Core likewise validates the emitted `source.domainId` against the held configuration's core-derived acquisition domain. Against `Invoke-SaveDigest`'s verified source receipt, `source.saveFile`, canonical `source.savedAt`, and `source.contentSha256` compare ordinally, while `source.byteLength` compares numerically. Supplying these arguments is not proof that an external mapper stamped them faithfully. A mismatch is `[digest]` before commit or historical comparison, never an unverified freshness input.

All eight parameters are required; the mapper exits 1 with a stderr message when any is absent (declared Mandatory, so a scripted invocation fails instead of prompting). `SourceDomainId`, `SourceLength`, and `AdapterVersion` bind as `[string]`, not PowerShell numeric parameter types. `SourceDomainId` must be lowercase 64-hex. Before conversion, `SourceLength` must match canonical positive decimal `[1-9][0-9]*` and fit Int64; `AdapterVersion` must match canonical signed decimal `0|-?[1-9][0-9]*` and fit the same Int64 domain as adapter.json. Exponents, fractions, leading plus, leading zeroes, negative zero, and overflow fail before conversion. The parsed integers, not the original strings, are stamped into the digest.

Before materializing any dump or vendored-DB value into PowerShell, the mapper validates the value through its `RootElement`. Every consumed leaf that maps to fixed schema reuses the target field's 6.1 JSON kind and numeric range. Every adapter-owned leaf reuses the normative 6.3 kind and range below. Cross-kind coercion is forbidden: strings do not become numbers or Booleans, numbers do not become strings, and an unexpected null fails. The mapper therefore cannot rely on PowerShell casts such as `[bool]'false'` or `[long]'100'`. Container presence checks and output-schema validation do not replace this source-kind validation. `Build-Digest.ps1` owns an adapter-side `Read-DiscoDumpDocument` validator that applies `Read-CompanionJson` budget `dump` and the complete 5.1 dump schema before any mapping. The pipeline invocation requires the `-variables-only` shape, including absent `tlv.allTables`; dump-tool verification applies the complementary complete-form shape. Both forms require `tlv.variables` to satisfy the same integer-valued `character.books_read_counter` plus exactly 1,131 total `TASK.*` keys, all Boolean, as the signature that the dump tool uses, so an external or regressed dumper cannot select a probe-only decoy table. The validator returns the same validated `CompanionJsonValue` or throws `CompanionDocumentValidationException`, never a partial value. Before mapping any dump value, the mapper requires ordinal equality between `dump.source.contentSha256` and `SourceSha256` plus numeric equality between `dump.source.byteLength` and parsed `SourceLength`. This independently binds the state being mapped to the archive bytes the dump tool actually opened; a command line that mentions the promised `{save}` placeholder but parses another archive cannot stamp the promised receipt onto that archive's state.

Failure contract: exit 0 on success; exit 1 with a stderr message when:

- `SavedAt` is not canonical UTC `o`, `SourceLength` or `AdapterVersion` violates its canonical Int64 grammar, or `SourceDomainId` or `SourceSha256` is not lowercase 64-hex;
- dump-owned byte length or content hash differs from the supplied source receipt;
- the dump JSON is missing or unparseable;
- a vendored DB file is missing or unparseable (the DBs ship with the adapter; absence means a broken install, fail closed);
- a digest-required source container is absent: `world.sunshineClockTimeHolder.time`, `world.playerCharacter`, `world.characterSheet`, `world.aquiredJournalTasks`, `world.inventoryState.itemListState`, `header.areaId`, `header.partyState`, `world.failedWhiteChecksHolder` (including its `WhiteCheckCache` and `SeenWhiteCheckCache` maps), and `tlv.variables` (revalidated mapper-side even though the dump tool's 5.1 probe fails closed: the mapper reads an already-written dump file and must not trust producer guarantees). This list is the complete set of required containers. Within them, a sub-field that 6.2/6.3 maps and that is absent is ALSO a fail-closed error, except where the mapping row states an explicit absent-value rule. The complete exception list: sparse TLV variables (initial-value defaults), a PRESENT resolution record without `dayCounter` (unresolved; the resolution key itself remains mandatory), the `isFresh`-based highlights predicate (absent entry or field = not fresh), an absent `SubtaskAquisitions` entry (the objective simply has no subobjectives; that container is sparse in every real save), and an absent `isOnlySeen` field (counts false, 6.3);
- the flattened `skills` array of vendored `skill_key_map.json` is not exactly 24 ordinally unique `skill_type` entries, or the DIRECT CHILDREN of `world.characterSheet` do not contain exactly one source object for every vendored `skill_type`. The mapper compares the ordinal sets and multiplicities, reporting missing, duplicate, and extra source types before digest construction. Each matched skill object's `abilityType` must also have one matching entry in the map's ordinally unique 4-entry `abilities` array. Both hops of the join therefore fail closed for missing source data and vendor drift, rather than merely validating the subset that happened to appear. The direct-children scope matters: `skillType` also appears on dozens of nested records inside modifier maps, including sentinel values with no map entry, and an any-depth scan would exit 1 on every real save. The `skillType` field is the discriminator: characterSheet also holds 4 ability objects without `skillType`, modifier maps, ownership arrays, and UI fields, all outside this check;
- `variables_reputation.json` does not contain exactly 21 rows whose non-empty `name` values are ordinally unique and whose `initial_value` values are integral numbers in the Int64 range, or a reputation mapping entry (6.3) does not resolve to exactly one row by ordinal name (catches mapping typos, duplicate vendor rows, and case-colliding vendor drift before fallback values are selected);

Fields that are legitimately absent early in a playthrough map to their documented defaults: individual `reputation.*` variables absent from the SAVE's store take the vendored `variables_reputation.json` initial value, and `auto.daychange_*` variables take the literal false pinned in 6.3 (that DB is not vendored). No partial digest is ever written: `OutPath` must be absent. The mapper creates a unique sibling `<OutPath>.<32-lowercase-hex>.tmp` with no-overwrite semantics, writes, flushes, and closes the complete digest, then atomically moves it to `OutPath` without overwrite. An existing temp or destination fails closed and remains unchanged. Since `OutPath` is the pipeline run's `work-*-digest.json` path, the sibling temp matches the `work-*` family, so the mapper's own best-effort finally cleanup covers ordinary failure after it acquires the sibling temp. `Invoke-SaveDigest` knows only its allocated outer paths and cannot name the mapper's private nonce after a hard kill, so startup reaping covers a pipeline mapper killed mid-write or mapper-local cleanup that failed. The CLI also accepts an arbitrary absent `OutPath` for ad hoc use. Ordinary failure still attempts exact sibling cleanup, but a hard kill may leave that private sibling outside companion reaper authority; cleanup is then caller or operator owned. Scanning arbitrary output directories is an explicit anti-goal, matching dedump's `-o` boundary.

### 6.1 Digest schema (documented in core/digest-schema.md, versioned)

```jsonc
{
  "schemaVersion": 2,                            // core schema version
  "adapterVersion": 2,                            // adapter-owned; bumped when adapter semantics require existing digests to be rebuilt
  "game": "disco_elysium",
  "generatedAt": "2026-08-07T10:00:00.0000000Z", // digest generation time, UTC 'o' format like every timestamp in this schema
  "source": {
    "domainId": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef",
    "saveFile": "AutoSave, ... .ntwtf.zip",     // original file name only, no path
    "savedAt": "2025-08-06T08:28:12.1234567Z",  // original save LastWriteTimeUtc, 'o' format
    "byteLength": 1048576,                       // verified source and work-copy byte count
    "contentSha256": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"
  },
  "session": {
    "day": 4,                                    // in-game day (int)
    "time": "08:09",                             // in-game clock HH:mm
    "location": "Martinaise-ext",               // engine area/scene id
    "playtimeNote": null                         // string or null; DE saves carry no playtime
  },
  "character": {
    "level": 25,
    "xp": 53,                                    // progress within the current level (DE: resets on level-up)
    "unspentPoints": 2,
    "money": { "amount": 49.53, "minorUnits": 4953, "minorUnitsPerUnit": 100,
                "currency": "real" },              // minorUnits is the arithmetic source; amount is display
    "vitals": [ { "name": "health", "value": 4 }, { "name": "morale", "value": 5 } ],
    "skills": [ { "name": "Logic", "value": 8, "group": "Intellect" } /* all 24 */ ]
  },
  "objectives": [
    {
      "id": "TASK.example_task",                 // engine id; cited only when precision demands it
      "title": "example task",                   // humanized id; see 6.2 for why not journal text
      "status": "active",                        // active | done | failed | locked
      "acquired": { "day": 1, "time": "08:23" },
      "resolved": null,                          // same shape when resolved
      "subobjectives": [ /* same fields; nested subobjectives is null; see 6.2 */ ]
    }
  ],
  "inventory": {
    "owned": [ "item_id" ],                      // all owned item ids
    "equipped": [ "item_id" ],                   // subset of owned
    "highlights": [ "item_id" ]                  // subset of owned; see 6.2
  },
  "extra": { /* game-specific, see 6.3 */ }
}
```

PowerShell 7.5 or later consumers parse digest and diff documents through `Read-CompanionJson`. Its `CompanionJsonValue.Materialized` view uses the PowerShell options in section 2, preserving case-distinct keys and exact timestamp strings, while its owned `RootElement` preserves exact JSON numbers and arbitrary subtrees for validation, comparison, and emission.

`Read-DigestDocument` owns the complete core structural validator and always materializes through `Read-CompanionJson` budget `digest`. Object-key matching is ordinal and case-sensitive. Objects outside `extra` have exactly the fields shown in the schema block: missing and unexpected fields both fail validation because an added field requires a schema-version decision. The required surface is:

| Path | Required type and invariants |
|---|---|
| Root envelope | Object containing integer `schemaVersion`, integer `adapterVersion`, non-empty string `game`, canonical UTC `o` string `generatedAt`, and object/array children `source`, `session`, `character`, `objectives`, `inventory`, and `extra`. |
| `source` | Object containing lowercase 64-hex string `domainId`, non-empty file-name-only string `saveFile`, canonical UTC `o` string `savedAt`, positive integer `byteLength`, and lowercase 64-hex string `contentSha256`. The name contains no directory separator. `domainId` binds the digest to its configured adapter and save selector. The latter two fields are the verified byte receipt and do not participate in save ordering. |
| `session` | Object containing integer `day`, valid zero-padded `HH:mm` string `time`, non-empty string `location`, and string-or-null `playtimeNote`. |
| `character` | Object containing nonnegative integer `level`, `xp`, and `unspentPoints`; `money`; array `vitals`; and array `skills`. |
| `character.money` | Object containing finite decimal `amount`, signed Int64 integer `minorUnits`, positive Int64 integer `minorUnitsPerUnit`, and non-empty string `currency`. `amount` must equal checked Decimal conversion of `minorUnits` divided once by checked Decimal conversion of `minorUnitsPerUnit`; overflow, a non-decimal JSON number, or inequality fails validation. |
| `character.vitals[]` | Objects containing non-empty string `name` and integer `value`; names are unique ordinally. |
| `character.skills[]` | Objects containing non-empty string `name`, integer `value`, and non-empty string `group`; names are unique ordinally. |
| `objectives[]` | Objects containing non-empty string `id`, string `title`, status in `active`, `done`, `failed`, or `locked`, acquired day/time object, resolved null or day/time object, and array `subobjectives`; objective ids are unique ordinally. |
| `objectives[].subobjectives[]` | Objects with the same scalar and timestamp fields as an objective plus `subobjectives: null`; ids are unique within their parent and no deeper nesting is legal. |
| `inventory` | Object containing non-empty-string arrays `owned`, `equipped`, and `highlights`; each array is ordinally unique, and equipped/highlights are subsets of owned. |
| `extra` | Object. Every JSON-compatible value, including null within the digest's maximum depth of 62, is legal beneath it; its game-specific shape is adapter-owned and is not a structural requirement of the game-neutral core validator. |

Every day/time object contains exactly integer `day` plus valid zero-padded `HH:mm` string `time`. Canonical UTC `o` means RoundtripKind parsing succeeds, the result is UTC, and formatting it back with `o` reproduces the input ordinally. Outside `extra`, JSON null is legal only where the table names it. Numeric fields reject strings that PowerShell could coerce. Every structural type and numeric check uses `RootElement`, never the PowerShell-materialized value. In this schema, `integer` means a JSON number whose raw token has integer grammar and for which `JsonElement.TryGetInt64` succeeds; `decimal` means a JSON number for which `JsonElement.TryGetDecimal` succeeds. A value accepted only through Double conversion is invalid. The validator returns the same validated `CompanionJsonValue` on success and throws `CompanionDocumentValidationException` on failure; it never calls `exit` and never returns a partial document.

Core structural validity is necessary but not sufficient for an authoritative state. Every adapter release exports `Assert-AdapterDigestSemantics` from the fixed package path `{artifactRoot}\adapters\<adapter>\Validate-Digest.psm1`. The function accepts one already core-valid `CompanionJsonValue`, writes no PowerShell stream, returns no object, and either returns normally or throws typed `AdapterDigestValidationException` for a semantic rejection. Import failure, a missing or extra export, stream output, an unexpected return object, or any other exception is validator infrastructure failure, never evidence that the digest is merely invalid. The core module owns validator loading through the process-local lease mechanism in section 8. A completed compatibility configuration owns one lease and its callable for the snapshot; provisional resolution owns the lease until it transfers to a completed snapshot or releases on failure. No loaded module instance is left unowned or reused after failed admission.

`Assert-AdapterDigestDocument -Document <CompanionJsonValue> -AdapterConfig <validated compatibility object>` is the game-neutral core wrapper. It first requires the running core version, adapter version, game, and source domain to equal the held configuration, then invokes that configuration's adapter callable and returns the same document only on success. `Read-AdapterDigestDocument` composes `Read-DigestDocument` with that assertion for path or JSON input. Only an adapter-admitted digest may become authoritative current or baseline state, enter a diff or walk comparison, suppress a watcher cycle, authorize a commit-guard discard, or appear as turn-context state. Keeping this second validation at the core trust boundary was chosen over trusting mapper self-validation because a miswired or regressed mapper can still emit a core-valid, correctly stamped document with the wrong adapter semantics.

Complete adapter-admission callers are `Invoke-SaveDigest` for pipeline and walk output; the shared baseline reader; the shared consuming operation's post-baseline fresh cache-target read; `Get-Diff.ps1` target reads; every initial, preflight, and locked-final `Get-TurnContext.ps1` target read; the watcher quiescence decision; the cycle commit-guard discard decision; historical walk comparisons; producer-mode nested state validation in `Read-TurnContextEnvelope`; and section 10's host-side nested-state gate. A narrow identity prefilter is not another admission caller. No other path may use a digest as adapter-valid.

Complete consumer list for the schema: `DigestDiff.psm1`'s differ and shared consuming operation; `Invoke-SaveDigest` through post-digest admission for both pipeline cycles and walk candidates; `Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1` through comparisons of admitted historical digests; `Get-Diff.ps1` target validation; `Get-TurnContext.ps1` target validation, source-floor comparison, and envelope emission; `companion/AGENTS.md`-guided chat sessions through the turn-context envelope; the shared cycle commit guard used by watcher, one-shot, and turn-context refreshes; and the watcher's cycle trigger. The two pipeline readers may first inspect a narrow prefilter containing `source.domainId`, `source.savedAt`, `source.saveFile`, `schemaVersion`, `adapterVersion`, and `game`, but a prefilter pass gains no authority: the exact frozen bytes must pass complete core and adapter admission before they can suppress a cycle or discard a candidate. Adapter mappers are producers of this shape, not consumers. No other consumers.

The differ consumes: `schemaVersion`, `adapterVersion`, `game`, `generatedAt`, and all five `source` provenance fields (for the 7.1 invalidation check and the 7.2 envelope); `session.day`, `session.time`, `session.location`; `character.level`, `character.xp`, `character.money.minorUnits` (with `minorUnitsPerUnit` for delta emission and compatibility, plus `currency` for compatibility), `character.skills` (name and value per entry); `objectives[]` and their `subobjectives[]` (id and status, flattened per 7.2); `inventory.owned` and `inventory.equipped`; and every leaf under `extra` except subtrees excluded via adapter.json `diffExcludePaths` (7.2). `Get-Diff.ps1` additionally reads the envelope fields (`schemaVersion`, `adapterVersion`, `game`) directly for its own 7.1 invalidation and upgrade checks, not merely as a conduit, and the section 8 post-digest validator reads `schemaVersion`, `adapterVersion`, `game`, and all five `source` provenance fields on the fresh digest before commit; both are part of the closed consumer list above, not additions to it.

The differ deliberately omits value deltas for: `session.playtimeNote`, `character.unspentPoints`, `character.vitals`, `character.skills[].group`, `inventory.highlights`, objective titles and timestamps, and `source.*`. Rationale, since this core-level list is fixed schema semantics (unlike `extra`, whose noise tuning is adapter data): vitals and highlights churn with nearly every save (combat and check damage; inspection badges) and would bury real changes; unspentPoints follows level; group is a static label; titles and timestamps are immutable once an objective exists, so id and status carry all change signal; playtimeNote and `source.*` identify the save, not the game state. The source-identity consumers in the complete list above still read `source.*`; only game-state comparison ignores it. The diff copies the current digest's complete source provenance so a host can bind the diff to its target. `character.money.amount` is a validated display derivation of represented `minorUnits`, while `currency` and `minorUnitsPerUnit` are pairwise compatibility metadata; none of those three participates in the unrepresented-change projection. Omitted game-state fields still participate in 7.2's `hasUnrepresentedChanges` projection, so their transition is detected even though no value delta is retained. Source and generation metadata do not participate in that flag. Skill names present on only one side are emitted as additions or removals per 7.2 rather than ignored.

Schema version 2 adds the acquisition-domain identifier, verified byte receipt, the closed diff contract, unrepresented-change detection, and JSON Pointer paths in 7.2. Every `schemaVersion: 1` `current.json`, `baseline.json`, and derived diff is version-stale under 7.1: the newest save is reprocessed once, the baseline is reset rather than compared across schemas, and no adapter-version bump is required because the semantic change is owned by core. Installation supplies the same adapterVersion's new schema-qualified release before activating the new core schema; release identity therefore has a core schema dimension even when adapter semantics do not change.

### 6.2 Field mappings (Disco Elysium adapter)

| Digest field | Source |
|---|---|
| source.domainId | `-SourceDomainId`, stamped from the core-derived acquisition domain held for this invocation |
| source.saveFile / savedAt / byteLength / contentSha256 | `-SourceName` / `-SavedAt` / parsed `-SourceLength` / `-SourceSha256` parameters, stamped from core's verified source receipt (savedAt is UTC `o`; the hash is lowercase SHA-256 hex) |
| session.day / time | `world.sunshineClockTimeHolder.time`: `dayCounter` and `dayMinutes` -> HH:mm (`dayMinutes` div/mod 60, zero-padded) |
| session.location | `header.areaId` |
| character.level / xp / unspentPoints | `world.playerCharacter.Level` / `.XpAmount` / `.SkillPoints` (the container holds name-adjacent decoys; these three fields are the bindings) |
| character.money | `world.playerCharacter.Money` is in cents: `minorUnits` := Money, `minorUnitsPerUnit` := 100, `amount` := checked Decimal(Money) / Decimal(100), `currency` := "real" |
| character.vitals | `world.characterSheet.endurance.value` -> current health, `.volition.value` -> current morale; `maximumValue`, `damageValue`, and `world.playerCharacter.healingPools` are not emitted |
| character.skills | the 24 characterSheet objects carrying `skillType`. Per entry: `value` := the object's `value` field (the effective score the in-game sheet shows, including modifiers and damage; transient skill damage therefore legitimately surfaces in the diff), `name` := the matching `skills[]` entry's display name in the vendored `skill_key_map.json`, `group` := the display name of the parent ability, a two-hop join: the object's `abilityType` -> the map's `abilities[]` entry with that type -> its display name |
| objectives | `world.aquiredJournalTasks`: acquired = key present in `TaskAquisitions`; status done when `TaskResolutions[id]` carries a `dayCounter` value, else active (unresolved is null in real saves, `{}` per editor docs; both lack `dayCounter`); title := the humanized id (strip the `TASK.` prefix, underscores to spaces); `acquired`/`resolved` objects built from the record's `dayCounter` and `dayMinutes` exactly like session.day/time (`realDayCounter` and `seconds` ignored). Titles deliberately do NOT come from the vendored task DB: its `description` strings are designer authoring notes, not journal text, several state intended outcomes of in-progress tasks (spoiler leak), and most are empty (section 4). The real journal display text lives in the conversation layer, which is deliberately unconsumed (section 12), so the humanized id is the only spoiler-safe title source |
| objectives[].subobjectives | `SubtaskAquisitions[id]` when present (an absent entry means the objective has no subobjectives, the common case): one entry per subtask id; same id/title/status/acquired rules as objectives (humanized-id titles); status done when the parent resolved, else active, and `resolved` is always null (DE does not persist per-subtask resolution); no deeper nesting. Acquired children stay present so parent resolution does not appear as a removal. Leaving a child active after parent closure would falsely present actionable work, while an `unknown` state would expand core status without a source distinction, so parent `done` is the least misleading projection. It means only closed with the parent, not independently completed; no child outcome or completion time is recoverable. |
| inventory.owned | `world.characterSheet.gainedItems` |
| inventory.equipped | `world.characterSheet.equippedItems` |
| inventory.highlights | ids in `gainedItems` whose `world.inventoryState.itemListState` entry (joined ordinally on `itemName`) has `isFresh` true (owned and not yet inspected). Every catalog `itemName` is a non-empty string and is ordinally unique; a duplicate fails before digest construction rather than overwriting. An owned id with no ordinal catalog match, including a case-only mismatch, or an entry without an `isFresh` field, counts as not fresh (excluded). The intersection is essential: `itemListState` covers all 206 game items with `isFresh` defaulting true, so unfiltered it names ~133 items the player has never seen (spoiler violation). The set is often empty on a mature save; that is correct. |

Before objective construction, the mapper validates the three journal containers as objects and builds ordinal key sets. Every acquisition key, resolution key, `SubtaskAquisitions` parent key, and nested subobjective key must begin ordinally with literal `TASK.` and contain at least one character after the prefix. `TaskAquisitions` and `TaskResolutions` must have exactly equal keys, including case; every `SubtaskAquisitions` parent key must belong to that acquisition set. Each present resolution value is null or an object; arbitrary other JSON kinds fail. Present null and an object without `dayCounter`, including `{}`, remain the two unresolved shapes. Missing, extra, case-only, invalid-namespace, empty-suffix, or orphan keys fail before digest construction instead of collapsing into active or disappearing.

Statuses `failed` and `locked` are defined by the core schema but not emitted by the DE adapter at `adapterVersion: 2`: the save's journal model does not distinguish failed from done resolutions, and unacquired tasks are deliberately not surfaced (spoiler discipline). Recorded as a known limitation in knowledge.md; revisit only if a reliable failed marker is found in the `TASK.*` variable layer.

Spoiler rule for the mapper: it only ever emits ids and humanized titles of tasks the player has ALREADY acquired (they are in the in-game journal), item and thought ids the player possesses or has encountered (filtered through the `characterSheet` owned arrays), and mechanical counters. It never reads the catalog-shaped lists (`itemListState`, `thoughtListState`) except for the ownership-filtered highlights badge, and never emits unacquired task metadata, dialogue text, conversation titles, or `SeenWhiteCheckCache` precondition expressions.

### 6.3 `extra` content (DE `adapterVersion: 2`)

```jsonc
{
  "party": { "kimInParty": true, "cunoInParty": false },
  "reputation": { "communist": 17, "moralist": 0, "ultraliberal": 0, "nationalist": 0,
                   "kim": -4, "copStyle": { "sorry": 0, "superstar": 0, "apocalypse": 0,
                   "boring": 0, "art": 0 } },
  "thoughts": { "cooking": ["id"], "fixed": ["id"], "forgotten": ["id"], "known": ["id"] },
  "whiteChecks": { "retryable": 0, "seenOnly": 0 },                  // counts only
  "dayPhase": { "hasSlept": true, "canSleep": false }                // auto.daychange_*
}
```

This is the exact normative DE `extra` schema for adapter version 2. The root contains exactly `party`, `reputation`, `thoughts`, `whiteChecks`, and `dayPhase`; null is illegal throughout. `party` contains exactly Boolean `kimInParty` and `cunoInParty`. `reputation` contains exactly signed Int64 `communist`, `moralist`, `ultraliberal`, `nationalist`, and `kim`, plus object `copStyle`; `copStyle` contains exactly signed Int64 `sorry`, `superstar`, `apocalypse`, `boring`, and `art`. `thoughts` contains exactly arrays `cooking`, `fixed`, `forgotten`, and `known`; every entry is a non-empty string, each array is ordinally unique and sorted ordinally, and the four arrays are pairwise disjoint under ordinal comparison. `whiteChecks` contains exactly nonnegative Int64 `retryable` and `seenOnly`. `dayPhase` contains exactly Boolean `hasSlept` and `canSleep`. The mapper validates this complete object through `RootElement` before writing the digest, and the release's independent `Assert-AdapterDigestSemantics` repeats the same check whenever core admits that digest. The game-neutral 6.1 structural validator still treats `extra` as opaque adapter-owned JSON.

The DE adapter assertion also closes every single-digest invariant from 6.2 that the core schema intentionally leaves game-neutral. Its complete fixed-schema checks are: `session.playtimeNote` is null; money has `minorUnitsPerUnit: 100` and ordinal currency `real`; vitals are exactly `health` then `morale`; skills contain exactly the 24 ordinal names and matching groups in `skill_key_map.json`; every objective and subobjective id has a nonempty `TASK.` suffix and its title is the exact humanization of that id; only `active` and `done` occur; a top-level done objective has a non-null resolution while an active one has null; each subobjective has null resolution and the same status as its parent; and every `extra` invariant in the preceding paragraph holds. Inventory ownership cannot be re-derived from one digest, so its complete single-document invariants remain the core uniqueness and subset rules in 6.1. Provenance equality remains core-owned.

Sources:

- `party`: `header.partyState.isKimInParty` / `.isCunoInParty`. These two booleans are the complete current subset. Inclusion requires a direct, player-observable companion-presence fact with a concrete spoiler-safe guidance use. Every other `partyState` field remains intentionally uncovered; this design does not infer its meaning. Extend the subset only after verifying the field's player-visible semantics, spoiler safety, consumer value, and diff-noise behavior, then decide whether the semantic change requires an `adapterVersion` bump.
- `reputation`: TLV store variables. The digest keys above are display names, NOT store variable names; the adapter maintains an explicit digest-key -> store-variable-name map validated against `variables_reputation.json` on every digest build:

  | Digest path | Store variable |
  |---|---|
  | `reputation.communist` | `reputation.communist` |
  | `reputation.moralist` | `reputation.moralist` |
  | `reputation.ultraliberal` | `reputation.ultraliberal` |
  | `reputation.nationalist` | `reputation.revacholian_nationhood` |
  | `reputation.kim` | `reputation.kim` |
  | `reputation.copStyle.sorry` | `reputation.sorry_cop` |
  | `reputation.copStyle.superstar` | `reputation.superstar_cop` |
  | `reputation.copStyle.apocalypse` | `reputation.apocalypse_cop` |
  | `reputation.copStyle.boring` | `reputation.boring_cop` |
  | `reputation.copStyle.art` | `reputation.art_cop` |

  The exact ten-key current subset consists of four political-alignment counters, the Kim relationship counter, and five named cop-style counters. Inclusion requires a stable high-level role-play or relationship axis with a concrete spoiler-safe guidance use, without interpreting dialogue content or hidden trigger rules. The other 11 vendored axes are intentionally uncovered rather than implicitly unsupported. Add an axis only after verifying the same semantics, spoiler, consumer, and diff-noise criteria and deciding whether the change requires an `adapterVersion` bump. A mapped variable absent from the SAVE's store takes the vendored DB's initial value for that axis.
- `thoughts`: from the `characterSheet` arrays only: `cooking` := `cookingThoughts`, `fixed` := `fixedThoughts`, `forgotten` := `forgottenThoughts`, `known` := `gainedThoughts` minus the other three (encountered but not started; forgotten ids are already absent from `gainedThoughts` per section 4, so that subtraction is defensive). Before subtraction, ordinal validation requires `cookingThoughts` and `fixedThoughts` to be subsets of `gainedThoughts`, `forgottenThoughts` to be disjoint from `gainedThoughts`, and `cookingThoughts` to be disjoint from `fixedThoughts`. These relationships also make forgotten disjoint from cooking and fixed. Membership and subtraction use `StringComparer.Ordinal`. Each source array retains its duplicates through validation so an invalid duplicate fails rather than disappearing in a set conversion. After validation, all four emitted arrays sort by ordinal identifier; the DE `extra` validator rejects noncanonical order. The arrays therefore encode sets deterministically even though the game-neutral extra differ compares arrays as sequences. The catalog-shaped `thoughtListState` is never read (section 4's catalog-vs-owned rule; a naive state-based mapping over it would leak every never-encountered thought id).
- `whiteChecks`: counts over `world.failedWhiteChecksHolder` (`WhiteCheckCache` entry count -> retryable; `SeenWhiteCheckCache` entries whose `isOnlySeen` is true, an absent field counting as false -> seenOnly). The current adapter exposes counts only: the cache entries embed dialogue precondition text; counts are safe. The current snapshot retains the counts for spoiler-safe, separately researched check guidance and inventory loadout advice without exposing identities. Their aggregate delta is not an actionable event and would add recap noise, so adapter.json excludes `/extra/whiteChecks` from ordinary diffs. That exclusion intentionally discards the old and new count values, sets `hasUnrepresentedChanges` when they differ, and leaves the latest counts available in the current snapshot.
- `dayPhase`: TLV store `auto.daychange_has_slept` / `auto.daychange_can_sleep`; a variable absent from the SAVE's store maps to literal false, the game's initial value (confirmed against the upstream repo's `variables_auto.json` at spec time and pinned here as a literal, since that DB is not vendored and the historical fallback must not drift with a file).

## 7. Component: differ and state files (core, game-agnostic)

### 7.1 State file lifecycle

Authority model: `current.json` and `baseline.json` are the authoritative pair; `diff-since-last-read.json` is a best-effort derived diagnostic cache, recomputed whenever a digest lands. Consuming reads never trust the cache; `Get-Diff.ps1` recomputes from the authoritative pair (7.3). This on-demand model was chosen over treating the materialized diff file as authoritative because recomputation from the pair removes the pipeline-writer read-reset race (7.3); `consume.lock` separately serializes competing consuming readers. The cache is retained for debugging convenience only.
