Pipeline and consuming cache writers do not share a lock, and baseline-read fallback may deliberately write a canonical empty cache, so the file may be stale or falsely empty. In particular, cached `hasChanges: false` is never evidence that no changes are pending.

`Read-CompanionJson` is the sole raw JSON materializer for core entry points and adapter PowerShell code. It has exactly two mutually exclusive input parameter sets plus one mandatory shared budget: `Read-CompanionJson -Path <string> -Budget <profile>` and `Read-CompanionJson -Json <string> -Budget <profile>`. `-Path` is the default positional set, opens the literal path with the share-friendly read mode below, and reads its complete UTF-8 bytes once. `-Json` is named-only and encodes the supplied .NET string as strict UTF-8. Neither route guesses whether a string is a path. Both run the same syntax, depth, recursive ordinal duplicate-property, and resource validation before materialization.

The budget profile is exact and caller-owned:

| Profile | Complete consumers | Max UTF-8 bytes | Max JSON tokens | Max decoded string UTF-8 bytes |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `config` | adapter.json, vendored JSON DBs, `last-processed.json`, terminal diagnostics, and 7.6's `consumer.json`, outbox ledger, receipts, and settlement results | 1,048,576 | 65,536 | 524,288 |
| `dump` | complete or variables-only dump documents | 16,777,216 | 2,000,000 | 8,388,608 |
| `digest` | fresh, current, baseline, and cached-state digests | 8,388,608 | 500,000 | 4,194,304 |
| `diff` | generated, cached, and stdout diff documents plus walk warning arrays | 16,777,216 | 1,000,000 | 8,388,608 |
| `walk-result` | idle walk-back result documents | 16,777,216 | 1,000,000 | 8,388,608 |
| `turn-context` | complete turn-context envelopes | 33,554,432 | 2,000,000 | 16,777,216 |
| `state-event` | complete state-event outbox records (7.6) | 33,554,432 | 2,000,000 | 16,777,216 |

These tiers protect different boundaries and intentionally do not imply that every independently accepted source shape can be published. Archive-member and TLV limits bound preallocation and decoded amplification while preserving compatibility with source data; the smaller publication profiles define the supported interchange envelopes, and a larger parse-valid projection fails closed at its bounded writer. On 2026-08-09 the largest retained local fixtures were 3,059,759 bytes for a complete dump, 55,242 bytes for a digest, and 2,735 bytes for a diff. The 16 MiB dump boundary therefore retained more than five times observed dump headroom; the 8 MiB digest, 16 MiB diff and walk, and 32 MiB envelope tiers reserve progressively compositional room without promising that simultaneous per-component maxima fit. Aligning source limits to publication limits was rejected because it would turn an interchange tuning boundary into an unnecessary source compatibility restriction.

All limits are inclusive. A token is each token reported by `Utf8JsonReader`, including a property name. Decoded-string bytes are the strict UTF-8 byte counts of every property name and string value after JSON escape decoding. `-Path` reads at most its byte cap plus one before rejection. Core owns one `UTF8Encoding(false, true)` instance and `-Json` uses that same throwing encoder for `GetByteCount` and `GetBytes`; isolated UTF-16 surrogates raise `EncoderFallbackException` rather than becoming U+FFFD. It rejects an excess from `GetByteCount` before allocating encoded bytes. The existing duplicate/depth prepass charges tokens and decoded text before `JsonDocument` creation or PowerShell materialization. Crossing any limit throws `CompanionJsonParseException`; file I/O before bytes are available retains its ordinary infrastructure classification.

On success the helper returns exactly one module-owned `CompanionJsonValue`. Its `Materialized` property is `[object]`: a hashtable for an object root, an object array for an array root, the corresponding scalar for a scalar root, or null for a JSON null root. Its `RootElement` property is an owned cloned `System.Text.Json.JsonElement` derived from the same bytes and remains valid after the helper disposes its temporary `JsonDocument`.

Every Companion JSON producer uses the matching profile through a bounded UTF-8 writer: dedump output uses `dump`, mapper output uses `digest`, the core differ and walk warning array use `diff`, the walk result uses `walk-result`, the wrapper uses `turn-context`, 7.6's record commit uses `state-event`, and terminal diagnostics use `config` before their stricter 4,096-byte record cap, as do 7.6's marker, ledger, receipts, and results. The writer stops before a cap-plus-one byte, token, or decoded-text charge and maps that rejection through the site's existing serialization or document-failure route. Dedump renders its own case as `dedump: output: output-budget-exceeded` and publishes no stdout or destination. These inequalities ensure every accepted producer document fits every downstream reader; persisted external or corrupt bytes are still checked independently on read.

Invalid encoding, including `EncoderFallbackException` from `-Json`, JSON syntax, duplicate properties, depth overflow, and materialization failures throw `CompanionJsonParseException`, preserving the underlying exception when one exists. File open and read failures from `-Path` propagate their original `System.IO` exception unchanged, so baseline classification distinguishes infrastructure failure from bytes that were read but invalid. Parameter binding rejects both inputs, neither input, and an empty path before the helper runs.

Expected content rejection uses one closed exception hierarchy. Abstract `CompanionContentException` derives from `System.Exception`; `CompanionJsonParseException`, `CompanionDocumentValidationException`, `AdapterDigestValidationException`, `CompanionDiffCompatibilityException`, `CompanionDiffSemanticException`, and `TurnContextProtocolException` derive from it. Running core owns the shared `AdapterDigestValidationException` declaration; an adapter release validator throws that exact loaded type and never declares a private same-named type. The current PowerShell class hierarchy keeps exception ownership inside the self-contained core module and avoids a separate compiled-type mechanism. PowerShell class visibility therefore requires core to import the validator from the type-owning module scope: a top-level import of the same separate module cannot resolve the class at invocation, while a nested import preserves exact identity. A shared CLR exception assembly is viable but was rejected as extra packaging, not because CLR identity fails. A validator wraps a lower-level expected content rejection only when it must change the domain type and preserves that exception as `InnerException`; in particular, turn-context validators wrap nested parse or document rejection in `TurnContextProtocolException`. Unexpected exceptions, including injected validator faults, propagate unchanged as implementation or infrastructure failures. Callers catch only the exact expected domain type for a recoverable rejected document; they never catch the base type to convert programmer faults into content-invalid state.

Adapter configuration, vendored DBs, dump documents, digest documents, baseline/current/cache state, emitted diffs, private and delivered turn-context envelopes, walk-result documents, walk warning arrays, terminal diagnostics, watcher bookkeeping in `last-processed.json`, and the 7.6 consumer marker, outbox records, outbox ledger, receipts, and settlement results are its complete consumer surface. No caller may materialize JSON first and attempt duplicate detection afterward. The duplicate check is syntax-level and applies inside adapter-owned `extra` data as well as fixed-schema objects; it does not impose fixed fields beneath `extra`. Callers use `Materialized` only for already validated ordinary orchestration values. Structural and numeric checks, arbitrary-subtree comparison, and arbitrary-subtree emission use `RootElement`.

`diff-since-last-read.json` lifecycle: the pipeline differ creates or refreshes it after a committed digest, and the shared consuming operation, or its 7.6 outbox-mode sibling, refreshes it after baseline advancement; all use atomic replacement. A parse, schema, or write failure makes the diagnostic cache unavailable or stale and is repaired by the next successful producer. Its scope is the companion root. Its complete machine-consumer list is empty: the runtime, guidance flow, watcher trigger, and standing state-acquisition procedure never read it. A human may inspect it only as a diagnostic artifact; `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek` recomputes from the authoritative pair when a non-consuming current view is required.

`baseline.json` lifecycle (single logical writer by design):

- Creation and refresh: the shared consuming operation in `DigestDiff.psm1` writes baseline := the exact current-digest snapshot whose validated diff payload was built. Consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` and `Get-TurnContext.ps1` are the complete caller list under bridge ownership; neither script implements baseline advancement independently. Under runtime ownership the outbox-mode operation of 7.6 is the writer instead. When baseline is absent (first run), the substitute is the target snapshot itself, so the first consuming read emits an empty diff by design.
- Invalidation: a `baseline.json` whose bytes were read successfully but whose JSON is invalid, whose `schemaVersion`, `game`, or `adapterVersion` differs from the target, whose `source.domainId` differs from the target, or whose source identity is invalid is content-invalid. Missing and content-invalid baselines follow the reset path with a typed note; consuming entry scripts render or carry it according to their process contract. A file-open, access, sharing, stream-read, or other infrastructure failure is not invalid content and must never authorize reset. Successfully read content-invalid bytes cannot provide a trustworthy prior state, so reset is the only recoverable comparison. An infrastructure failure can instead conceal a still-valid baseline; resetting then could erase a pending baseline-to-target change that a later retry could recover. The pipeline differ NEVER writes baseline: with baseline absent or content-invalid it writes the canonical empty diff to the cache, notes why on stderr, and leaves baseline alone for the next consuming read to establish.
- Scope: per companion install (this folder); single configured game timeline and one logical Companion consumer for the current bridge. The core does not infer playthrough identity.
- Complete writer list: under bridge ownership, the shared consuming operation, invoked by consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` or `Get-TurnContext.ps1`; under runtime ownership, 7.6's reconciliation apply and commit apply; and 7.6's abandon release, which deletes the file. Complete reader list: the pipeline differ step invoked by `Watch-Saves.ps1`, `Update-CompanionState.ps1`, or a `Get-TurnContext.ps1` or `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` refresh; the shared consuming operation and its 7.6 outbox-mode sibling; 7.6 reconciliation; and non-consuming `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek`. Nothing else touches it.

The shared baseline reader returns exactly four typed outcomes:

| Outcome | Definition | Pipeline-cache behavior | Consuming or peek behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| `valid` | File opened and read, JSON parsed, core structure and adapter semantics admitted, versions and source identity valid | Compare from it. | Compare from it. |
| `absent` | Direct open returned typed `FileNotFoundException` or `DirectoryNotFoundException` | Write canonical empty cache with a note. | Substitute target, emit baseline reset. |
| `content-invalid` | Bytes were read completely, then JSON parsing, core or adapter validation, version validation, or source-identity validation failed | Write canonical empty cache with a note. | Substitute target, emit baseline reset. |
| `infrastructure-failure` | Open or read failed for sharing, access, device, stream, or any reason other than typed file-not-found | Write canonical empty cache with a cautionary note because the cache is derived; never touch baseline. | Exit `[compute]` before payload emission or mutation. |

Implementation separates file open/read from JSON parsing so the outcome cannot be inferred from one catch-all exception. The infrastructure classification is fail-safe: an unrecognized open or read exception maps there, never to absent or content-invalid. No Boolean existence predicate has reset authority because such APIs can collapse access and other infrastructure failures into a false absence result.

Structural-validation handling is complete by caller:

| Reader | Missing | Infrastructure failure | JSON, core-structural, or adapter-semantic rejection | Version, adapter, or game mismatch | Source-identity failure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Baseline reader | `absent`. | `infrastructure-failure`. | `content-invalid`. | `content-invalid`. | `content-invalid`. |
| Pipeline or walk fresh digest | Its typed stage failure. | Its typed stage failure. | `[digest]` before commit or comparison. | `[digest]` before commit or comparison. | `[digest]` before commit or comparison. |
| `Get-Diff.ps1` target | `[missing-current]`. | `[missing-current]`. | `[missing-current]`. | `[stale-current]`. | `[missing-current]`. |
| `Get-TurnContext.ps1` or 7.6 `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` initial current gate | Treat as absent, append `pipeline-current-self-healed`, and refresh. | Treat as absent, append `pipeline-current-self-healed`, and refresh. | Treat as absent, append `pipeline-current-self-healed`, and refresh. | Treat as absent, append `pipeline-current-self-healed`, and refresh. | Treat as absent, append `pipeline-current-self-healed`, and refresh. |
| `Get-TurnContext.ps1` or 7.6 `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` post-refresh target | `[freshness]` and reacquire within budget. | `[freshness]` and reacquire within budget. | `[freshness]` and reacquire within budget. | `[freshness]` and reacquire within budget. | `[freshness]` and reacquire within budget. |
| Watcher trigger prefilter plus admission | Treat as absent with a caution so the next cycle self-heals. | Emit a caution, skip this poll, clear its stability sample, and retry the read on a later poll. | Treat as absent with a caution so the next cycle self-heals. | Treat as absent with a caution so the next cycle self-heals. | Treat as absent with a caution so the next cycle self-heals. |
| Commit guard prefilter plus admission | Treat as absent and self-heal. | Fail closed with `failurePhase: commit`; preserve current and skip cache and bookkeeping. | Treat as absent and self-heal. | Treat as absent and self-heal. | Treat as absent and self-heal. |

The missing and infrastructure columns distinguish absence from failed access, while the rejection column covers bytes that were read but invalid under either owned schema; only the baseline reader gives that distinction reset authority. Before baseline advancement, a failure inside either validator implementation itself, rather than a rejected document, is `[compute]` for a consuming operation and `[digest]` for a fresh mapper output. A watcher trigger validator-infrastructure failure skips the poll, and a commit guard validator-infrastructure failure is `failurePhase: commit`; neither treats the current document as absent. The post-baseline cache-target case follows 7.3's consumed failure rule instead. No caller silently passes a partial document to the differ.

`consume.lock` lifecycle: every consuming caller opens this path with an exclusive cross-process file handle before freezing its authoritative `current.json` target or reading `baseline.json`, and holds the handle through payload construction, any `Get-Diff.ps1` raw emission, and both consuming writes. The turn-context wrapper delivers its selected private result only after the operation releases the lock. Creation uses open-or-create; the file has no meaningful content and remains after release, while handle disposal on every success or failure releases ownership. It is never refreshed or invalidated. Its scope is the companion root, and its complete consumer list is consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` and `Get-TurnContext.ps1` through the shared operation, plus the three 7.6 scripts `Publish-StateEvent.ps1`, `Set-StateEventSettlement.ps1`, and `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1`. A caller waits for the single module-owned literal interval of 10 seconds, then exits `[busy]` without reading or writing the baseline if another consumer still owns the handle. This lock prevents concurrent baseline mutation; it does not turn two sequential clients into two recipients of the same diff. The product contract remains one logical Companion consumer; 7.6's ownership check is what enforces it between the bridge and runtime roles.

`cycle.lock` lifecycle: watcher, one-shot, and turn-context refresh paths open this path with an exclusive cross-process file handle before the selected candidate enters `Invoke-SaveDigest`'s source-receipt protocol. The owner holds it through the `current.json` publication, derived-cache attempt, `last-processed.json` bookkeeping, and finally-scoped work cleanup, then releases it by disposing the handle on every success or failure. Creation uses open-or-create; the file has no meaningful content, remains after release, and is never refreshed or invalidated. Its scope is the companion root, and those three pipeline paths, plus the identical refresh path of 7.6's `Publish-StateEvent.ps1`, are its complete consumer list. After acquiring it, the caller re-enumerates saves and revalidates the selected candidate before source acceptance: the watcher abandons a changed selection and starts a fresh sample sequence, while one-shot and turn-context repeat their normal selection and stability gate. This prevents time spent waiting for the lock from authorizing stale pre-lock observations.

Walk-back intentionally does not acquire `cycle.lock`. It shares extraction plumbing, not publication ownership: each walk invocation uses an isolated leased work group, verifies its own source receipt, and never publishes companion state. Serializing that read-only enrichment behind a publication cycle was rejected because it would add bounded busy or blocking behavior without protecting shared authoritative state.

The watcher never waits for an owned `cycle.lock`; it notes the busy cycle, skips the poll, and retries through its ordinary next poll. One-shot and turn-context wait for the module-owned literal interval of 300 seconds, covering both sequential adapter commands' 120-second limits plus source receipt and bounded publication work. Live contention after that wait is `[busy]`; path creation, open, access, or I/O failure is `[lock]`. The one-shot exits 1; the turn-context attempt stops without invoking or consuming a cycle; and neither case alters pipeline or baseline state. `cycle.lock` is never held while acquiring `consume.lock`: a turn-context refresh releases the cycle lock before it begins the shared consuming operation, so the two locks have no nested acquisition order.

`source.domainId` lifecycle: every successful adapter resolution computes one lowercase SHA-256 acquisition-domain identifier before save enumeration. The hash input is a length-prefixed UTF-8 tuple with literal domain tag `companion-source-domain-v2`, the selected adapter directory's normalized full path, the validated `saveDirectory`'s normalized full path, and the exact configured `saveGlob`, in that order. Each length is an unsigned 32-bit big-endian byte count. Length framing makes tuple boundaries unambiguous, so two distinct path and glob partitions cannot alias through raw concatenation. The literal tag namespaces this framing recipe, allowing a later recipe to evolve without sharing the old identifier space. On Windows, full-path normalization replaces the alternate separator with the directory separator, preserves a root's trailing separator while trimming it elsewhere. The two normalized path spellings are hashed ordinally without case conversion or Unicode normalization. This conservative identity may treat case-only or other alias spellings of one physical directory as a new domain and safely force reprocessing, rather than risk merging distinct filesystem locations. `saveGlob` remains exact because it is adapter configuration, not a filesystem path. Only the hash is persisted or enters machine documents; absolute paths do not.

The identifier is created with each validated adapter snapshot, is immutable for that snapshot, and is refreshed only by resolving a later snapshot. It is stamped into every digest, current-source envelope, and watcher bookkeeping record. Its complete consumers are current and baseline validation, source comparability and floors, commit guards, watcher trigger and restart recovery, the watcher stability-sample key, post-digest provenance validation, the section 10 guidance-phase configuration-drift gate, walk comparisons, and 7.6's producer-mode record validation. A domain mismatch is never ordered: a current or baseline digest from another domain is stale or content-invalid under its caller's existing route, bookkeeping from another domain does not match, and an in-memory watcher sample is cleared. This prevents state from one selected adapter or save selector being accepted merely because another domain contains the same filename and timestamp. The existing `game` and `adapterVersion` checks remain semantic compatibility checks within this acquisition boundary.

`last-processed.json` lifecycle: written after a processed cycle by the watcher, the one-shot, or a `Get-TurnContext.ps1` refresh with exactly three fields, `{ "domainId": "<lowercase SHA-256 hex>", "name": "<save file name>", "lastWriteTimeUtc": "<ISO 8601 round-trip ('o') format>" }`; the trigger comparison parses the timestamp back to a DateTime, and the 'o' format round-trips to tick precision, so exact tuple equality is well-defined across the persistence boundary (a lossy encoding here would make every poll re-trigger, invisibly thanks to the commit guard). Read inside the poll gate, guarded: unparseable or missing content, including a domain mismatch, is treated as absent (reprocess the newest save once, note on stderr, never crash the poll loop). The trigger predicate is pair inequality: a cycle runs when the resolved domain plus newest matching file's (name, LastWriteTimeUtc) tuple differs from the stored tuple, so a deleted newest file triggers reprocessing of its predecessor (harmless: the commit guard discards regressions). Deleting the file is always safe.

Every freshness and commit comparison uses one shared source-domain and source-identity order rather than timestamp alone. A save candidate's identity is `(domainId, LastWriteTimeUtc, Name)` and a digest's identity is `(source.domainId, source.savedAt, source.saveFile)`. Identities with different domains are incomparable. Within one domain, compare timestamps first and, on equality, compare filenames with `[string]::CompareOrdinal`; the greater identity is newer, matching `Get-SaveCandidates` exactly. Missing, unreadable, core-invalid, adapter-invalid, or version-stale digest identity is not comparable and maps to the cautionary stale or absent path at the caller. Candidate selection, the watcher trigger, the pipeline commit guard, the turn-context freshness floor, the shared consumer's stale-target check, `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek`'s unlocked stale-target check, walk-result span-order validation, traversal-evidence replay, and 7.6's outbox-mode floor check, stale-target check, and reconciliation superseded test (which, for a record whose own predecessor is no longer the baseline, alone breaks a tie between differing digests of one source identity, by `generatedAt`) are the complete consumers of this ordering. This prevents an equal-timestamp digest from the non-selected filename from being accepted as current.

All state-file writes everywhere in the pipeline are write-to-temp-then-replace on the target with temp and target on the same NTFS volume. For an absent target, the writer uses `[IO.File]::Move(temp, target)` without overwrite. For an existing target, it creates a second unique target-local backup sibling and calls `[IO.File]::Replace(temp, target, backup)`. The backup is then best-effort deleted only when this writer owns it. A live probe on 2026-08-10 established that `File.Move(temp, target, $true)` and `MoveFileExW` return access denied under a reader opened with ReadWrite+Delete sharing, while `File.Replace` succeeds and preserves the old reader's view of the replaced bytes. Both branches publish only complete bytes, so a reader never observes a partial document. Two companion rules make this hold under concurrency, both discovered by the live probe: pipeline READS open the file with ReadWrite+Delete sharing (a default-sharing reader holding the destination open blocks the replace with a sharing violation for the whole read). The WRITE attempts the move immediately and at most five times total. Only an `IOException` whose Win32 code is `ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION` (32) is retryable. After failures on attempts 1 through 4 it waits 40, 80, 120, and 160 milliseconds, respectively. A fifth sharing violation is rethrown unchanged with no further delay. Every other exception, including `UnauthorizedAccessException` and a non-sharing `IOException`, is rethrown unchanged after the first attempt. The replacement backup uses the same exact target-local random sibling grammar as the temp and has separate ownership tracking, so cleanup never deletes another writer's backup. Temp locations: the pipeline's digest temp lives in `.tmp\` as a per-run work file (section 8); every other state write generates 16 random bytes and creates one unique sibling `<target>.<32-lowercase-hex>.tmp` with `FileMode.CreateNew`, write access, and `FileShare.None`. It writes the complete bytes, flushes, closes, then runs the bounded move above. A local `tempOwned` flag begins false, becomes true only after this writer's `CreateNew` succeeds, and returns to false after a successful move transfers the path. The finally block best-effort deletes the exact temp only while `tempOwned` is true. A writer whose nonce collides and whose `CreateNew` fails therefore never deletes the other writer's temp. The nonce is scoped to one write and is never reused intentionally; concurrent same-process writes cannot share or truncate an owned temp, and an ordinary failed write can retry immediately without colliding with its orphan. The startup reaper remains the hard-crash and failed-delete backstop. No per-target in-process serialization is required beyond the existing logical locks because atomic replacement defines the winning complete publication for concurrently writable derived state.

### 7.2 Diff shape

```jsonc
{
  "schemaVersion": 2,
  "adapterVersion": 2,
  "game": "disco_elysium",
  "hasChanges": true,                             // represented gameplay delta exists
  "hasUnrepresentedChanges": false,                // ignored or excluded game-state fields changed
  "baselineGeneratedAt": "...",                    // null when no baseline existed (first run / invalidated)
  "currentGeneratedAt": "...",
  "currentSource": {
    "domainId": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef",
    "saveFile": "...",
    "savedAt": "...",
    "byteLength": 1048576,
    "contentSha256": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"
  },
  "sessionChange": { "from": { "day": 3, "time": "23:46" }, "to": { "day": 4, "time": "08:09" },
                      "locationChanged": true },
  "objectives": {
    "added":         [ { "id": "...", "status": "active", "parentId": null } ],
    "removed":       [ { "id": "...", "lastStatus": "active", "parentId": null } ],
    "statusChanged": [ { "id": "...", "from": "active", "to": "done", "parentId": null } ]
  },
  "character": { "levelDelta": 0, "xpDelta": 12, "moneyDelta": -3.50,
                  "skillChanges": [ { "name": "Logic", "from": 7, "to": 8 },
                                    { "name": "New Skill", "from": null, "to": 3 } ] },
  "inventory": { "gained": ["id"], "lost": ["id"], "equippedChanged": true },
  "extraChanges": [
    { "path": "/extra/thoughts/fixed", "fromPresent": true, "toPresent": true,
      "from": ["id"], "to": ["id", "other_id"] },
    { "path": "/extra/reputation/kim", "fromPresent": true, "toPresent": true,
      "from": -4, "to": -2 }
  ]
}
```

`Read-DiffDocument` owns the complete core diff validator and always materializes through `Read-CompanionJson` budget `diff`. It uses the same ordinal, case-sensitive exact-field, timestamp, numeric, and no-coercion rules as `Read-DigestDocument`. It returns the same validated `CompanionJsonValue` on success, throws a typed structural validation exception on failure, and never calls `exit` or returns a partial document. The required surface is:

| Path | Required type and invariants |
|---|---|
| Root envelope | Object containing exactly integer `schemaVersion`, integer `adapterVersion`, non-empty string `game`, Boolean `hasChanges`, Boolean `hasUnrepresentedChanges`, canonical UTC `o` string-or-null `baselineGeneratedAt`, canonical UTC `o` string `currentGeneratedAt`, object `currentSource`, object-or-null `sessionChange`, and object/array children `objectives`, `character`, `inventory`, and `extraChanges`. |
| `currentSource` | Exact five-field source provenance from 6.1. It identifies the acquisition domain and digest used as the comparison target. |
| `sessionChange` | Null or object containing exactly `from`, `to`, and Boolean `locationChanged`. `from` and `to` each contain exactly integer `day` and valid `HH:mm` string `time`. The object is legal only when day, time, or location changed; day and time equality requires `locationChanged: true`. |
| `objectives` | Object containing exactly arrays `added`, `removed`, and `statusChanged`. Across all three arrays the ordinal composite `(parentId, id)` is unique. Null parent sorts before strings. |
| `objectives.added[]` | Object containing exactly non-empty string `id`, valid status `status`, and non-empty string-or-null `parentId`. |
| `objectives.removed[]` | Object containing exactly non-empty string `id`, valid status `lastStatus`, and non-empty string-or-null `parentId`. |
| `objectives.statusChanged[]` | Object containing exactly non-empty string `id`, differing valid statuses `from` and `to`, and non-empty string-or-null `parentId`. |
| `character` | Object containing exactly integer `levelDelta`, integer `xpDelta`, finite decimal `moneyDelta`, and array `skillChanges`. |
| `character.skillChanges[]` | Object containing exactly non-empty string `name`, integer-or-null `from`, and integer-or-null `to`. Names are ordinally unique. Exactly one null means addition or removal; when neither is null the values must differ; both null is illegal. |
| `inventory` | Object containing exactly ordinally unique non-empty-string arrays `gained` and `lost`, plus Boolean `equippedChanged`. The two arrays are disjoint. |
| `extraChanges[]` | Object containing exactly non-empty string `path`, Boolean `fromPresent`, Boolean `toPresent`, and owned cloned `JsonElement` values `from` and `to`, within the standalone diff's maximum depth of 63. Paths are ordinally unique valid RFC 6901 JSON Pointers whose first decoded token is `extra` and which contain at least one further token. Empty tokens are legal. At least one presence flag is true; a false flag requires the corresponding value to be null; when both are true the values must differ under `JsonElement.DeepEquals`. |

`sessionChange` retains day and time endpoints because they date the transition for recap consumers. Location deliberately retains only the fact of movement: the paired current digest already supplies the destination, no current consumer needs the origin, and adding `from.location` and `to.location` was rejected as duplicate payload surface.

Every collection has deterministic ordering: objective buckets sort by parent-id nulls first, then parent id and id ordinally; skills and inventory ids sort ordinally; and `extraChanges` sorts by path ordinally. The validator rejects non-canonical order as well as duplicates. `hasChanges` is recomputed rather than trusted: it is true exactly when `sessionChange` is non-null, any objective bucket or `skillChanges` is non-empty, any character delta is nonzero, either inventory array is non-empty, `equippedChanged` is true, or `extraChanges` is non-empty. When `baselineGeneratedAt` is null, every change surface must have its canonical empty value and both change Booleans must be false because a reset does not claim knowledge of the prior state.

`Read-DiffDocument` cannot recompute non-reset `hasUnrepresentedChanges` from the diff alone because the omitted before and after values are deliberately absent. It validates that the field is Boolean and requires false on a baseline reset, but otherwise treats its value as producer-owned. `Assert-DiffSemantics -Diff <document> -Baseline <digest> -Target <digest> -AdapterConfig <validated AdapterCompatibilityConfig>` is the producer-side semantic validator. It recomputes `hasChanges`, `hasUnrepresentedChanges`, exclusions, and every emitted bucket from the digest pair, throws `CompanionDiffSemanticException` on any mismatch, and returns no sentinel. Every pipeline, consuming, and walk-back differ calls it before emission or state mutation; standalone cache and envelope readers perform structural validation only.

The canonical omitted-field projection is closed. It compares `session.playtimeNote` and `character.unspentPoints` as scalar JSON values; vitals by the ordinal union of `name`, with presence and `value` significant; and `inventory.highlights` as an ordinal set. For skills present under the same ordinal name on both sides it compares `group`; a one-sided skill contributes only its represented skill addition or removal. For objectives present under the same `(parentId, id)` composite on both sides it compares `title`, `acquired`, and `resolved`; a one-sided or reparented objective contributes only its represented objective bucket. Source, generation, version, `money.amount`, `currency`, and `minorUnitsPerUnit` do not participate. Adapter exclusions contribute only through the effective exclusion rules below. Presence inequality or value inequality under `JsonElement.DeepEquals` in any projected member sets `hasUnrepresentedChanges` true. This flag is independent of `hasChanges` and may accompany either value. A consuming read may advance baseline without retaining the omitted values, but it no longer silently loses the fact of a transition.

Objective comparison keys top-level objectives by `(null, id)` and subobjectives by `(parentId, id)`. A top-level id may equal a subobjective id, and the same subobjective id may appear under different parents, without collision or loss. A subobjective moved between parents emits one removal under the old composite and one addition under the new composite. Bucket semantics are otherwise ordinary: added is current-only, removed is baseline-only, and statusChanged is present under the same composite with differing status. A removed objective, negative delta, or backwards session clock is expected when the player loads an older save. A negative `xpDelta` alongside a positive `levelDelta` is also normal forward play because `character.xp` resets on level-up.

`inventory.gained` and `lost` compare `inventory.owned` sets; `equippedChanged` compares `inventory.equipped` sets. It intentionally retains only the change fact: the paired current digest is the authoritative current loadout, while the diff does not preserve prior loadout history. Owned-item acquisition and loss remain itemized because those identities are the change itself. Character deltas come from `level`, `xp`, `money.minorUnits`, and skills keyed by ordinal name. One-sided skill names emit nullable additions or removals. Because validated `level` and `xp` are each in the closed range 0 through Int64 maximum, current minus baseline is always representable as signed Int64 in either direction. Before money comparison, baseline and current must have ordinally equal currency and identical `minorUnitsPerUnit`; mismatch throws `CompanionDiffCompatibilityException` before payload emission or baseline mutation. `moneyDelta` converts both validated Int64 `minorUnits` values to `System.Numerics.BigInteger`, subtracts there, converts the exact difference to Decimal with checked conversion, and divides once by the validated positive Decimal `minorUnitsPerUnit`. It emits the result as a Decimal using shortest round-trip decimal serialization. Int64 subtraction, Double conversion, and subtraction of already-divided values are forbidden.

`extraChanges` is game-neutral and lossless. The differ walks the validated digest `RootElement` trees rather than their PowerShell materializations. Each present `from` or `to` value retained in an entry is an owned clone of the corresponding `JsonElement`; `ConvertTo-CompanionJson` writes it directly through `JsonElement.WriteTo`. The differ walks objects recursively by the ordinal union of keys. Arrays are leaves and compare as sequences, so order and multiplicity changes are retained in raw `from` and `to` values. A type change, scalar change, null change, or one-sided path also emits raw values with presence flags that distinguish absent from explicit null. An added or removed empty object emits at that object's path because recursion would otherwise have no leaf. Paths use RFC 6901 JSON Pointer encoding: `/` separates reference tokens, `~` becomes `~0`, `/` inside a key becomes `~1`, and dots have no special meaning. Therefore literal key `a.b`, nested keys `a` then `b`, slash-containing keys, tilde-containing keys, and empty keys remain distinct. Equality uses `System.Text.Json.JsonElement.DeepEquals`: object property order is insignificant, array order and multiplicity are significant, strings compare ordinally, and numeric spellings with the same JSON number value compare equal. Paths in adapter.json `diffExcludePaths` suppress the named diff-addressable node and its complete subtree by decoded, case-sensitive reference-token match. A configured pointer is diff-addressable for one digest pair only when the object walker can visit that exact node before emitting an atomic ancestor: every strict prefix below `extra` that is present on either side must be an object, and at least one side must contain the target node or a descendant reached through a one-sided object. Arrays, scalars, nulls, and cross-side type changes are atomic leaves. A pointer descending through one is valid RFC 6901 configuration but unresolved for diff purposes; it cannot suppress raw content inside the emitted ancestor and does not contribute to `hasUnrepresentedChanges`. Resolution runs independently for every configured pointer against the unpruned raw baseline/current pair before any subtree is suppressed. A pointer therefore remains resolved when a configured ancestor also resolves. After all pointers are classified, the differ forms the suppression frontier by dropping each resolved pointer that has a resolved strict ancestor. Filtering and omitted-change projection use that frontier; unused-path warnings are produced only for pointers that failed independent raw-pair resolution, not for resolved descendants removed from the frontier. Configuration order cannot change resolution, warnings, emitted changes, or `hasUnrepresentedChanges`. A pointer unresolved for diff purposes on the pair earns one typed `diff-exclude-path-unused` warning per diff computation. The caller adds it to its operation-wide typed sink, which deduplicates `(code, detail)` before an entry point renders stderr or turn-context carries `warnings`. To exclude an array change, configuration names the array node itself, such as `/extra/list`, never an element beneath it.

The canonical empty diff is the full envelope with the target's `currentGeneratedAt` and `currentSource`, both change Booleans false, `sessionChange` null, empty arrays in every bucket, zero deltas, and `equippedChanged` false. Every emission site writes or prints this exact shape; consumers validate with `Read-DiffDocument` and then test the appropriate change Boolean, never file size or absence. Guidance uses `hasChanges`; diagnostics may also inspect `hasUnrepresentedChanges`. When a diff is paired with a digest, the host also requires `currentGeneratedAt` to equal `state.generatedAt`; the four `currentSource` string fields equal `state.source` ordinally and `byteLength` equals it numerically. Neither timestamp nor receipt alone is treated as sufficient.

Complete validator ownership is closed. The differ runs both `Read-DiffDocument` and `Assert-DiffSemantics` on every document it creates before an emission or state write. A structurally or semantically invalid generated diff is `[compute]` in the shared consuming operation, `[compare]` in walk-back, and a cache-phase failure in the pipeline. `Read-TurnContextEnvelope` and `Read-WalkResultDocument` each structurally validate any populated nested diff again and map failure to their respective `[protocol]` path; neither can recreate producer-only omitted values. 7.6's `Read-StateEventRecord` does the same for a record's nested diff and maps failure to an invalid record. A persisted `diff-since-last-read.json` is diagnostic only; any future manual or presentation reader must call `Read-DiffDocument`, treat rejection as absent, and allow the next pipeline or consuming operation to repair it. No consumer accepts a partially validated diff.

### 7.3 Reset-on-read: `Get-Diff.ps1 [-Adapter <name>] [-Peek]`

`DigestDiff.psm1` owns one shared consuming operation. Its inputs are a validated `AdapterCompatibilityConfig` (or full `AdapterConfig` subtype), an optional minimum source identity, a payload builder, an attempt-local typed warning sink, and delivery mode `rawStdout` or `privateResult`. It acquires `consume.lock`, performs the 7.6 ownership check for role `bridge`, then reads and adapter-admits `current.json` into the exact target snapshot while holding the lock. A supplied minimum identity is the turn-context freshness floor; the target must meet or exceed it. It then reads and validates the baseline, computes and validates the diff, and builds either the raw diff (`Get-Diff.ps1`) or a turn-context envelope value (7.5) in memory before mutation. In `rawStdout` mode it serializes and writes that value before mutation; in `privateResult` mode it keeps the value in the typed result and writes no process stream. Only then does it advance the baseline and refresh the cache. Failed construction, raw serialization, or raw stdout emission skips both writes. A target read or validation failure is returned as a typed pre-baseline failure for the caller to map: Get-Diff uses its missing-current or stale-current class, while turn-context uses freshness and may reacquire. After baseline validation and before diff computation, the operation rejects a target whose source identity precedes the baseline source identity as `[stale-target]`. This monotonic guard never rewinds baseline. If a consumed source has rotated away and an invalid current self-heals to its older predecessor, automatic attempts end in `[stale-target]`; the host directs the player to create a fresh in-game save. That save receives a new source identity, after which the ordinary next transaction can deliver the reverse content diff from the retained baseline to the freshly saved state. No baseline reset or other state mutation occurs before that save. Under bridge ownership this operation is the sole logical baseline writer and authoritative target freezer (7.6 defines its outbox-mode sibling for runtime ownership); entry scripts choose the payload shape and may perform advisory preflight validation, but cannot supply an older frozen target.

The payload boundary is typed and module-owned. `New-ConsumePayload` is the only payload builder and has two exact, mutually exclusive parameter sets. Raw mode is `-DeliveryMode rawStdout -Diff <CompanionJsonValue>`; `State` and `Context` are forbidden, and it returns the immutable validated diff `CompanionJsonValue`. Private mode is `-DeliveryMode privateResult -Diff <CompanionJsonValue> -State <CompanionJsonValue> -Context <TurnContextPayloadContext>`; all three values are mandatory and non-null.

`TurnContextPayloadContext` is a module-owned immutable value with exactly typed source identity `SourceFloor`, Boolean `RefreshAttempted`, Boolean `RefreshCommitObserved`, and Int32 `RefreshAttempts` from 0 through 3. The identity has the section 7.5 exact `domainId`, `saveFile`, and `savedAt` fields. Context validation requires `RefreshAttempted == (RefreshAttempts > 0)`, requires `RefreshCommitObserved` to imply `RefreshAttempted`, and requires `State.source` to meet or exceed `SourceFloor` in the same domain. Private construction also validates the complete diff/state pairing, adapterVersion, game, provenance, generation-time, and baseline-reset invariants used by the later envelope before returning. Missing, malformed, or cross-inconsistent private inputs are therefore `[compute]` before baseline mutation, never a deferred post-consumption protocol failure.

Private mode returns one owned `TurnContextPayloadCandidate`: those validated primitive context values plus immutable `CompanionJsonValue` references for `Diff` and `State`, a Boolean `CacheDegraded` initialized false, and an initially empty typed warning collection. The candidate derives turnContextVersion, adapterVersion, game, and baselineReset from the fixed contract and validated nested documents. It preserves the exact JSON representation of those documents. Only the attempt layer may set `CacheDegraded` and replace `Warnings` with the final sorted and deduplicated warning array before its one serialization. Callers cannot mutate it, and a string, hashtable, or already serialized document is not a legal payload. Ownership transfers into `ConsumeResult`; its selected wrapper attempt is the final consumer, and selection, rejection, or transaction completion invalidates the candidate. No mutation is legal after serialization.

0. Resolve the adapter (section 8 rules) and read `diffExcludePaths`, `adapterVersion`, `game`, and derived `sourceDomainId` from its validated snapshot. A missing, unparseable, or unresolvable adapter.json is exit 1 with a `[config]` stderr message, like the other entry points.
1. In both modes, perform an advisory direct read and validation of `current.json` before any lock open. With `-Peek`, retain it as the exact target snapshot. In consuming mode, discard a successful advisory snapshot and defer the authoritative target read to step 2 under `consume.lock`. If the applicable advisory read is missing or unparseable, or if its `schemaVersion` does not match the version the running core supports, its `adapterVersion` does not match the resolved adapter.json's declared value (stale state from before an upgrade), or its `game` does not match the resolved adapter's (a digest from a different adapter must not be diffed under this one's config), or its `source.domainId` does not equal the held domain: exit 1 with a stderr message directing to run `Update-CompanionState.ps1`, which regenerates it under the current code. The stderr message distinguishes the classes by heal path: an `adapterVersion` mismatch heals via one one-shot run (the bumped adapter.json value flows through the placeholder). A `schemaVersion` mismatch in `current.json` is STALE STATE, not proof of a live lockstep gap (the stamp records the mapper that produced the old digest): one one-shot run also heals it when the mapper has been upgraded alongside core, so the message directs to run the one-shot ONCE; only if that run itself fails on the section 8 post-digest schemaVersion check does a genuine core-vs-mapper lockstep gap exist, and that failure names the stamped vs supported versions and directs to update the lagging mapper. Core schemaVersion bumps are lockstep upgrades of core and every adapter mapper together, by design. (A missing or version-stale current state is a failed check, not an empty diff.) This preflight makes an absent `state\` directory or `current.json` a deterministic `[missing-current]` result before the open-or-create lock path is attempted. A concurrent directory deletion after successful preflight may still produce `[lock]`.
2. In consuming mode, enter the shared operation, which acquires `consume.lock`, freezes and validates the exact `current.json` target, and only then reads `baseline.json`; contention that outlives the bounded wait exits `[busy]` with no payload or state-file mutation, while non-contention lock-path open, access, or I/O failure exits `[lock]` with the same guarantees. A target that became missing, invalid, or version-stale while waiting maps exactly as step 1 and causes no baseline read or mutation. With `-Peek`, read baseline without the lock because peek never writes it, but treat the output as advisory if a consumer runs concurrently. Apply 7.1's typed baseline outcome: `absent` and `content-invalid` substitute the target snapshot (yielding an empty diff) with a typed reset note; `Get-Diff.ps1` renders it to stderr, while turn-context uses `baselineReset`; `infrastructure-failure` exits `[compute]` before payload emission or mutation. For both modes, a valid baseline whose source identity is newer than the target exits `[stale-target]` without stdout or mutation. Peek performs that check immediately after its unlocked baseline read; its result remains advisory because another consumer may advance baseline after the check. The substitution feeds diff COMPUTATION only; the printed and written envelopes set `baselineGeneratedAt` to null in this case, matching the pipeline differ's no-baseline output, so both producers of the no-baseline condition emit the same envelope.
3. Compute diff(baseline snapshot, target snapshot) via `DigestDiff.psm1`, validate it structurally with `Read-DiffDocument` and semantically with `Assert-DiffSemantics`, and build the caller's complete typed payload in memory through `New-ConsumePayload`. Module import, compatibility, differ, or validator failure is `[compute]`. Payload-construction failure is `[compute]` before mutation. In `rawStdout` mode, serialize and write the bytes now; a serialization or stream-write failure is `[emit]`, can leave partial nonparseable bytes, and callers ignore stdout under that class. In `privateResult` mode, retain the typed candidate only in memory. Every failure in this step skips step 4, so nothing is consumed and the next explicit read can recompute the complete payload.
4. Consuming mode only (default): write the authoritative target SNAPSHOT from step 2 to `baseline.json`, then rewrite `diff-since-last-read.json` as diff(new baseline, a FRESH read of `current.json` at this moment), rather than unconditionally writing an empty diff. This sample gives the diagnostic cache its best available view of a pipeline digest that landed during consumption, but another cache writer may supersede it before or after replacement. No correctness decision may depend on the result. When nothing different is sampled, the fresh read equals the snapshot and the cache is the canonical empty diff. Validate the fresh digest against the held adapter configuration through `Read-AdapterDigestDocument` before comparing it. An absent, unreadable, core-invalid, adapter-invalid, or version-incompatible fresh digest uses the documented canonical-empty fallback with a typed `consume-cache-fallback` warning; `Get-Diff.ps1` renders it to stderr while the turn-context attempt retains it for the envelope. This handled fallback is non-fatal because the cache is non-authoritative and self-heals on the next save or consuming read. An unexpected implementation failure inside fresh-target validation is `[reset-write:consumed]` / `cache-target-invalid`, because baseline already advanced; it is not the handled content-invalid fallback and cannot map to pre-mutation `[compute]`. Once a valid fresh digest enters cache comparison, any compare, cache serialization, temp-write, or replacement failure is `[reset-write:consumed]`, since baseline has already advanced. A failure writing the canonical-empty fallback has the same class. With `-Peek`, skip this step entirely: peek is for manual checks that must not consume the diff (user request 2026-08-07).

The shared operation returns a typed `ConsumeResult` with exactly string `outcomeClass`, string `consumptionState`, module-owned `payload`, Boolean `cacheDegraded`, and string-or-null `detail`, plus module-owned `targetDocument` or null. `detail` is null on success and otherwise is produced by the shared terminal-detail registry below. The producer enforces the 512 Unicode-scalar limit before returning `ConsumeResult`; exception messages, paths, command lines, child streams, and arbitrary JSON or save values cannot enter it. `targetDocument` carries the admitted locked-final digest only when a payloadless `stale-target` or post-freeze `compute` result otherwise has no authoritative target container. It is created from the locked `current.json` read, is never refreshed, and exists only until the attempt stamps diagnostic provenance. It is null before a locked target is admitted, for every payload-bearing result whose private payload already owns the target state, and for raw-output failures. It is never serialized directly or persisted. The attempt falls back to the admitted preflight target only when `targetDocument` is null. Structured class values are canonical unbracketed tokens; prose renders a token such as `compute` as `[compute]`. Its complete legal tuples are:

| Outcome token | Consumption | Payload | Cache degraded | Detail | Target document |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `success` | `consumed` | Complete mode-specific payload above | false | null | null |
| `reset-write:not-consumed` | `notConsumed` | Complete mode-specific payload above | false | non-empty text | null |
| `reset-write:consumed` | `consumed` | Complete mode-specific payload above | true | non-empty text | null |
| `busy`, `lock`, or `freshness` | `none` | null | false | non-empty text | null |
| `config`, from the 7.6 ownership check only | `none` | null | false | non-empty text | null |
| `stale-target` | `none` | null | false | non-empty text | Admitted locked-final digest |
| `compute` before locked-final admission | `none` | null | false | non-empty text | null |
| `compute` after locked-final admission | `none` | null | false | non-empty text | Admitted locked-final digest |
| `emit` in rawStdout mode | `none` | null, even when a private value was built before the stream failure | false | non-empty text | null |

Every other tuple is invalid. `Get-Diff.ps1` maps this result to its historical missing-current or stale-current class from the typed freshness reason before any payload emission; `Invoke-TurnContextAttempt` preserves `freshness` for reacquisition. Neither caller parses detail text.

Race-freedom argument: the reset writes the exact target snapshot the validated diff was computed from, not a post-computation read of `current.json`. The authoritative target freeze occurs after `consume.lock` acquisition. If another consumer first advances the baseline from receipt H1 to a forced equal-identity replacement H2, a delayed consumer cannot later publish H1: after it acquires the lock it freezes current H2 instead. If a pipeline writer lands a newer digest after the locked freeze, that delta remains outstanding relative to the new baseline and appears in the next read. `consume.lock` serializes the two entry-script callers of the shared writer. Pipeline writers and one active consuming operation can interleave freely.

Exit codes: 0 only when the diff was printed AND, in consuming mode, both step 4 writes succeeded, including a successfully written canonical-empty fallback. Exit 1 classes are `[config]`, `[missing-current]`, `[stale-current]`, `[busy]`, `[lock]`, `[stale-target]`, `[compute]`, `[emit]`, and the two reset-write classes. Consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` performs no automatic retry: `[busy]`, `[lock]`, and `[stale-target]` produce no stdout or state-file mutation, and a caller may invoke the command again only as a new explicit read. Compute and emit failures also skip step 4 entirely. A failed baseline write after a successful payload is `[reset-write:not-consumed]`: the changes reappear on one full retry, preserving duplicate-show and never-loss. A failed cache write after a successful baseline write is `[reset-write:consumed]`: this class covers the entire unhandled post-baseline cache phase, not only final replacement. The emitted payload remains the payload for this read, and the caller must not replace it with a second consuming read. The cache is non-authoritative and the next pipeline or consuming operation repairs it. `-Peek` never changes the printed output for the same inputs, only the reset.

### 7.4 Idle walk-back: `Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1 [-Adapter <name>] [-CompanionRoot <path>] [-MaxSaves 8] [-MaxAgeHours 72]`

Purpose: the situation brief (section 10) needs "what happened in the last stretch of real play", but idle autosaves make the consuming diff truthfully uneventful while the last real progress sits several saves back. This entry point walks the on-disk save history backwards, pairwise-diffing neighboring saves, and reports the most recent EVENTFUL span. It is a script rather than session-side prose because the equivalent prose procedure accumulated review findings across three passes, all of the class "prose re-implementing plumbing the pipeline already guards" (review ledger 2026-08-08); the script owns that plumbing once.

Definitions. A diff is UNEVENTFUL iff, computed with the adapter's `diffExcludePaths` (the same exclusions every other diff in the system applies), its content differs from the canonical empty diff (7.2) only in `hasUnrepresentedChanges`, `sessionChange`, and/or `extraChanges` entries whose `path` is tolerated by the adapter's `ambientExtraPaths` (below). `hasUnrepresentedChanges` is tolerated because it deliberately combines high-churn ignored values with other unreported state and carries no actionable delta. Everything else, meaning any objectives bucket, any character delta or skill change, any inventory change, or any non-tolerated extraChange, is progress and makes the diff EVENTFUL. Session clock and day move on their own during idle play; location does NOT (an area transition is player-driven), and tolerating the whole sessionChange bucket anyway is deliberate: a transit-only pair carries nothing briefable (the brief reports current location from current.json independently), so a pure-transit span never headlines a recap, at the accepted cost that the dated span can be older than the last pure movement. `xpDelta` is deliberately NOT tolerated (a passive-check award is progress; decided 2026-08-08, recorded in Hardening). The predicate is an exported core helper:

    Test-UneventfulDiff -Diff <hashtable> -AmbientExtraPaths <string[]>

so tests can pin it and section 10's trigger language references one definition. The comparison basis is the CONTENT buckets only: the derived `hasChanges` summary flag (a disjunction over those buckets, 7.2) and the envelope's provenance stamps are outside it, so a sessionChange-only diff, whose `hasChanges` is true, is still uneventful. `hasUnrepresentedChanges` is also explicitly tolerated below. Tolerance is token-aware: parse both paths as RFC 6901 JSON Pointers, then compare decoded reference-token arrays ordinally. A change is tolerated when all prefix tokens equal the change's leading tokens and the prefix has no more tokens than the change. Thus `/extra/dayPhase` tolerates `/extra/dayPhase/hasSlept` but never `/extra/dayPhaseExtra` nor a case variant. String-prefix comparison is forbidden because it mishandles token boundaries and escaping. Section 7.2 uses the identical decoded-token rule for exclusions. No diagnostic at all is emitted for an `ambientExtraPaths` prefix that resolves to no node (deliberate anti-goal, standing on observability: an ineffective ambient prefix surfaces as an eventful diff whose extraChanges the brief shows, so it cannot hide; adding a note would also just multiply stderr noise the way the retained `diffExcludePaths` note already does, firing once per diff computation and hence up to MaxSaves-1 times in a walk with a mistyped exclusion entry, an accepted cost 7.2 now states).

`ambientExtraPaths` is a new OPTIONAL adapter.json key: an array of strings, each a valid JSON Pointer rooted beneath `/extra`, default `[]`, validated by the section 8 startup contract under the same rules as `diffExcludePaths`. Consumers, complete list: `Get-AdapterConfig -Profile Acquisition` validates it. `Test-UneventfulDiff` is the direct policy reader used by `Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1` traversal, by `Read-WalkResultDocument` and `Assert-WalkResultSemantics` using their one held AdapterConfig, and by section 10's session-opening guidance trigger using one distinct guidance-phase acquisition snapshot held through walk-result validation. The pipeline cycle, the watcher trigger, and `Get-Diff.ps1` ignore it by design. It must never be folded into `diffExcludePaths`: ambient paths still appear in ordinary diffs (they are real changes; they are merely not progress). The DE adapter sets `["/extra/dayPhase"]` (6.3: the dayPhase booleans are clock/sleep derived).

Every validated acquisition `AdapterConfig` also derives lowercase SHA-256 `walkPolicyId`. Its framed byte sequence is: length-prefixed UTF-8 literal `companion-walk-policy-v1`; unsigned 32-bit big-endian count of `diffExcludePaths`; each canonical pointer from that array sorted ordinally and encoded as an unsigned 32-bit big-endian UTF-8 byte length followed by its bytes; then the same count and sorted-entry encoding for `ambientExtraPaths`. The separate counts distinguish the two policy sets and sorting makes array order irrelevant. The identifier is created with the held acquisition snapshot, is immutable for that snapshot, and is refreshed only by resolving a later snapshot. Its complete consumers are the walk producer, `Assert-WalkResultSemantics`, and every `Read-WalkResultDocument` caller. It is carried only in walk-result provenance, not in durable companion state. The version tag covers both this framing recipe and every core-owned game-neutral rule that can change `found`, `walkedSaves`, `boundHit`, `span`, or `diff` for identical candidate metadata, digests, bounds, and policy arrays. Any such core semantic change requires a new tag. `adapterVersion` cannot substitute because it does not own walk interpretation and ambient policy may change without an adapter-version bump. Including `diffExcludePaths` remains deliberate even though a conforming edit bumps `adapterVersion`: version discipline is not evidence that two held snapshots contain identical policy, so the hash also rejects mis-versioned or in-flight policy drift.

Contract:

- Adapter resolution and section 8's acquisition startup profile are identical to the other acquisition entry points. `-CompanionRoot <path>` keeps the same resolution semantics across `Watch-Saves.ps1`, `Update-CompanionState.ps1`, `Get-Diff.ps1`, `Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1`, and `Get-TurnContext.ps1` (default: the parent of the script's directory, i.e. the companion root above `core\`; it roots the resolution of `adapters\` and, for the entry points that use them, `state\` and `.tmp\`: Get-Diff uses no `.tmp\` and the walk uses no `state\` (section 3); section 11's scratch-root fixtures lean on supplying it explicitly). `-MaxSaves` and `-MaxAgeHours` are `[int]` (`System.Int32`) parameters. PowerShell 7.5 argument transformation and binding finish before the script applies either clamp; non-finite values and values outside the Int32 range fail binding before startup or clamp diagnostics. A successfully bound fractional argument follows PowerShell's Int32 conversion, including midpoint-to-even behavior. `-MaxSaves` (default 8) bounds how many saves are examined and is clamped to a minimum of 2 with typed warning `walk-max-saves-clamped` (below 2 no pair exists); `-MaxAgeHours` (default 72) bounds how much OLDER than the NEWEST candidate an examined save may be (ORIGINAL LastWriteTimeUtc deltas, both UTC). Producer traversal and semantic replay use one shared exact predicate: subtract candidate UTC ticks from newest UTC ticks, divide the nonnegative delta by `TimeSpan.TicksPerHour`, and classify it as over-age when the quotient exceeds `MaxAgeHours` or equals it with a nonzero remainder. They never construct a `TimeSpan` from `MaxAgeHours` or shift a `DateTime` by that bound, so the complete accepted Int32 domain is overflow-safe and both consumers make the same boundary decision. The defaults are sized against the observed idle-autosave cadence of roughly one save per 30 minutes (live directory, 2026-08-08): 8 saves spans a long idle evening (smaller defaults were observed to exhaust on a single night's idle stack), and because the bounds are CONJUNCTIVE the saves bound is what caps a dense idle stack (8 saves at that cadence is ~3.5 hours); the 72-hour age bound earns its keep on SPARSE histories (manual saves only, or a gap where few autosaves accumulated), where 8 candidates can legitimately span days. Walk expected cost is modest: one candidate's copy/dump/digest measured ~3.6 seconds on this machine (probed 2026-08-08), so a default walk runs tens of seconds. MaxSaves x 2 x the 120-second per-command timeout bounds cumulative child wait calls, not total elapsed time. Stability sampling, file I/O, hashing, validation, comparison, emission, cleanup, startup, and process termination add surrounding work, so the walk script's own elapsed wall time has no hard ceiling. Section 7.4's outer host supervisor still applies its independent 3,600-second enrichment cap. The first hung adapter command normally fails after about its 120-second child wait plus surrounding work, and section 10 routes that fail-closed exit to a current-state brief. The age anchor is the newest save, NOT wall-clock now, deliberately: the walk serves the returning-player brief, and a wall-clock anchor would render every candidate over-age after a week away, returning dry in exactly the case the feature exists for. Bound checks are ordered with the saves bound evaluated first, so a stop point where both bounds trip reports `saves` (a deterministic tie rule). The bounds are parameters, not adapter config: they are session budget, not game knowledge.
- The script ensures `.tmp\` exists at startup with a direct single-directory create (NOT `Initialize-CompanionDirs`, which creates `state\` alongside), and runs the reaper over the `.tmp\` half of its scope only: `Invoke-WorkFileReaper` gains a `-TmpOnly` switch that skips the orphaned-state-temp sweep, the walk passes it, and the pipeline callers keep the full two-directory sweep (section 8). Startup reaping is what cleans a killed walk's debris in sessions that never run the pipeline. With those two carve-outs the invariant is exact: the script reads NOTHING and writes NOTHING under `state\` and never creates that directory; it is fully decoupled from the baseline/cache lifecycle (7.1) and cannot disturb reset-on-read semantics. Its inputs are `SaveGames\` (read-only, copies only) and adapter.json; its outputs are stdout JSON and its own work files.
- Candidate enumeration uses the adapter glob, sorted by LastWriteTimeUtc descending with ordinal file-name descending as tie-break: the same ordering as the watcher trigger (section 8), extracted into a shared helper (`Get-SaveCandidates`, whose first element `Get-NewestSave` returns) so the ordering rule keeps one home. `Get-SaveCandidates` returns an EMPTY ARRAY on zero glob matches (strict-mode safe to enumerate), and `Get-NewestSave` keeps its documented contract of returning `$null` in that case: the extraction must not regress the watcher's keep-polling and the one-shot's exit-1 zero-match outcomes (section 8). An ENUMERATION FAILURE is not zero matches: `Get-SaveCandidates` enumerates without a silencing error action and RE-RAISES read errors (ACL denial, I/O failure, a directory vanishing after startup validation), and each consumer maps the throw to its own handling (the watcher's poll exception boundary, the one-shot's exit 1, the walk's `[config]` class below); a swallowed enumeration error masquerading as an empty history would make the brief confidently wrong. Discovery itself has two core-owned inclusive ceilings: inspect at most 65,536 direct child entries and charge at most 16,777,216 cumulative strict UTF-8 bytes across their file names. `Get-SaveCandidates` uses a lazy direct-child enumeration and charges each entry before glob filtering, metadata materialization, or retention; the first excess throws typed `SaveCandidateLimitException`. These values mirror the archive metadata ceilings and bound a misconfigured or hostile directory while remaining far above a save rotation. Matching candidates within the ceilings are then fully retained and sorted, and `WalkTraversalEvidence` may freeze that bounded array. `MaxSaves` bounds copying and digest traversal, not discovery. The watcher logs and skips the poll; the one-shot and turn-context map the exception to `[source]` / `candidate-limit-exceeded`; the walk maps it to `[config]`. No caller treats it as zero matches.
- The NEWEST candidate passes the one-shot's write-stability gate (section 8: two size samples at least 2 seconds apart, up to 5 attempts) before copying; older candidates skip the gate, with the provisional runtime assumption that the engine writes each matching save once and never rewrites older matching files, while every cloud-sync artifact rewritten in place remains outside the configured glob. Like the other reused plumbing, the gate gets ONE home: the one-shot's inline sampling loop is extracted into an exported helper (`Test-FileWriteStable`: a file path in; `$true`/`$false` out after the two-sample/2-second/5-attempt protocol). An unequal sample pair advances to the next attempt. The helper returns true on the first equal pair and false only after all five attempts complete without one. Metadata failure throws typed `SaveSourceException` with `SourceDisposition: vanished` for file-not-found during either sample or `SourceDisposition: unreadable` for access, provider, and unrecognized I/O; the underlying exception is preserved. The helper is consumed by the one-shot, the walk, and the turn-context bridge, so the constants cannot drift between copies (the watcher's cross-poll sampling is a different mechanism and keeps its own implementation, section 8). An unstable newest candidate is exit 1 `[unstable]`. BOTH bound checks use enumeration metadata only (candidate counts and ORIGINAL mtimes), so every stop decision about the NEXT pair is made BEFORE any gate, copy, or digest work on it; a candidate is processed only once the walk has decided a pair involving it will actually be evaluated. Consequently: with FEWER THAN TWO candidates the walk exits found:false / boundHit `exhausted` with `walkedSaves` 0 and no processing at all; and when the IMMEDIATE PREDECESSOR is already over-age (two or more candidates, c1 more than MaxAgeHours older than c0), the walk likewise exits found:false / boundHit `age` / `walkedSaves` 0 before touching c0, so the stability gate, copies, and post-digest checks never fire on a run that is known dry from metadata (a mid-write or corrupt save is the pipeline's concern; the walk has no consumer for a digest that will never be compared). `walkedSaves` is therefore always 0 or at least 2, never 1. `-MaxAgeHours` below 1 is clamped to 1 with typed warning `walk-max-age-hours-clamped`, symmetric with the `-MaxSaves` clamp. Each clamp warning is appended to the walk-owned sink after binding and is rendered only through the final machine-readable warning line below.
- Work files: `Invoke-SaveDigest` owns allocation. The walk passes the role-specific stem `work-walk-<i>`. At the start of each invocation the helper generates 16 random bytes, renders them as one 32-character lowercase hexadecimal nonce, and creates `work-walk-<i>-<nonce>-save.zip` / `-dump.json` / `-digest.json` in the `.tmp\` root. Before creating any of those paths it also creates `work-walk-<i>-<nonce>.lease` with `FileMode.CreateNew`, holds that file handle with `FileShare.None` until failure cleanup or successful ownership transfer, and never refreshes its timestamp. The nonce and lease are created once, scoped to that invocation, never reused, and become invalid when the owning result is disposed. Only the helper, its adapter child commands, and the owning walk or cycle consume the group. Failure cleanup before a result exists, and result disposal after success, delete the known paths, release the lease handle, then best-effort delete the lease. The one-hour age test is only a crash and failed-delete backstop: before deleting an aged canonical group, the reaper must open its lease exclusively. Sharing violation means the invocation is live and the entire group is skipped without warning; successful acquisition proves only that the helper-held lease owner has released the group. The reaper closes that probe handle and may delete every aged direct-child member with the same canonical group prefix, deleting the lease last; it does not claim that a detached adapter descendant is quiescent. A descendant write after its command's composite completion violates the adapter command contract. Output from a failed command is never accepted or published, and late writes may race only best-effort cleanup. A file recreated after reaper enumeration is a new unleased orphan whose own `LastWriteTimeUtc` starts its one-hour aging interval. Any other lease open failure returns the typed cleanup warning and skips the group. A crash closes the handle while leaving the lease file, so the group becomes reapable after the lease itself passes the one-hour cutoff. Lease creation before every other member closes the window where live canonical work could exist without liveness evidence. The `work-` prefix keeps the files inside reaper scope, and the `walk` token keeps them visually distinct from cycle files.

  Every work destination is no-overwrite. The save destination opens with `FileMode.CreateNew`, write access, and `FileShare.None`; the dump and digest publishers use their sibling-temp CreateNew and no-overwrite move contracts. An existing destination produces the typed failure for that stage and is never truncated or replaced. Random naming reduces collision probability; CreateNew supplies the safety guarantee.

  The copy/dump/digest plumbing is SHARED with cycle steps 1-3, extracted into an exported helper (`Invoke-SaveDigest`: adapter dir plus config, a source save FileInfo, a role-specific work-file stem, and a caller-owned module-typed warning sink in; one owned disposable `SaveDigestResult` out with `digestPath`, parsed `digest`, `sourceIdentity` containing the held `domainId`, `saveFile`, and canonical UTC `savedAt`, and `sourceReceipt` containing the latter two fields plus positive `byteLength` and lowercase `contentSha256`, plus the live lease handle, complete known path set, and the same warning-sink reference). The sink accepts only validated envelope-shaped warning records and deduplicates them ordinally by `(code, detail)`; the helper and result never write a process stream. On success, ownership of that group transfers atomically into the result and the helper cleans nothing. The walk and cycle hold the result in `try/finally`; the walk disposes it after retaining the parsed digest needed for comparison, and the cycle disposes it only after commit, cache, and bookkeeping finish or their path terminates. Disposal is idempotent, performs the cleanup lifecycle above, and invalidates the returned path and lease ownership; the immutable parsed digest and source scalars remain ordinary values. A failure before successful return remains helper-owned and runs the same cleanup before throwing. Any failed deletion appends the null-detail `pipeline-cleanup-degraded` record to the supplied sink without replacing the primary exception. After transfer, idempotent result disposal appends the same record on a failed deletion without throwing. Callers dispose before freezing or rendering their final warnings, so cleanup degradation survives success, primary failure, and watcher cancellation without changing the primary outcome. The helper captures source metadata A from a newly constructed or explicitly refreshed `FileInfo`, copies its timestamp and length into scalars, and rejects a length above the held positive Int64 `maxSourceBytes` before allocating a work destination or hashing. It hashes the source with SHA-256, streams it into the CreateNew work file, hashes the work copy, hashes the source again, and captures source metadata B through a second independent refresh or newly constructed `FileInfo` before copying those properties into scalars. Repeated property reads from the enumerated candidate object without refresh are forbidden because its metadata may be cached. The initial source hash, streaming copy, work-copy hash, and final source hash independently stop at `maxSourceBytes + 1`; observing the extra byte throws the nonretryable copy-stage reason `source-size-limit-exceeded`, and the copy never writes that extra byte. Metadata B above the ceiling fails the same way. Acquisition validation forbids `maxSourceBytes` equal to Int64.MaxValue so cap-plus-one arithmetic is exact. It returns only when metadata A and B match ordinally, both source hashes and the work-copy hash are identical, and both observed lengths equal `byteLength`. The receipt therefore binds mapper input to the exact accepted work-copy bytes and detects a same-length mutation even if its timestamp is unchanged or restored during this protocol. It does not claim that the save can never change after the final source observation. Automatic discovery assumes a later write changes file name or LastWriteTimeUtc, as normal game saves do. A post-acceptance rewrite that preserves both ordering fields is outside the automatic freshness model: watcher and turn acquisition may retain the old receipt until an explicit one-shot refresh forces reprocessing. Hashing every equal-identity candidate on every poll is an explicit current-design anti-goal. Failure throws a typed `SaveDigestStageException` with `Stage` exactly `copy`, `dump`, or `digest`, `SourceDisposition` exactly `stable`, `vanished`, `changed`, or `unreadable`, and registry-valid `Reason` plus typed `Atoms` under one canonical detail owner: `copy` owns a `source` detail, while `dump` and `digest` own their same-named details. Copy-stage typed file absence reports `vanished`; a metadata, length, or hash mismatch reports `changed`; provider, access, metadata, hash, and other copy I/O failures report `unreadable`. A source length above `maxSourceBytes`, whether metadata-decided or observed by a bounded stream, reports `unreadable` with reason `source-size-limit-exceeded` and is nonretryable. Dump and digest failures occur after a stable copy and therefore report `stable`.
