When an underlying exception exists it is preserved as `InnerException`; observational values and developer messages remain internal and never enter a rendered detail. The walk uses the typed reason and atoms only to validate and classify the failure, then maps `Stage` to its exact bare `[copy]`/`[dump]`/`[digest]` primary frame without rendering reason or atoms. The cycle maps the typed properties without message parsing: `copy` becomes `failurePhase: source` with the exception's disposition, while `dump` and `digest` become their same-named phases with disposition `stable`. It derives `retryable: true` only from `vanished` or `changed`. Lease creation or work-destination allocation failure is `Stage: copy`, `SourceDisposition: unreadable`, and is not retryable. Neither caller parses or renders provider or exception message text. This closed failure channel means the plumbing keeps one home, mirroring the `Get-SaveCandidates` extraction. The helper records the ORIGINAL file name, LastWriteTimeUtc, length, and verified hash. Each of the initial source hash, streaming copy, and final source hash opens the literal source path separately with `FileMode.Open`, `FileAccess.Read`, and `FileShare.ReadWrite | FileShare.Delete`, then disposes that handle after its pass. No source handle survives into adapter execution. Share-friendly reads let the game write, replace, or rotate a source while the receipt protocol detects the resulting disappearance, unreadability, or change instead of blocking the game operation. The helper streams bytes into the newly created work-save file; it never uses a metadata-preserving copy API or applies the source timestamp to the work file. The destination is fresh from its first visible instant, remains protected by the no-share writer while active, and receives a current UTC LastWriteTime after close. It passes the ORIGINAL name and the ORIGINAL timestamp in round-trip 'o' format plus domain and byte receipt as `{sourceDomainId}`/`{saveName}`/`{savedAt}`/ `{sourceLength}`/`{sourceSha256}` (walk digests match pipeline digests in provenance), and verifies each command's promised output exists after the call (the placeholder pass-through hazard: an unsubstituted `{token}` can exit 0 while writing a stray file). After the digest command, the helper reads the digest through `Read-AdapterDigestDocument` with the held configuration and performs the remaining shared post-digest provenance check: schemaVersion equals the core-supported version, adapterVersion equals the held config integer, game equals the held config string ordinally, `source.saveFile`, canonical `source.savedAt`, and `source.contentSha256` equal their receipt fields ordinally, `source.byteLength` equals the receipt numerically, and `source.domainId` equals the held domain ordinally. Any parse, structure, version, adapter, game, or adapter-semantic rejection or provenance mismatch throws `SaveDigestStageException` with `Stage: digest` and `SourceDisposition: stable`; no caller receives an unverified result. The script cleans its own known work files and lease before throwing on failure. On success it transfers that live ownership to `SaveDigestResult`; caller disposal performs the same best-effort cleanup, tolerating already-gone files such as a committed digest path. Mapper-local cleanup owns its private sibling nonce; the reaper remains the hard-crash and failed-cleanup backstop for every group member.
- Walk order: with candidates c0 (newest) to cN, digest lazily and evaluate diff(c1 -> c0), then diff(c2 -> c1), and so on: each pair's BASELINE is the older save, so the reported diff reads forward in WRITE time. Write order is not world order when the player reloaded an older save inside the window: such a pair is eventful BY CONSTRUCTION (removals, negative deltas; 7.2's loaded-timeline-wins semantics), the walk correctly stops there, and the diff is self-describing, so section 10's brief presents a removal-and-negative-delta-dominated span as a reload rewind, never as forward progress. The first eventful pair ends the walk: the brief needs where real play most recently happened, and the alternative of aggregating consecutive eventful pairs into one union span was considered and rejected because the union re-reports history that earlier consuming reads already delivered and grows the brief without bound; a session wanting deeper history raises BOTH `-MaxSaves` and `-MaxAgeHours` (either bound alone can be the binding one; where the last real play sits far behind a fresh idle stack, raising only the saves bound merely trips the age bound) and reads the span's diff in context.
- Walk digests are produced by the CURRENT mapper on every run (no reuse of state files or previous digests). Every result has already passed `Invoke-SaveDigest`'s shared structure, version, adapter, game, and source-provenance check; the walk uses the returned parsed digest and source identity directly. Core supplies the mapper arguments but still verifies every emitted field because an external or miswired mapper may ignore them. Without this, a mapper ahead of core (or a digestCommand wired to another adapter's mapper) would produce self-consistent stale-shape digests that misclassify silently instead of failing loudly.

Output (stdout, exit 0), one JSON object:

    {
      "provenance": { "game": "...", "adapterVersion": 2,
                      "sourceDomainId": "<lowercase SHA-256 hex>",
                      "walkPolicyId": "<lowercase SHA-256 hex>" },
      "found": true | false,
      "walkedSaves": <integer: count of candidates copied and digested>,
      "boundHit": null | "saves" | "age" | "exhausted",
      "span": { "from": { "domainId": "...", "saveFile": "...", "savedAt": "..." },
                "to":   { "domainId": "...", "saveFile": "...", "savedAt": "..." } } | null,
      "diff": <the eventful pair's 7.2 diff object> | null
    }

`provenance` is always present, including metadata-decided dry results. It contains exactly non-empty string `game`, signed Int64 JSON integer `adapterVersion`, and lowercase 64-hex strings `sourceDomainId` and `walkPolicyId`, stamped from the producer's one held validated adapter snapshot. `walkedSaves` counts candidates actually copied and digested on this run: 0 for every metadata-decided dry exit (empty directory, lone candidate, immediate predecessor over-age; nothing is processed on those paths), and at least 2 whenever any pair was evaluated; the value 1 is impossible by the pre-processing bound rule in the contract above. `found: true`: `span` and `diff` are populated and `boundHit` is null; the `savedAt` values are ORIGINAL save timestamps ('o' format) and they, not the diff's generatedAt stamps, date the span. `found: false`: `span` and `diff` are null and `boundHit` names why the walk ended dry: `saves` (the walk stopped at MaxSaves with at least one further candidate REMAINING on disk, so a wider re-run has material to examine), `age` (the next candidate is more than MaxAgeHours older than the NEWEST candidate; the newest itself is never over-age, its delta being zero), `exhausted` (the walk consumed the ENTIRE candidate set, including the one-save, empty-directory, and exactly-MaxSaves-on-disk cases; no wider re-run can change the result, and the enumeration's full match set makes the remaining-vs-none distinction metadata-decidable). Where the saves and age bounds trip at the same stop point the saves bound wins (the ordered check in the contract above). A dry walk is a RESULT, not an error: exit 0.

`Read-WalkResultDocument -Json <string> -EffectiveMaxSaves <Int32> -AdapterConfig <validated held object>` is the shared exact validator for this protocol. It uses `Read-CompanionJson`, requires exactly the six root fields shown above with their stated JSON kinds under budget `walk-result`, and returns the validated `CompanionJsonValue` or throws `CompanionDocumentValidationException`. `walkedSaves` is exactly 0 or an integer from 2 through the already clamped `EffectiveMaxSaves`. The root provenance must equal `AdapterConfig.game`, `adapterVersion`, `sourceDomainId`, and `walkPolicyId` ordinally or numerically as appropriate. The validator derives `ambientExtraPaths` from that same held object, so domain and eventfulness policy can never come from different configuration snapshots. `span`, when populated, contains exactly `from` and `to`; each is an exact source identity object with lowercase 64-hex `domainId`, non-empty file-name-only `saveFile` containing neither directory separator, and canonical UTC `o` `savedAt`; both identities equal root `provenance.sourceDomainId` ordinally, `from` precedes `to` under the shared source order, and `span.to` equals the saveFile and savedAt members of `diff.currentSource` ordinally, including its domainId. A populated `diff` must pass `Read-DiffDocument`, its `game`, `adapterVersion`, and domain must equal root provenance, it must not be a baseline reset, and it must be eventful under this section's complete eventful predicate using the held configuration's `ambientExtraPaths`.

Cross-field states are closed. `found: true` requires null `boundHit`, populated span and diff, and walkedSaves from 2 through the effective bound. `found: false` requires null span and diff plus `boundHit` exactly `saves`, `age`, or `exhausted`; `saves` requires walkedSaves equal to `EffectiveMaxSaves`; `age` permits 0 or 2 through `EffectiveMaxSaves - 1`; and `exhausted` permits 0 or 2 through the effective bound. Every other state is invalid.

Producer-only semantics use two exact `Assert-WalkResultSemantics` parameter sets. Found mode is `-Result <typed found candidate> -TraversalEvidence <WalkTraversalEvidence> -AdapterConfig <validated held object>`. Dry mode is the same parameter set with a typed dry candidate. Baseline, Target, separately supplied bounds, and separately supplied ambient paths are forbidden in both modes.

`WalkTraversalEvidence` is module-owned and immutable. It contains exactly the complete frozen ordered candidate-metadata array, the effective MaxSaves and MaxAgeHours Int32 values, and an ordered evaluated-pair receipt array. Only the module-private `New-WalkPairReceipt` factory can mint a receipt, and only after `Assert-DiffSemantics` and eventfulness validation succeed under the held AdapterConfig. A receipt contains exactly nonnegative Int32 `baselineIndex`, nonnegative Int32 `targetIndex`, the two exact source identities, and Boolean `eventful`; no full digest or diff reference survives in it. Both modes require root provenance to equal the held adapter snapshot before any other semantic decision and prove that every receipt corresponds to adjacent frozen candidates in processing order.

Traversal retains at most the rolling two full digests and one full diff. After an uneventful pair is certified, it releases that diff and the digest not needed as the target of the next older pair. Found mode retains the selected pair's full Baseline, Target, and Diff only until candidate construction and producer validation finish; dry mode retains no full pair after traversal. This compact evidence representation was chosen over replaying every full document because the 65,536-candidate discovery ceiling combined with document byte ceilings could otherwise retain about 1.5 TiB before object overhead.

The validator replays the saves-first bound algorithm over the complete candidate array. For found mode, every receipt before the last must be uneventful, the last must be eventful, replay must stop at that first eventful pair, and the separately retained selected Baseline, Target, and Diff must equal the last receipt's identities and derive the exact span and diff fields. Replay derives exact `walkedSaves`. For dry mode, every receipt must be uneventful and replay derives exact `walkedSaves` plus `saves`, `age`, or `exhausted` termination. A receipt cannot substitute for current semantic validation of the selected full pair; found mode reruns `Assert-DiffSemantics` on that retained pair before result construction. Empty-directory, lone-save, and immediate-age results carry an empty pair array and need no fabricated digests.

A producer-side rejection is `[protocol]` before serialization. The producer then serializes the complete candidate exactly once in memory; serialization failure is `[emit]` with no stdout. It passes that exact string to `Read-WalkResultDocument` with the held validated `AdapterConfig`, maps rejection to `[protocol]`, and writes the already validated string without reserialization. Every machine consumer passes its one held validated adapter snapshot after buffering exit-zero stdout. A consumer-side rejection of otherwise exit-zero bytes is a local `[protocol]` enrichment failure, not a dry result.

After successful parameter binding, every script-owned exit 1 is fail-closed with the exact bare primary stderr line `find-span: [<class>]`, with no suffix, matching the other entry points' stderr discipline: `[config]` (resolution, validation, a candidate-enumeration read failure, or `SaveCandidateLimitException` from `Get-SaveCandidates`), `[unstable]` (newest candidate never stabilized), `[copy]` (work-file allocation, streaming copy, or other work-area I/O failure, including a vanished candidate between enumeration and copy, a gate-stage I/O error re-raised by `Test-FileWriteStable`, and a failed `.tmp\` ensure), `[dump]` and `[digest]` (adapter command failure, including a promised output file absent after apparent success and a walk digest failing the post-digest structure/schemaVersion/adapterVersion/game/source-provenance check), `[compare]` (module import or differ failure), `[protocol]` (the constructed result failed its semantic or document validator), `[emit]` (result serialization or the final stdout write failed). Every failure before final emission produces no stdout. An emission failure exits 1 but may have left an unusable prefix; callers buffer through EOF and ignore all bytes unless the process exits 0 and the bytes parse as exactly one valid result document. The finally-scoped work-file cleanup runs on failure paths too. The class-only primary frame is deliberate because a failed walk is non-gating enrichment and always selects the current-state-only brief. Adding reasons and atoms would create a second bounded primary-detail protocol; the accepted consequence is that reason-specific hints, including `adapter-release-corrupt`, are unavailable from walk-back alone. The walk owns one warning sink from startup through final disposal. Every pair's differ appends its warnings to that sink instead of writing a process stream. After every owned result has been disposed, the walk renders each unique `(code, detail)` record once on success or failure without changing the primary `find-span:` class, exit code, or stdout document. A warning repeated by 65,535 pairs therefore remains one bounded rendered record rather than an output multiplier. The unified stderr sideband is deliberate: warnings can precede a later failure that has no result JSON, and one transport covers both exit paths.

The rendering is one optional machine-readable final stderr line: `find-span-warning: <compact-json-array>`. The array is nonempty, sorted, and ordinally unique by `(code, detail)` and accepts exactly `pipeline-cleanup-degraded`, `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded`, `diff-exclude-path-unused`, `walk-max-saves-clamped`, and `walk-max-age-hours-clamped`. The first three use the shared warning code/detail rules in 7.5 except that the walk accepts only `tmp`, never `state`, for reaper discovery exhaustion; both clamp warnings require null detail. `Read-WalkWarningArray -Json <string>` validates it through `Read-CompanionJson` budget `diff`. On success stderr is empty or exactly that line. On failure the exact bare `find-span: [class]` line comes first and the optional warning line comes last; no other line is legal.

The execution runtime owns one outer walk supervisor; the future TUI never parses raw streams or owns the process. It starts stdout and stderr pumps concurrently, validates strict UTF-8 incrementally, and retains at most 16,777,216 bytes from each stream. A 3,600-second composite deadline covers process exit plus clean EOF from both pumps. An overflow latches at cap plus one and stops retention. Overflow, deadline, process-start, capture or pump, process-wait, abnormal-exit, or teardown failure triggers process-tree termination, stream closure and pump cancellation, with at most a fixed five-second teardown and drain grace. The host then discards both captures, never retries or branches on partial output, and uses the local `[protocol]` current-state-only fallback. Exit code 0 or 1 alone is ordinary; every other exit code is abnormal. After a clean composite, the host validates complete stderr framing and the warning array before using the primary class or any warning. Malformed JSON, duplicate or unsorted warnings, an unknown code, or an invalid detail is the same local `[protocol]` failure. Exact-cap stdout may continue to result validation; cap plus one cannot. The outer deadline is a liveness cap, not a promise that every accepted MaxSaves value completes: it gives the default eight-save walk substantial headroom beyond its 1,920-second adapter-command subtotal, while intentionally ending oversized enrichment before it can gate the brief.

PowerShell parameter-binding failures occur before the script body and therefore have the host's unframed invocation diagnostic rather than `find-span:` framing. Machine hosts omit the bounds or validate them as Int32 values before invocation. Any exit 1 without a valid `find-span: [class]` frame and optional validated warning line is a local `[protocol]` enrichment failure, never a dry walk result.

Downstream: the exploring TUI companion-app feature (FEATURES.md, features/ tui-companion-app.md) plans an automated idle-aware recap that would invoke this script and consume this JSON contract; contract changes should keep that consumer in mind.

### 7.5 Game-turn acquisition: validated host entry and consuming transaction

`Get-ValidatedTurnContext.ps1 [-Adapter <name>] [-CompanionRoot <path>]` is the host-neutral entry point for both Codex and Claude Code. The host invokes it exactly once per game-related turn. It starts sibling `Get-TurnContext.ps1` exactly once as a child process, drains both streams concurrently, accepts only the exit-0 or exit-1 framing defined below, and validates the exact child output. On exit 0 it first applies `Read-TurnContextEnvelope -TransportOnly`, then resolves one fresh Acquisition config and applies producer-mode envelope validation under that config, which covers ordinal game and source-domain identity, numeric adapter-version identity, and nested adapter digest admission. Only then does it write the original complete child envelope unchanged to its own stdout. On a valid child exit-1 diagnostic it validates with `Read-TurnContextDiagnostic` and forwards the exact diagnostic unchanged. Legal child exit-1 classes are exactly `config`, `busy`, `lock`, `stale-target`, `no-saves`, `stability`, `source`, `dump`, `digest`, `refresh`, `freshness`, `compute`, `reset-write:not-consumed`, `protocol`, and `delivery`; class, consumption, lock-stage, reason, and warning tokens use ordinal case-sensitive matching. Invalid exit-1 framing becomes `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: unknown` and `attempt-result-invalid`; invalid exit-0 framing or admission becomes `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: consumed` and `envelope-invalid`. A failed final stdout write becomes `[delivery]` with `consumptionState: consumed` and `stdout-write-failed`; partial stdout may precede that diagnostic but is never a usable envelope. Every outer failure injects no envelope and never starts another child. The entry point is plain PowerShell 7.5 with JSON stdout, typed diagnostic stderr, and process exit codes, so neither host supplies an adapter script or host-specific parsing path.

This temporary bridge combines freshness acquisition with one consuming read for the current CLI companion. `Get-TurnContext.ps1 [-Adapter <name>] [-CompanionRoot <path>]` is the one executable transaction wrapper the validated host entry invokes. It runs acquisition attempts in-process through the internal `Invoke-TurnContextAttempt` function, captures every candidate envelope in private memory, performs the one permitted retry itself, and writes only the selected final envelope to process stdout, with no leading or trailing whitespace. Neither outer host nor validated entry invokes or captures a single attempt directly. Before attempt A, the wrapper resolves and validates exactly one adapter configuration snapshot. It owns that snapshot through selection, passes the same object to A and any B retry, and supplies it to every candidate-envelope validation. A mid-transaction adapter.json edit never replaces that held snapshot. If a refresh reaches `cycle.lock`, the post-lock receipt confirmation below detects the edit and stops without publication; otherwise the edit takes effect on the next wrapper invocation. The snapshot is transaction-local and is discarded on return without serialization or logging. Resolution failure produces the ordinary terminal `config` result with `consumptionState: none`; no internal acquisition attempt starts. After a successful resolution the wrapper performs 7.6's advisory consumer-ownership preflight, and a failed preflight ends the same way with a 7.6 consumer reason. The wrapper also owns one transaction-wide operational-warning sink shared by A and any B retry. Its closed domain is `pipeline-cleanup-degraded` with null detail and `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded` with detail `tmp` or `state`, so it contains at most three sorted, ordinally deduplicated records. Attempt warning routing sends these operational records to that sink and keeps every other warning attempt-local. A selected envelope merges the transaction sink with its selected-attempt warnings; a terminal diagnostic carries the transaction sink instead. Cleanup or reaper degradation from a discarded notConsumed A therefore survives into selected B or the final failure without leaking configuration-sized pointer warnings across attempts. `pipeline-bookkeeping-degraded` remains attempt-local because bookkeeping is non-authoritative derived trigger state that B neither reads nor depends on; the watcher self-heals it at the accepted cost of at most one redundant cycle. Carrying it would attribute A's discarded refresh history to B's selected-attempt summary. Retry B repairs storage failure inside the same semantic transaction rather than starting a new configuration transaction. Acquisition, diff rules, and envelope validation therefore share the original snapshot. Re-resolving for B was rejected because a concurrent edit would make the result depend on timing and could mix adapter semantics inside one transaction. For acquisition semantics, configuration changes intentionally begin with the next wrapper invocation. The bridge does not create a durable event, acknowledgement, or second snapshot. The execution runtime replaces it with the durable lifecycle in the runtime persistence pattern, whose core half is 7.6. The two never consume the same root concurrently: 7.6's ownership marker selects one, and the bridge stays the default owner until the runtime has been verified in live play and the user retires the bridge.

Each internal acquisition attempt performs these stages in order:

1. Accept the wrapper-owned validated adapter configuration snapshot, ensure `state\` and `.tmp\`, and run the work-file reaper. Merge any returned typed reaper warning through the attempt warning router; the reaper and every other attempt callee remain stream-silent. The attempt never re-reads adapter.json; the held object is used through save selection, any extraction, target validation, and consumption.
2. Enumerate matching saves and select the newest by the shared source-identity order. Capture this identity as the freshness floor. Each reacquisition pass re-enumerates and may raise the floor to a newer identity, but never lowers it during the attempt.
3. Read and adapter-admit `current.json`. If it is valid and its source identity meets or exceeds the floor, no extraction is needed. A missing, unreadable, structurally invalid, version-stale, or identity-invalid document appends `pipeline-current-self-healed` to the attempt-local warning sink before refresh, so a concurrent writer that repairs the file later cannot erase the initial caution. A valid document below the floor requires refresh without that warning. The refresh acquires `cycle.lock`, re-enumerates and stability-gates after acquisition, then processes the selected save through the existing pipeline cycle.
4. Read one exact preflight digest snapshot into memory through `Read-AdapterDigestDocument`, validate its source identity, and require its identity to meet or exceed the current floor.
5. Re-enumerate matching saves by the shared order after the preflight target snapshot is frozen. A successful enumeration has an observation interval from the directory call's start through its return. Raise the floor to the maximum of its prior value and the newest identity now observed; an empty second enumeration leaves the existing floor unchanged. If the frozen target falls below the raised floor, reacquire within the three-pass budget before any consumption. Enumeration failure maps to non-retryable `[source]` because freshness could not be established. The call does not claim an atomic directory snapshot. A save created concurrently during the interval may be returned or omitted; if returned, the attempt's abstract linearization point is after it became visible and the floor includes it. If omitted, or if created after the call returns, the save is outside this attempt's minimum freshness obligation. This observation is not an upper cutoff: a serialized pipeline cycle may publish that newer save before stage 6 freezes `current.json`, and a same-domain target above the floor is valid for this turn. The observation authorizes proceeding but does not select the consumption target: stage 6 ignores the preflight object and freezes the authoritative current snapshot under `consume.lock`.
6. Pass the floor, not the preflight snapshot, to the shared consuming operation from 7.3. After `cycle.lock` has been released, that operation acquires `consume.lock` and re-reads `current.json` into the authoritative target snapshot. It repeats complete core and adapter admission, then validates receipt and floor while holding the lock. Missing, invalid, version-stale, or below-floor current state releases the lock and returns a typed `[freshness]` outcome for reacquisition before any baseline read or mutation. A qualifying snapshot replaces the preflight snapshot even when the two share source identity but have different receipts; diagnostic generation provenance follows this final target. The operation builds a provisional candidate turn-context envelope in attempt-local memory before either baseline or cache mutation; `cacheDegraded` and the final warning array remain provisional until the typed `ConsumeResult` is known. It writes no process stream. The operation then advances baseline to that exact final target and samples `current.json` only to refresh the non-authoritative cache. A different digest observed after the locked target read remains pending relative to the new baseline and is acquired on a later turn.

The freshness floor is acquisition-attempt-local. It is created by the first successful enumeration, refreshed only by taking the maximum identity on reacquisition or the post-target enumeration, consumed by the current-state gate, target linearization check, and emitted `sourceFloor`, and discarded on every terminal attempt success or failure. It is never persisted or reused by the wrapper's one permitted new attempt after a not-consumed reset failure. That second attempt establishes a later linearization point from the saves and current state then available. It may consume an older identity only if the previously observed newer save has vanished, its current digest is also missing, invalid, or version-stale, and no valid baseline is newer than the replacement target. A still-valid newer current digest continues to satisfy the new floor, while a still-valid newer baseline produces `[stale-target]` and requires the fresh-save recovery in 7.3. Carrying the maximum floor across both attempts was rejected because it would add whole-transaction monotonicity beyond the per-attempt freshness contract without resolving the independent baseline guard.

The acquisition attempt budget is the module-owned literal value `3`: one initial pass and at most two reacquisition passes. A retryable stability, typed gate disappearance, source-copy, below-floor target, or stale-target failure starts the next pass, re-enumerates saves, and reapplies the full gate. Permanent adapter-command, mapper-validation, configuration, diff, and consuming-write failures stop immediately. A pipeline failure after `current.json` was committed preserves that fact in its typed result; the bridge revalidates the committed target and may continue when it meets the floor. A failed pipeline cache or `last-processed.json` write after a qualifying current commit adds the corresponding structured envelope warning for this bridge, rather than discarding fresh state: consumption rewrites the cache. A bookkeeping failure can cause one redundant watcher cycle when the retained tuple is absent or differs from the newest source identity. An equal-identity forced refresh may require no watcher cycle because bookkeeping deliberately carries no byte receipt.

The three-pass acquisition budget and two-attempt consuming transaction budget are deliberately asymmetric. Source churn can invalidate a pass before baseline mutation, so two immediate reacquisitions absorb a short save-write burst without risking duplicate consumption. A second baseline-write `notConsumed` result instead indicates repeated durable-state failure after two complete payload computations; the same delta remains pending for the next explicit transaction, so an unbounded or third immediate consuming attempt would add storage pressure without improving the never-loss rule.

The stdout envelope has exactly these fields:

```jsonc
{
  "turnContextVersion": 2,
  "adapterVersion": 2,
  "game": "disco_elysium",
  "sourceFloor": { "domainId": "...", "saveFile": "...", "savedAt": "..." },
  "refresh": { "attempted": true, "commitObserved": true, "attempts": 1 },
  "warnings": [ { "code": "pipeline-cache-degraded", "detail": null } ],
  "baselineReset": false,
  "cacheDegraded": false,
  "diff": { /* complete 7.2 diff */ },
  "state": { /* exact complete 6.1 target digest */ }
}
```

`turnContextVersion` versions this host envelope independently of the digest schema. Top-level `adapterVersion` and `game` must equal both nested objects' corresponding fields. `diff.currentGeneratedAt` must equal `state.generatedAt`; its four string-valued `currentSource` fields must equal `state.source` ordinally and `byteLength` must equal it numerically. `baselineReset` must equal `(diff.baselineGeneratedAt is null)`. `sourceFloor` is the final freshness floor of the selected acquisition attempt and `state.source` must meet or exceed it. The emitted `refresh` summary and non-operational warnings are likewise selected-attempt local; a discarded A contributes none of those fields to selected B. The wrapper's separately bounded transaction operational warnings are the sole exception and may survive A cleanup or reaping into B. `refresh.attempted` means at least one pipeline cycle was invoked; `refresh.commitObserved` means at least one invoked cycle committed `current.json`, including a commit later reported through a post-commit failure; and `refresh.attempts` counts cycle invocations, so all three values are respectively false, false, and zero when initial current state was fresh. A guard-discarded cycle increments attempts without setting commitObserved. `cacheDegraded` is false for an ordinary successful consume and true only when the selected attempt advanced baseline and then failed in the non-authoritative cache phase.

`warnings` is the complete success-warning channel; successful wrapper stderr remains empty. Each entry has exactly non-empty string `code` and string-or-null `detail`. Entries are ordinally unique by `(code, detail)` and sorted by that composite. Legal codes are `pipeline-cache-degraded`, `pipeline-bookkeeping-degraded`, `pipeline-baseline-unreadable`, `pipeline-cleanup-degraded`, `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded`, `pipeline-guard-discarded`, `pipeline-current-self-healed`, `consume-cache-fallback`, and `diff-exclude-path-unused`. `diff-exclude-path-unused` requires non-null detail equal to the configured JSON Pointer; `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded` requires detail exactly `tmp` or `state`; every other code requires null. The attempt warning router accumulates non-operational records locally and cleanup or reaper records in the wrapper-owned transaction sink. The attempt stamps the final sorted union before envelope serialization. Watcher, one-shot, and Get-Diff entry scripts may render their typed warnings to stderr; the turn-context wrapper carries them only here. The host discloses actionable degraded-state warnings without parsing process stderr. `pipeline-current-self-healed` covers either an initial turn-context current-state gate that treated current as absent or a cycle commit guard that replaced an invalid or version-stale current document. When both occur, composite uniqueness deduplicates them. `pipeline-cleanup-degraded` covers both a cycle's finally-scoped owned-work cleanup and startup orphan reaping, including enumeration, metadata, lease-probe, and deletion failures; repeated failures still deduplicate to one null-detail warning. Discovery ceiling exhaustion uses the separate root-bearing warning because it persists until an operator reduces that companion-owned root below both ceilings. Cycle outcomes additionally permit null-detail `pipeline-baseline-absent` and `pipeline-baseline-content-invalid` notes. They are not legal envelope warnings: watcher and one-shot render them, while a turn-context attempt consumes and omits them when `baselineReset` carries the same ordinary reset fact. The filter runs before the remaining cycle warnings merge into the envelope.

There is deliberately no `newerStatePending` field. Without excluding pipeline writers for the whole command, false could mean only that one sample saw no different digest; it could never prove absence at envelope delivery. The authoritative baseline/current pair and the next acquisition carry later changes without making that unreliable claim.

Failure classes and retry behavior are complete:

`Invoke-TurnContextAttempt` returns one structured `TurnContextAttemptResult` directly to the wrapper. Its exact fields are `outcomeClass` (a canonical unbracketed token), `consumptionState` (`none`, `notConsumed`, or `consumed`), `envelopeJson` (string only for `success` and the two reset-write outcomes, otherwise null), Boolean `cacheDegraded`, and `diagnostic`. `diagnostic` contains exactly string `outcomeClass`, string `consumptionState`, string-or-null `lockStage`, object-or-null `sourceIdentity`, string-or-null `generationTimestamp`, string-or-null `detail`, and array `warnings`. The diagnostic warning array is the wrapper-owned operational subset only: `pipeline-cleanup-degraded` with null detail and at most one `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded` record for each `tmp` and `state` detail. It is sorted and ordinally unique by `(code, detail)` and contains no pointers, paths, or adapter-controlled text. `lockStage` is exactly `cycle` or `consume` for `busy` and `lock`, and null for every other class. The producer stamps it from the typed operation it invoked; no validator infers it from provenance. A non-null `sourceIdentity` has exactly lowercase 64-hex `domainId`, non-empty file-name-only `saveFile`, and canonical UTC `o` `savedAt`. A non-null `generationTimestamp` is the canonical UTC `o` `generatedAt` of the validated target snapshot. `detail` is null only for success and otherwise contains one canonical registry value produced by `New-TurnContextDetail` below, capped at 512 Unicode scalar values at creation. The compact UTF-8 JSON record is capped at 4096 bytes; fixed fields are never removed. Its class and consumption state equal the containing result. Invariants are closed: `success` and `reset-write:consumed` mean consumed; `reset-write:not-consumed` means notConsumed; all other attempt classes mean none unless `protocol` follows serialization or validation of a result that reported consumed or notConsumed, in which case that state is preserved. `cacheDegraded` is true only for `reset-write:consumed`. Attempt tokens are exactly `success`, `config`, `busy`, `lock`, `stale-target`, `no-saves`, `stability`, `source`, `dump`, `digest`, `refresh`, `freshness`, `compute`, `reset-write:not-consumed`, `reset-write:consumed`, and `protocol`. `delivery` is wrapper-only and is never a `TurnContextAttemptResult.outcomeClass`. The attempt function maps typed operation results into this record and never communicates class, consumption state, or cause through free-form stderr text. The diagnostic takes registry-valid `detail` from the typed cycle outcome, `ConsumeResult`, or direct config, source, freshness, protocol, and delivery mapper. Child output and exceptions select a reason and typed atoms only; their text is never copied. The wrapper validates this complete result before trusting any field. If that validation fails, the rejected result cannot prove whether baseline mutation occurred, because both copies of `consumptionState` came from the same invalid producer. The wrapper emits its own `[protocol]` terminal diagnostic with reason `attempt-result-invalid`, `consumptionState: unknown`, and null lock stage and provenance, then performs no retry. For an envelope-bearing `ConsumeResult`, the attempt stamps the payload value's `cacheDegraded` from the result, merges and sorts final warnings from the attempt-local and transaction operational sinks, and serializes it once into `envelopeJson`. Serialization failure maps to `[protocol]` while preserving `consumptionState`; the wrapper therefore never retries merely because a post-consumption payload could not be encoded.

`New-TurnContextDetail -Class <token> -Reason <token> [-Atoms <typed object>]` is the only formatter for cycle `detail`, `ConsumeResult.detail`, attempt diagnostics, and the wrapper terminal record. Its canonical output is `<reason>` or `<reason> [<atom>=<value>,<atom>=<value>]`; atoms appear only in registry order. Allowed atom names and types are exactly nonnegative Int32 `attempts`, positive Int32 `timeoutSeconds`, Int32 `exitCode`, signed Int64 `expectedVersion`, and signed Int64 `actualVersion`. The complete class-to-reason registry is:

| Class | Allowed reasons |
|---|---|
| `config` | `adapter-resolution-failed`, `adapter-config-invalid`, `adapter-release-corrupt`, `adapter-config-confirmation-failed`, `adapter-config-changed`, `companion-root-invalid`, and the 7.6 consumer reasons `consumer-not-owner`, `consumer-owner-invalid`, `consumer-owner-unreadable`, `consumer-outbox-pending` |
| `busy` | `lock-timeout` |
| `lock` | `lock-open-failed` |
| `stale-target` | `target-precedes-baseline` |
| `no-saves` | `no-matching-saves` |
| `stability` | `stability-exhausted` |
| `source` | `state-setup-failed`, `work-setup-failed`, `save-enumeration-failed`, `candidate-limit-exceeded`, `candidate-metadata-failed`, `source-size-limit-exceeded`, `source-vanished`, `source-changed`, `source-unreadable` |
| `dump` | `command-start-failed`, `adapter-release-corrupt`, `command-timeout`, `command-transport-failed`, `command-exit-nonzero`, `promised-output-missing` |
| `digest` | `command-start-failed`, `adapter-release-corrupt`, `command-timeout`, `command-transport-failed`, `command-exit-nonzero`, `promised-output-missing`, `document-invalid`, `adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed`, `schema-version-mismatch`, `adapter-version-mismatch`, `game-mismatch`, `source-domain-mismatch`, `source-receipt-mismatch` |
| `refresh` | `current-commit-failed`, `cycle-outcome-invalid` |
| internal `cycle-cache` | `adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed`, `diff-compatibility-failed`, `diff-compute-failed`, `diff-validation-failed`, `cache-serialize-failed`, `cache-temp-write-failed`, `cache-replace-failed` |
| internal `cycle-bookkeeping` | `bookkeeping-serialize-failed`, `bookkeeping-temp-write-failed`, `bookkeeping-replace-failed` |
| `freshness` | `target-missing`, `target-unreadable`, `target-invalid`, `target-schema-stale`, `target-adapter-stale`, `target-game-mismatch`, `target-domain-mismatch`, `target-identity-invalid`, `target-below-floor` |
| `compute` | `adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed`, `baseline-read-failed`, `diff-compatibility-failed`, `diff-compute-failed`, `diff-validation-failed`, `payload-build-failed` |
| `reset-write:not-consumed` | `baseline-serialize-failed`, `baseline-temp-write-failed`, `baseline-replace-failed` |
| `reset-write:consumed` | `cache-target-invalid`, `cache-compare-failed`, `cache-serialize-failed`, `cache-temp-write-failed`, `cache-replace-failed` |
| `protocol` | `attempt-result-invalid`, `adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed`, `envelope-serialize-failed`, `envelope-invalid` |
| raw `emit` | `payload-serialize-failed`, `stdout-write-failed` |
| `delivery` | `stdout-write-failed` |

Only `lock-timeout` accepts `timeoutSeconds`; `stability-exhausted` accepts `attempts`; `command-timeout` accepts `timeoutSeconds`; `command-exit-nonzero` accepts `exitCode`; and schema or adapter version mismatch reasons accept `expectedVersion`, then `actualVersion`. These atoms are optional when the typed value is unavailable. No other reason accepts atoms. Paths, save names, adapter or game values, JSON pointers or fragments, commands or arguments, exception messages or type names, stack traces, provider messages, and child stdout or stderr are forbidden. The separately validated `sourceIdentity` carries the opaque domain plus save filename and timestamp. Underlying exceptions and developer-facing messages remain internal to typed errors.

`adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed` has no atoms. Unexpected invocation failure on fresh mapper output uses owner `digest`; on an initial, preflight, locked-final, or baseline target before mutation it uses `compute`; during pipeline baseline/cache production it uses internal `cycle-cache`; and during producer envelope validation it uses `protocol`. The post-baseline fresh cache-target route deliberately retains its existing `reset-write:consumed` / `cache-target-invalid` owner because consumption has already occurred. Commit-guard infrastructure remains `refresh` / `current-commit-failed`. Missing, unloadable, stream-writing, or wrong-export validator packages fail configuration resolution as `config` / `adapter-release-corrupt`; ordinary adapter.json validation remains `adapter-config-invalid`.

`cycle-cache` and `cycle-bookkeeping` are formatter registry owners only. They are valid only for the matching `Invoke-CompanionCycle.detail` rows, are never attempt outcome classes, and are never accepted by a terminal diagnostic validator. When bridge target revalidation succeeds, their detail is reduced to the existing null-detail pipeline warning; failed revalidation produces a new `freshness` detail. Only an invalid cycle tuple maps to `refresh:cycle-outcome-invalid`.

The formatter enforces the registry, canonical integer spelling, atom order, and the 512-scalar limit. Terminal serialization then enforces the 4096-byte compact UTF-8 limit. If optional atoms prevent the record from fitting, it drops complete trailing atom clauses and reserializes, never cutting a token or escape sequence. A reason-only record that cannot fit is a terminal serialization failure, not permission to emit malformed JSON or free text.

Diagnostic provenance follows the attempt stage. Selection means successful save enumeration in acquisition stage 2. A validated preflight target is the stage-4 `current.json` snapshot after `Read-AdapterDigestDocument` plus source and floor validation. It supplies provenance for stage-5 and pre-freeze stage-6 failures. Once the shared operation freezes and validates the authoritative target under `consume.lock`, that final target's source identity and generation replace the preflight provenance for every later outcome. In the table, `selected identity` means this currently latched identity. A mapper digest validated privately inside a refresh cycle is not yet an acquisition target and does not set `generationTimestamp`. Provenance latches only within the current attempt. A `compute` validator-infrastructure failure before any target admission carries the selected save identity and null generation; after preflight or locked-final admission it carries the most recently admitted target identity and generation. Every `config` outcome is the one provenance-clearing exception, including `adapter-config-confirmation-failed` or `adapter-config-changed` after source selection and `cycle.lock` acquisition; those late configuration failures discard the selected identity rather than contradicting the null `config` matrix:

| Class or stage | `lockStage` | `sourceIdentity` | `generationTimestamp` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `config`, `no-saves` | null | null | null |
| `source` before selection | null | null | null |
| `source` after selection | null | selected identity | null |
| `stability`, `dump`, `digest` | null | selected identity | null |
| cycle `busy` or `lock` | `cycle` | selected identity | null |
| `refresh` | null | selected identity | null |
| `freshness` before target validation | null | selected identity | null |
| `freshness` after target validation | null | selected identity | target `generatedAt` |
| `compute` before any target admission | null | selected identity | null |
| `compute` after preflight or final target admission | null | selected identity | target `generatedAt` |
| consume `busy` or `lock` | `consume` | selected identity | target `generatedAt` |
| `protocol` with `attempt-result-invalid` and `unknown` consumption | null | null | null |
| `stale-target`, both reset-write outcomes, `success`, other `protocol`, `delivery` | null | selected identity | target `generatedAt` |

`Read-TurnContextDiagnostic -Json <string> -ExpectedClass <token> [-ExpectedLockStage <cycle|consume>] [-HostGenerated]` is the shared wrapper/host validator. It uses `Read-CompanionJson` budget `config`, requires the exact seven fields and canonical unbracketed class, validates the bounded operational warning subset and ordering, checks the detail registry plus the consumption, lock-stage, and provenance matrix, requires non-empty `detail` for every failure and null for success, enforces the scalar and UTF-8 byte caps, and rejects a record whose class differs ordinally from the terminal prefix. The wrapper supplies `ExpectedLockStage` from its typed operation result; the host omits it and validates the producer-stamped field against the closed matrix. The parameter is forbidden for non-lock classes. `HostGenerated` is a narrow admission flag for the validated entry's own null-provenance protocol records. It admits only `none` plus `attempt-result-invalid` or `consumed` plus `envelope-invalid`; it is never used while validating child output. The function returns the validated `CompanionJsonValue` or throws `TurnContextProtocolException`; it never returns a partial value. `unknown` consumption is accepted only with class `protocol`, detail reason `attempt-result-invalid`, and all three provenance fields null.

Cycle-outcome mapping is exact: `initialize` and `source` map to `[source]`; `dump` and `digest` map to their same-named classes; `commit` maps to `[refresh]`. A cache or bookkeeping failure with currentCommitted true causes target revalidation: a target that meets the floor continues with the corresponding structured warning, while an absent, invalid, or below-floor target maps to `[freshness]`. Every legal outcome with `guardDiscarded: true`, including bookkeeping failure after a discard, follows the same revalidation rule because the newer current state that caused the discard may satisfy the floor. A qualifying target continues; a failed guard-discard outcome also adds a bookkeeping warning. In both guard-discard cases `refresh.attempts` increments and `refresh.commitObserved` remains false. Any outcome inconsistent with section 8's invariants maps fail-safe to non-retryable `[refresh]`.

The mapping also closes stage provenance. Cycle-lock `busy` and `lock` retain the selected identity, use null generation, and stamp `lockStage: cycle`. Commit failure and fail-safe rejection of an invalid cycle tuple return `[refresh]` with selected identity and null generation. Post-commit cache, bookkeeping, and guard-discard handling that reaches stage-4 target validation uses that target only if validation succeeds; failure there is `[freshness]`, not `[refresh]`. Consume-lock `busy` and `lock` carry selected identity, validated target generation, and `lockStage: consume`.

| Class | Stage | Automatic behavior |
|---|---|---|
| `[config]` | Adapter resolution or validation, or the 7.6 consumer-ownership preflight or locked check | Stop. |
| `[busy]` | Required cycle or consume lock unavailable after that lock's bounded wait | Stop before cycle invocation or baseline read, respectively, with no payload emission. |
| `[lock]` | Required lock path creation, open, access, or I/O failed for a reason other than live contention | Stop before cycle invocation or baseline read, respectively, with no payload emission. |
| `[stale-target]` | Another consumer advanced baseline beyond the selected target before lock acquisition, or self-healing selected a predecessor of a retained baseline | Reacquire while the three-pass budget remains. After exhaustion, stop with no mutation and direct the player to create a fresh in-game save; never reset baseline automatically. |
| `[no-saves]` | Successful enumeration returned no matches | Stop. |
| `[stability]` | Candidate remained size-unstable for the gate's complete sample window | Reacquire while the three-pass budget remains. Gate metadata exceptions map to `[source]` instead. |
| `[source]` | State/work-area setup, save enumeration, candidate metadata, or copy failed | Reacquire for typed gate or cycle disposition `vanished`, and for cycle disposition `changed`; enumeration, setup, and `unreadable` failures stop. |
| `[dump]` | Dump command failed or omitted promised output | Stop. |
| `[digest]` | Mapper command, promised output, core structure, adapter semantics, schema, adapter, game, or source-provenance validation failed | Stop. |
| `[refresh]` | Commit failed or a cycle outcome violated its invariants | Stop. |
| `[freshness]` | The post-refresh target was absent, unreadable, core-invalid, adapter-invalid, version-stale, or below the monotonic floor | Reacquire while the three-pass budget remains, then stop and name the final condition. |
| `[compute]` | Baseline infrastructure read, pre-baseline validation machinery, or diff computation failed | Stop before payload emission or baseline mutation. Post-baseline cache-target validation machinery maps to `[reset-write:consumed]`. |
| `[protocol]` | The wrapper rejected an attempt result or in-memory candidate envelope as malformed or invariant-breaking | Emit no stdout and perform no further consuming attempt. Preserve a valid result's consumption state; use `unknown` only when the attempt result itself was invalid. |
| `[reset-write:not-consumed]` | Candidate payload value built, baseline write failed | The wrapper starts one new in-process attempt and applies the exhaustive transaction table below. |
| `[reset-write:consumed]` | Candidate payload value built and baseline advanced, then the cache phase failed | Select this envelope with `cacheDegraded: true`; do not run another consuming attempt. |
| `[delivery]` | Process stdout write of the selected validated JSON failed | Emit no usable payload, never retry consumption, and disclose that the selected attempt consumed state. |

`Read-TurnContextEnvelope` is a concrete core-module function with two parameter sets: producer mode is `-Json <string> -AdapterConfig <validated object> -ExpectedCacheDegraded <bool>`; host transport mode is `-Json <string> -TransportOnly`. It parses with `Read-CompanionJson` budget `turn-context` and the same JSON rules as the two document validators and returns the validated `CompanionJsonValue` or throws typed `TurnContextProtocolException`. It requires the exact top-level field set shown above; integer `turnContextVersion: 2`; integer `adapterVersion`; non-empty string `game`; an exact source-identity object; a refresh object with exactly Boolean `attempted`, Boolean `commitObserved`, and integer `attempts` from 0 through the acquisition budget 3; the exact `warnings` array above; Boolean `baselineReset`; Boolean `cacheDegraded`; one `Read-DiffDocument`-valid diff; and one core-valid state. Producer mode additionally requires that state to pass `Assert-AdapterDigestDocument` against its exact held `AdapterConfig`. It enforces every equality and floor invariant listed below the envelope block, plus `attempted == (attempts > 0)`, `commitObserved` implies `attempted`, `baselineReset == (diff.baselineGeneratedAt is null)`, both nested `schemaVersion` values equality with `Get-CoreSchemaVersion`, and pairwise equality of top-level adapterVersion and game, sourceFloor domainId, and nested diff and state provenance where the envelope contract requires it. Producer mode additionally requires `adapterVersion` equality with `AdapterConfig.adapterVersion`, ordinal top-level `game` equality with `AdapterConfig.game`, and domainId equality with the snapshot-derived `AdapterConfig.sourceDomainId`. Pairwise agreement does not substitute for these running-config checks in producer mode. Host transport mode repeats the exact structure, core nested-document, pairwise, floor, and running-core checks but neither accepts nor rereads mutable adapter configuration. Adapter admission at that boundary belongs to the validated host entry's immediately following fresh Acquisition snapshot and must succeed before it returns nested state. When producer mode is selected, `cacheDegraded` must equal the mandatory structured attempt result flag. Missing, extra, malformed, wrong-version, or invariant-breaking content is `[protocol]`.

The wrapper always supplies its transaction-owned adapter snapshot and the validated `TurnContextAttemptResult.cacheDegraded` value to this validator; it never derives either from envelope fields or re-reads adapter.json after an attempt returns. The validated host entry first uses `-TransportOnly`: the wrapper already performed producer validation against its discarded snapshot, while transport validation protects against child-output corruption without binding itself to adapter.json. The entry then resolves a fresh Acquisition snapshot, checks configuration identity, and independently adapter-admits nested state before returning it. A configuration change is therefore handled by that explicit admission gate, not by silently rereading mutable bytes inside transport validation. Section 10 may resolve another short-lived snapshot only for opening-session walk-back policy after the state has already been admitted.

For a structurally valid attempt result, the wrapper validates every envelope-bearing attempt before selection. An envelope validation failure is fail-closed: discard the full candidate, perform no further consuming attempt, and preserve the attempt's structured consumption state in the final bounded diagnostic. This applies even when the attempt reported notConsumed because the candidate contract itself is unreliable. No arbitrary attempt stderr is parsed for status, retained, or forwarded. Adapter and pipeline warnings remain typed in the attempt-local sink while an envelope is viable; selection merges them plus the transaction operational sink into that envelope. A terminal failure discards the attempt-local remainder but carries the bounded operational sink in its diagnostic. This privacy boundary is structural, not a convention around redirection: `Invoke-TurnContextAttempt` and every core library function it calls write no process output, error, warning, verbose, debug, information, or progress stream. They return typed outcomes and warnings to an attempt-local diagnostic sink. The adapter command runner drains child stdout and stderr through the bounded zero-retention pumps in section 8 and returns only typed command results. Executable entry scripts are the sole stream renderers. A test helper that writes directly to any process stream is therefore a contract violation, not a warning the wrapper is expected to scrub after logging.

The host starts one validated entry process at the start of every game-related turn and reads only that entry's final stdout, stderr, and exit code. The validated entry owns the child transport supervisor and starts exactly one acquisition transaction child; the calling host never captures or interprets the child's streams. The consuming wrapper normally makes one internal attempt; the single prescribed `[reset-write:not-consumed]` retry remains part of that child process and transaction. The validated entry drains child stdout and stderr concurrently through strict UTF-8 byte-counting pumps, applies one entry-owned 3,600-second composite deadline, and allows a final five-second teardown and drain grace. Stdout retains at most 33,554,432 bytes, covering the complete 32-MiB turn-context document; stderr retains at most 65,536 bytes, well above the 4,096-byte terminal record. A cap-plus-one byte latches overflow, stops retention, attempts process-tree termination, and drains or closes both streams only within the teardown grace. Overflow is host-only `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: unknown`, because forced termination cannot establish whether a baseline mutation completed. A process-start failure before successful process creation is host-only `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: none`. Once creation succeeds, an abnormal exit other than 0 or 1, deadline, wait failure, capture failure, or teardown failure that yields neither one accepted envelope nor one accepted terminal diagnostic is host-only `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: unknown`. The deadline path attempts process-tree termination, closes the redirected streams, and observes both drains only within the fixed grace. Every transport failure injects nothing, discloses its state, and never retries the wrapper. The 3,600-second value is an outer liveness cap, not an execution-time promise. Six legal passes across two attempts can approach 3,300 seconds in explicit cycle-lock, child-command, and consume-lock waits; their requested stability sleeps add a nominal 60 seconds. The remaining nominal 240 seconds is headroom, not a hard completion guarantee, because scheduling, hashing, synchronous file I/O, validation, and publication have no absolute elapsed-time ceiling. The exit-0 and exit-1 rules below apply only after clean transport completion. The following table is exhaustive for protocol-valid envelopes. `A` is the first attempt's envelope. `B` is the retry envelope and may select a newer source. A and B exist only in transaction-local memory until envelope selection finishes. Superseded or rejected envelopes are never injected, persisted, or logged in full and are discarded before the transaction returns; diagnostic summaries may retain only outcome class, consumption state, source identity, generation timestamp, and terminal detail. The wrapper is their complete consumer and transaction completion is their invalidation point, so this bridge creates no second state-snapshot store.

| Attempt | Structured result | Wrapper action | Further consuming attempt |
|---|---|---|---|
| First | `success` with valid A | Select A with `cacheDegraded: false`. | None. |
| First | `reset-write:consumed` with valid A | Select A with `cacheDegraded: true`. | None. |
| First | `reset-write:not-consumed` with valid A | Reduce A to its diagnostic summary, retain only its bounded operational warnings transaction-wide, and discard the full envelope. | Start one new attempt. |
| First | Any other result | Emit no envelope and return its bounded diagnostic. | None. |
| Retry after A was not consumed | `success` with valid B | Select B, merging transaction operational warnings; discard the rest of A's summary at completion. | None. |
| Retry after A was not consumed | `reset-write:consumed` with valid B | Select B with `cacheDegraded: true` and transaction operational warnings; discard the rest of A's summary at completion. | None. |
| Retry after A was not consumed | `reset-write:not-consumed` with valid B | Reduce B, emit no envelope, and return one bounded failure summary. | None. |
| Retry after A was not consumed | Any other result | Emit no envelope and return one bounded failure summary. | None. |

Consumption state, not an attempt outcome label alone, determines whether an envelope is eligible for selection. In particular, B remains selected when it advanced baseline but failed later in the cache phase. No branch performs a third consuming attempt. Once selection finishes, the wrapper writes the selected already-validated `envelopeJson` bytes verbatim as the only stdout value. Exit 0 means exactly one complete selected envelope. Stderr is empty on exit 0; degraded state is represented only by validated envelope fields, including `cacheDegraded` and `warnings`. Exit 1 means stdout is empty except that a failed stream write may have left partial bytes, which the host ignores; stderr contains exactly one line of the form `[turn-context:<class-token>] <bounded-json-summary>`, for example `[turn-context:protocol] {...}`. This terminal record describes only the wrapper's final outcome. The JSON object has exactly the seven diagnostic fields above; its unbracketed `outcomeClass` equals `<class-token>` ordinally, and its `consumptionState` is the wrapper-derived state rather than a host inference from text. The wrapper may use `unknown` only for `[protocol]` with detail reason `attempt-result-invalid`, because the rejected result was the only attempted source of baseline-mutation evidence. That combination carries null lock stage and provenance and never authorizes a retry. No valid `TurnContextAttemptResult` uses `unknown`. Both wrapper and validated entry validate it with `Read-TurnContextDiagnostic`; the calling host trusts the resulting structured fields and never parses `detail` to infer class, consumption, provenance, or retry behavior. `[delivery]` is the only failure after selection and always reports consumed, whether the consuming wrapper cannot write its selected envelope or the validated entry cannot complete its final raw-byte write. The validated entry validates the successful child stdout once more with `Read-TurnContextEnvelope -TransportOnly`, then performs fresh-config identity and adapter admission before returning it. Child exit 0 with any terminal record, child exit 1 with missing, duplicate, malformed, or class-contradictory framing, and any success-payload admission failure map to the outer `[protocol]`; the entry emits no envelope and never reruns the child. A valid child exit-1 diagnostic supplies its validated `none`, `notConsumed`, or `consumed` state. An invalid, absent, duplicate, or contradictory child exit-1 diagnostic instead produces `consumptionState: unknown`. Invalid child exit-0 output produces `consumptionState: consumed`, because inner exit 0 is reachable only after selection of an attempt that advanced baseline. A calling tool that truncates or rejects the validated entry's own exit-0 stdout likewise injects nothing and discloses a host-only consumed state without rerunning the entry. Outside a wrapper- or validated-entry-generated `attempt-result-invalid` diagnostic, `unknown` exists only in the host failure/disclosure result. `Read-TurnContextDiagnostic` accepts it only for that exact protocol shape, and it never authorizes a retry. Host-generated protocol failures trust and reproduce no provenance or detail from rejected bytes. A message is game-related when it concerns the game world, mechanics, lore, the player's playthrough or choices, saves, or any state-derived guidance; a mixed message qualifies if any part meets that predicate. Repository work, backlog management, companion feature design, and other development-only conversation do not qualify. The selected final envelope is injected into that same turn and processed under the notice, baseline-reset, research-cache, and guidance rules even when the immediate question is general game lore. After the consuming child exits 0, this temporary bridge is at-most-once rather than replayable: baseline has advanced, so outer admission failure, calling-tool transport rejection, or host or model session death before presentation can lose that consumed transition. When the validated entry or calling host remains alive to disclose such a post-exit-0 failure, it reports `consumptionState: consumed`; the next ordinary transaction compares from the advanced baseline and does not replay the notice. The execution runtime's durable event journal and acknowledgement protocol close this complete loss window; this bridge does not pretend to.

### 7.6 Durable consumption: consumer ownership, state-event outbox, and settlement

Status: designed for the [companion execution runtime](companion-execution-runtime.md) coordinator MVP and not yet implemented. Until it ships, no code path produces the outcomes below, `state\consumer.json` and `state\outbox\` do not exist, and the bridge of 7.5 is the only consumer. The sentences this section adds to sections 1 through 12, each of which cites 7.6, describe the same unimplemented design.

This section is the core half of the durable state-event lifecycle in the [runtime persistence pattern](../patterns/runtime-work-projection-persistence-boundaries.md#durable-state-event-lifecycle). The execution runtime does not call the 7.5 bridge. It consumes through `Publish-StateEvent.ps1`, which runs the 7.5 freshness acquisition unchanged and then commits the consumed transition as one durable outbox record before the baseline advances, so a consumed transition can no longer be lost between baseline advancement and delivery. The journal, delivery, acknowledgement, and replay that follow are runtime-owned and are specified in the runtime feature. The core stays game-agnostic and runtime-agnostic: it knows a consumer role, an opaque principal token, and an outbox, and never a session, a model, or a presentation client.

Three entry scripts make up the surface: `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` (the runtime-role consuming transaction), `Set-StateEventSettlement.ps1` (accepts the runtime's settlement of one record), and `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1` (switches consumer ownership). Each is plain PowerShell 7.5. At exit 0 it writes exactly one compact JSON value to stdout; at exit 1 it writes exactly one typed terminal diagnostic line to stderr; it writes no other stream output. Each enters the migration entry fence first, as the five consuming and publishing entry scripts do (`Get-ValidatedTurnContext.ps1` does not, because it only supervises a child that does). A fence refusal is the fence's own fixed `[maintenance]` line with exit 1, outside this section's diagnostic protocol, exactly as for those scripts. Every operation in this section that reads or writes `state\consumer.json`, `state\outbox\`, or `baseline.json` holds `consume.lock` (7.1) for its whole critical section, so the three scripts and the two bridge-role consumers are mutually serialized.

This section adds no exception type. Marker, ledger, and record rejection throw `CompanionDocumentValidationException`, or `CompanionJsonParseException` from the reader beneath it; adapter rejection of a record's state throws `AdapterDigestValidationException`; and diagnostic rejection throws `TurnContextProtocolException`. The hierarchy of 7.1 therefore stays closed.

#### Consumer ownership

Exactly one consumer role owns consumption for a companion root: `bridge` (consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` and the 7.5 turn-context transaction) or `runtime` (`Publish-StateEvent.ps1`). `state\consumer.json` is the ownership marker. It contains exactly integer `consumerVersion: 1`, string `owner` equal ordinally to `bridge` or `runtime`, and canonical UTC `o` string `changedAt`, and is read through `Read-CompanionJson` budget `config`. The shared marker reader separates open and read from parsing, like the baseline reader in 7.1, and returns exactly four typed outcomes:

| Outcome | Definition | Effective owner |
|---|---|---|
| `absent` | Direct open returned typed `FileNotFoundException` or `DirectoryNotFoundException` | `bridge`, the literal owner of every install that predates this section. |
| `valid` | Bytes read, JSON parsed, exact fields and values admitted | The stored `owner`. |
| `content-invalid` | Bytes were read completely, then parsing or exact-field validation failed | None. Every consuming caller fails closed. |
| `infrastructure-failure` | Open or read failed for any other reason | None. Every consuming caller fails closed. |

Every consuming operation declares its role and performs the authoritative ownership check after acquiring `consume.lock` and before freezing a target or reading the baseline. The check is exhaustive:

| Caller role | Marker outcome and outbox | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `bridge` | `absent`, or `valid` with owner `bridge`; no pending outbox record | Proceed. |
| `bridge` | `absent`, or `valid` with owner `bridge`; at least one pending outbox record | `config` / `consumer-outbox-pending`. |
| `bridge` | `valid` with owner `runtime` | `config` / `consumer-not-owner`. |
| `runtime` | `valid` with owner `runtime` | Proceed. |
| `runtime` | `absent`, or `valid` with owner `bridge` | `config` / `consumer-not-owner`. |
| either | `content-invalid` | `config` / `consumer-owner-invalid`. |
| either | `infrastructure-failure` | `config` / `consumer-owner-unreadable`. |

Every failing row returns before any target freeze, baseline read, payload construction, or state mutation, with `consumptionState: none`. The bridge-role pending test enumerates `state\outbox\` for record file names only and counts a record file as pending without consulting the ledger; an absent directory is zero records, and an enumeration failure is `config` / `consumer-owner-unreadable`. A settled record file whose delete keeps failing therefore also blocks the bridge until `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1` or the operator removes it; failing closed is the safe reading of a file the bridge cannot classify. That row closes the hole a deleted marker would otherwise open: a committed record whose baseline apply is still outstanding must never be bypassed by a bridge read. `consume.lock` serializes callers but cannot make two clients one consumer (7.1); this check is what makes the two roles exclusive. The turn-context wrapper additionally performs the marker read without the lock after configuration resolution and before attempt A, so a non-owner ends as terminal `config` before any refresh work; that preflight is advisory and the locked check remains authoritative. `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek`, the watcher, the one-shot, and the walk never consume and are not gated by ownership.

The marker's lifecycle is closed. Creation and refresh: only `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1`, under `consume.lock`, through the 7.1 atomic state-write discipline. Invalidation: none is automatic; a content-invalid marker is repaired only by rerunning `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1`, which may overwrite it. Scope: the companion root. Complete reader list: the locked ownership check (run by both consuming operations and by settlement), the turn-context wrapper's advisory preflight, and `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1`. Bounds: one fixed-size file. Deleting the marker by hand returns ownership to `bridge` only when no record file remains in the outbox; otherwise every consumer fails closed until `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1` settles the obligation. Hand deletion is not a supported release even then: the runtime journal may still hold settled events it has not delivered, which the core cannot see. The supported way to return ownership is the runtime's release command (runtime feature), whose abandon mode removes the baseline whatever the marker says.

`Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1 [-CompanionRoot <path>] -Owner <bridge|runtime> [-DeliveryConfirmed] [-AbandonUndelivered]` is the only writer. Its argument check is typed, not left to parameter binding, and runs before the lock is taken: an `-Owner` that is missing or not exactly `bridge` or `runtime` is `config` / `owner-argument-invalid`, and both release switches together, or either switch with `-Owner runtime`, is `config` / `release-switch-invalid`. The script then acquires `consume.lock`, reads the marker, and runs outbox reconciliation (below), in repair mode when `-AbandonUndelivered` is present. A marker `infrastructure-failure` is `config` / `consumer-owner-unreadable` with no mutation. Whether the root is *releasing* is judged once, after reconciliation step 5 and before step 6: it is releasing when the marker is `valid` with owner `runtime`, when it is `content-invalid` (the most cautionary reading of an unknown owner), or when a record file is pending at that point under any marker outcome, valid or not. Under `-AbandonUndelivered` the script deletes `baseline.json` at that point, whatever the marker outcome and whether or not a record is pending, before step 6 writes any quarantine tombstone, and step 7 is skipped because the release removes the baseline anyway. A quarantine therefore never retires the last pending record while the baseline survives, and an abandon on a root that is not releasing still leaves no baseline, because the runtime journal may hold undelivered events the core cannot see. The transition table is exhaustive:

| Root state | Requested owner | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Not releasing | `bridge` with `-AbandonUndelivered` | The baseline is already deleted (before step 6). No ledger write and no marker write. Report `changed: false` and `abandonedCount: 0`. |
| Not releasing | `bridge` with `-DeliveryConfirmed` or neither switch | No marker write. Report `changed: false`. |
| Not releasing | `runtime` | Write the marker with owner `runtime`. |
| Releasing, marker `valid` with owner `runtime` | `runtime` | No marker write. Report `changed: false`. |
| Releasing, any other marker outcome | `runtime` | Write the marker with owner `runtime`; pending records stay pending for the runtime to drain. |
| Releasing | `bridge` with `-DeliveryConfirmed` | Require zero pending records; otherwise `config` / `release-pending-records` with no mutation. (An invalid pending record has already stopped reconciliation as `outbox-record-invalid`.) Then write the marker with owner `bridge`. The baseline is untouched. |
| Releasing | `bridge` with `-AbandonUndelivered` | In this order, after the baseline deletion before step 6: tombstone every remaining pending record with outcome `abandoned` in one ledger write; delete those record files; write the marker with owner `bridge`. Reconciliation's repair mode has already quarantined every invalid record. |
| Releasing | `bridge` with neither switch | `config` / `release-decision-required` with no mutation. |

`-DeliveryConfirmed` is the caller's assertion that every transition the runtime ever journaled was also delivered and acknowledged. The core cannot see the runtime journal and so cannot verify it; the runtime's own release command is the supported caller and passes it only when its journal shows no unacknowledged event and a verified history, no record file remains in the outbox, and no repair is unfinished (runtime feature), and a direct operator call owns the same decision. `-AbandonUndelivered` records the explicit decision to discard undelivered transitions. Because it always removes the baseline, whatever the marker outcome, the bridge's next consuming read takes 7.1's `absent` path and reports `baselineReset: true`, so the standing rule discloses that changes since the previous read may not be shown. That reset disclosure is how the discarded obligation is carried forward instead of being dropped silently.

The abandon order makes every crash state safe. The baseline is removed before the release writes its first tombstone, a quarantine tombstone included, so it is gone before the last pending record is tombstoned. While a pending record remains, the root is still releasing whatever the marker says, so a rerun repeats the release and removes the baseline again if a `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` reconciliation restored it in between. Once no pending record remains, the baseline is already gone, so even when the marker was absent or `bridge` and the rerun finds nothing to release, the bridge's next read still reports the reset. A first run on a root that is not releasing (an absent or `bridge` marker with no pending record) deletes the baseline too, so a settled event the runtime never delivered is not discarded without a reset; a rerun or a cautious abandon there can delete a baseline the bridge wrote in between, which costs one extra reset disclosure and loses nothing. A crash before the marker write under a `runtime` marker leaves the root releasing, and either the rerun or the next `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` (which reports an ordinary baseline reset) is correct. The exit-0 stdout value is exactly `{ "owner": <string>, "previousOwner": <string>, "changed": <bool>, "baselineRemoved": <bool>, "abandonedCount": <nonnegative integer> }`, where `owner` is the requested owner; `previousOwner` is the marker outcome the script read under the lock before any write: exactly `absent`, `bridge` or `runtime` for a valid marker's owner, or `invalid` for a `content-invalid` marker (an unreadable marker fails before any result); `changed` is true exactly when this run wrote the marker; `baselineRemoved` is true exactly when this run deleted an existing `baseline.json`, and after every exit-0 `-AbandonUndelivered` result the baseline is absent whether or not this run removed it, so no caller branches on the value; and `abandonedCount` is the number of pending records this run tombstoned with outcome `abandoned`. The runtime trusts its journal's delivery history after a hand-over only when `previousOwner` is `bridge`, because only a supported release writes a valid bridge marker (runtime feature).

#### Outbox layout, record, and ledger

`state\outbox\` holds record files, one ledger, and a quarantine directory. A record file is a direct child named `event-<sequence>.json`, where `<sequence>` is exactly 16 decimal digits, zero-padded, from 1 through 9,007,199,254,740,991; exhaustion fails closed as `outbox` / `outbox-sequence-exhausted`. The ledger is `ledger.json`. `quarantine\` holds invalid record bytes set aside by a repair. A record file is *pending* exactly when the ledger holds no tombstone for its sequence.
