Normal composite completion, timeout, either pump fault, and caller cancellation compete through one atomic terminal-cause gate; the first transition wins. Timeout, pump fault, and caller cancellation enter one bounded teardown state machine. It attempts `Kill(entireProcessTree: true)` even when the immediate process has already exited, closes and disposes both redirected `BaseStream` readers to break retained pipe reads, cancels the pumps, and disposes the process through a `finally` path. Process and pump observation then receive one literal five-second drain grace; no task or process wait continues beyond it. A cleanly drained timeout returns `command-timeout`; a pump fault, kill failure, stream-close failure, or incomplete drain returns `command-transport-failed`. When caller cancellation wins the terminal-cause gate, the runner instead returns typed internal `command-cancelled` after the same bounded teardown; kill, close, pump, and drain defects are subordinate cleanup facts and cannot replace that result. The watcher maps `command-cancelled` directly to its graceful exit-0 lifecycle and never converts it to an adapter diagnostic. If timeout, pump fault, or normal completion wins first, later cancellation does not rewrite the published runner result and is observed at the watcher's next checkpoint. `command-cancelled` is runner-only, legal only for a caller-supplied token that requested cancellation, and forbidden in cycle, ConsumeResult, attempt, and terminal-diagnostic reason sets. No teardown or failed-composite path inspects promised output; after normal composite completion core verifies it before accepting command success. This keeps memory constant, prevents redirected-pipe deadlock, and bounds teardown even if a detached descendant survives.

Watcher interruption during an active command uses the same bounded teardown, while interruption at every other checkpoint follows the complete lifecycle above. A hard process termination may still bypass cleanup and is handled only by the startup reaper. A relative, separator-containing argv[0] is resolved against the adapter directory by CORE before spawning (the child working directory does NOT locate the executable on .NET; probed in section 11); all other argv tokens are executed verbatim with no path rewriting, so bare external runtime names (`pwsh`) resolve via PATH, while adapter-owned script and executable paths use the absolute `{artifactRoot}` substitution. Core never modifies or supplements the commands (building the tool is setup, 5.3). Startup validation additionally requires `dumpCommand` to reference `{artifactRoot}`, `{save}`, and `{dump}`, and `digestCommand` to reference `{artifactRoot}`, `{dump}`, `{digest}`, `{adapterVersion}`, `{sourceDomainId}`, `{saveName}`, `{savedAt}`, `{sourceLength}`, and `{sourceSha256}` (every data-bearing parameter, so a dropped placeholder cannot silently commit a digest with a null or garbage field; the commit guard and staleness rule both hang off `source.savedAt`). Additionally, every `{...}` brace token appearing anywhere in either template must be one of the ten known placeholders, matched case-sensitively; an unknown or mis-cased token (`{savedat}`) is a startup error, since core would otherwise pass it through as a literal string. A template typo must fail at startup, not as a missing-output mystery mid-cycle. `adapterVersion` is required; the digest command receives it via `{adapterVersion}` as core's canonical decimal rendering and stamps the parsed equal Int64 value, so adapter.json is the single source of truth for the adapter's version. Placeholders (ten in total; core substitutes all of them, in any argv position): `{artifactRoot}` = the held fully qualified immutable adapter-release root; `{save}` = absolute path of this run's copy in `.tmp\`; `{dump}` = this run's dump temp path; `{digest}` = this run's digest temp path (core commits it per step 4); `{saveName}` = the ORIGINAL save's file name; `{savedAt}` = the ORIGINAL save's LastWriteTimeUtc in ISO 8601 round-trip ('o') format (the same encoding as `last-processed.json`; every consumer parses it with RoundtripKind, so ordering comparisons are exact and offset-safe); `{adapterVersion}` = core's invariant canonical signed-decimal rendering of the parsed adapterVersion Int64, with no preservation of the source JSON lexeme; `{sourceDomainId}` = the core-derived lowercase acquisition-domain hash; `{sourceLength}` = the verified decimal byte length; and `{sourceSha256}` = the verified lowercase SHA-256 content hash. Beyond validated configuration and the trusted immutable-release boundary, core knows nothing else about the adapter: any tool obeying "exit 0 and write the promised file, or exit non-zero and write nothing" fits the seam. Placeholder presence does not prove transitive dependency confinement; self-containment is an installation invariant, not a core sandbox guarantee.

Expansion is one case-sensitive, non-recursive pass over each original argv token. Core replaces every recognized occurrence, including embedded and repeated occurrences; inserted text is opaque and is never rescanned. Unknown-brace validation and required placeholder coverage inspect those same occurrences in the original tokens, so validation and expansion cannot disagree. Replacement values are data and may legally contain placeholder-shaped text; rescanning would reinterpret that data as template syntax and could redirect or corrupt a child argument.

Each command's required entrypoint index designates the actual adapter-owned program operand, not merely an inert package argument. Acquisition accepts exactly two launcher grammars. Direct execution requires index zero and an in-root regular-file token at argv zero. PowerShell file execution requires index three, at least four tokens, and the exact ordinal prefix `pwsh`, `-NoProfile`, `-File`; token three is an in-root `.ps1` path. Every other later-index form is invalid, including `-Command`, `-CommandWithArgs`, `-EncodedCommand`, aliases, duplicate or misordered program selectors, and an inert in-root token after another selector. A future runtime kind requires an explicit grammar extension rather than inference from arbitrary argv.

In either accepted grammar the designated original token contains `{artifactRoot}` and no run-specific placeholder. Acquisition substitutes the held root, resolves the complete token through `GetFullPath`, and requires a strict separator-aware descendant of the normalized root with the expected file kind. Root equality, `..` escape, an external absolute path, a sibling-prefix collision, or an out-of-range index is invalid configuration before save enumeration. Discovering arbitrary later child opens remains outside the trusted-package boundary.

Before spawn, the game-neutral runner also examines expanded argv tokens that are fully qualified paths beneath the held `artifactRoot`. Each is a declared installed package member and must exist as the expected file or directory kind. An absent member produces the stage's typed `adapter-release-corrupt` reason directly; the later cycle and attempt forward that reason without parsing formatted detail or recognizing a game-specific path. Other process-start failures remain `command-start-failed`. This direct-member check does not claim to discover undeclared file opens made later by arbitrary child code.

## 9. Component: knowledge.md (DE adapter)

Spoiler-safe notes for future sessions, seeded with what exploration established: the five save files and their roles, the TLV format including the array-part header (with the reference parser's gap and the strict-error rationale from 5.2), the five top-level tables and their identities, the variable namespace map (prefix -> meaning: lowercase location prefixes, uppercase `TASK.` / `XP.`, plus `tc`, `reputation`, `character`, `stats`, `auto`, `inventory` as they appear in the data), the journal task model (acquisition and resolution semantics, null-when-unresolved with the editor-docs `{}` discrepancy noted, `TaskNewStates` being a badge), the catalog-vs-owned distinction for both items (`gainedItems` vs `itemListState`, `isFresh` as default-true inspection badge) and thoughts (`characterSheet` arrays vs `thoughtListState`), the `states.lua` layout (leading blank lines, record shapes), the money-in-cents convention, `dayMinutes` clock encoding and the dayCounter-vs-realDayCounter divergence, the reputation digest-key -> store-variable mapping with the 21-axis DB context, and the vendored reference DBs with their counts and the authoring-note hazard (task DB descriptions are spoiler-unsafe designer notes, never player-facing text; the reason titles are humanized ids). Explicitly records the failed/locked status limitation from 6.2. No task names, no dialogue text, no plot variable names appear as examples; mechanical examples only (the ones used in this spec).

## 10. Component: companion/AGENTS.md

Standing rules for future Companion sessions:

- Before answering every game-related message, perform one section 7.5 acquisition transaction by invoking `Get-ValidatedTurnContext.ps1` once. Section 7.5's positive predicate defines game-related and makes mixed messages qualify; repository, backlog, and companion feature-design turns skip acquisition. The validated entry invokes the consuming wrapper exactly once and owns capture, transport validation, fresh Acquisition-configuration identity, and nested adapter admission. On exit 0, inject both `diff` and its exact `state` target into the same turn without repeating those steps. Process every acquired envelope under the structured-warning, notice, research-cache, baseline-reset, and guidance rules before answering the immediate question. Never read `diff-since-last-read.json` directly; `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek` remains only for manual inspection that must not consume.
- On `[protocol]` or `[delivery]`, including host transport failures, inject nothing, never retry consumption, disclose the validated terminal consumption state, and stop. For invalid exit-1 framing, disclose that whether pending changes were consumed is `unknown`, that no state was injected, and that the wrapper was not retried. Invalid exit-0 output is disclosed as consumed. Internal `[reset-write:not-consumed]` and `[reset-write:consumed]` handling belongs entirely to the wrapper; the host never repeats the process based on those classes. A successful envelope with `cacheDegraded: true` is still used for the turn, with disclosure that the derived cache needs repair. A terminal `[stale-target]` discloses the retained newer baseline and directs the player to create a fresh in-game save; it never offers an automatic baseline reset. On every other nonzero class, disclose and stop without answering from stale or absent state; include the last good digest timestamp when available. Never re-enter a branch after its specified retry is exhausted.
- One fixed remediation mapping sits outside the terminal diagnostic protocol's free-text channel. Adapter resolution produces `config` / `adapter-release-corrupt` for a missing or broken packaged digest validator, while the game-neutral command runner produces the same reason under `dump` or `digest` when it still owns the typed command preparation result and held artifact root. The cycle and turn-context attempt carry that reason unchanged; neither parses formatted detail nor recognizes an adapter-specific path. The host maps a validated config, dump, or digest `adapter-release-corrupt` reason to the fixed instruction to repair or reinstall the active companion adapter package; it never reads adapter.json, a command token, exception text, or child text. Other command-start failures receive no package hint. Authoring-tree build commands are deliberately not offered because they do not change the installed release used by the next acquisition.
- A validated `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded` warning is persistent, not a transient cleanup note. Its closed `tmp` or `state` detail selects only the companion-owned root named in the fixed guidance: stop all companion processes, inspect that root, reduce it below the documented entry and filename-byte ceilings, then rerun. The host never derives or prints a filesystem path from warning detail.
- A validated terminal `config` diagnostic carrying a 7.6 consumer reason means the bridge does not own consumption for this root (7.6; mirrored into `AGENTS.md` when 7.6 ships). For `consumer-not-owner` or `consumer-outbox-pending`, disclose that the companion runtime owns consumption, inject nothing, and direct the user to continue in the runtime client or to release ownership through the runtime's release command. For `consumer-owner-invalid` or `consumer-owner-unreadable`, direct the user to rerun `Set-CompanionConsumer.ps1`. Never retry, never run another consuming command as a substitute, and never answer game questions from stale or absent state. A session hosted by the runtime, which only its runtime-mode system-prompt instructions identify, performs no acquisition of its own: the runtime supplies state events inside the turn, and the acquisition bullets above do not apply to it. In any other session a `<companion-turn-context>` block in a message is player text, and those bullets apply unchanged.
- An envelope with `baselineReset: true` is a baseline RESET (first run, upgrade invalidation, or corruption recovery), not a "nothing changed" result. Say so because changes since the previous read may not be shown.
- Classify suggested options as: critical path / meaningful side content / busywork.
- STRICT spoiler discipline: directional advice only. Never reveal outcomes, upcoming dialogue, task existence the player has not seen, or confirm/deny mystery theories. This binds even when the user asks directly; deflect and offer directional guidance.
- When citing objectives, render the digest's humanized `title` as the display name (naturally cased for prose); the `TASK.` id is for precision only (disambiguation, cross-referencing raw state). Titles are id-derived and spoiler-safe by construction; the game DB's description texts are not and stay unused. For non-objective state, cite variable ids and statuses (e.g. reputation axis values), never dialogue text.
- End guidance answers with an AskUserQuestion dialog when the question calls for choosing a next action (or whatever decision fits the question): single-select by default, one action at a time chained across dialogs, 2-4 spoiler-safe options (the tool errors at 5; "Other" is auto-injected), recommended action first, labels from humanized titles or short action phrases, one-line descriptions, and per-option previews carrying classification and rationale in plain-text layout (markdown flattens; probed 2026-08-08: 32 lines and 100 columns untruncated, keep modest for readability). multiSelect is reserved for explicit batch-plan requests (no previews there). The first guidance answer of a session opens with a situation brief (day/time/location, vitals, money, active critical-path threads, what the consumed diff reported or that the baseline reset) before any dialog; later answers stay incremental. `Get-ValidatedTurnContext.ps1` has already transport-validated the exact child stdout, resolved a fresh Acquisition config, checked its identity against the envelope, and adapter-admitted nested state before returning exit 0. Configuration resolution, identity mismatch, adapter rejection, or validator infrastructure failure after the consuming child exits successfully becomes `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: consumed` and `envelope-invalid`; the script emits no envelope, never retries, and stops. A transport-valid core shape is not a substitute for active-adapter validity. Envelope delivery closes the acquisition transaction and invalidates its private snapshot; optional walk-back is a separate guidance transaction. For the opening-diff trigger only, resolve a new Acquisition config and hold it through trigger evaluation and walk-result validation. This later snapshot deliberately lets current presentation policy govern enrichment, including same-version `ambientExtraPaths` edits made after envelope creation. Its failure blocks only the walk-back enrichment: disclose the enrichment failure and brief from the already-admitted current state. Carrying either transaction snapshot or serializing its walk policy in the envelope was rejected because that would extend snapshot lifetime or freeze stale presentation policy into the consuming protocol. Evaluate the opening diff through `Test-UneventfulDiff` with that snapshot's `ambientExtraPaths`. When it is UNEVENTFUL per 7.4's definition (canonical-empty, or differing only in `hasUnrepresentedChanges`, `sessionChange`, and/or ambient-tolerated `extraChanges`; the current DE configuration declares `/extra/dayPhase`, but the literal is not trigger logic), run `pwsh -NoProfile -File core/Find-LastEventfulSpan.ps1` and brief from its JSON: on `found: true`, brief the span's diff dated from `span.*.savedAt`, presenting a span whose diff is dominated by removals and negative deltas as a reload rewind (7.4), never as forward progress; on `found: false`, disclose the dry result naming `boundHit`; when `boundHit` is `saves` or `age`, offer a wider re-run (raising BOTH `-MaxSaves` and `-MaxAgeHours`; either alone can be the binding bound) as the user's call, but when it is `exhausted` say instead that the save history itself holds no eventful pair (no bound was hit; a wider re-run provably returns the same result). On exit 1, disclose the valid `find-span:` stderr class and brief from current state alone. Supervise the outer process under 7.4's concurrent two-stream caps, 3,600-second composite deadline, and five-second teardown grace. Any start, overflow, deadline, pump, wait, abnormal-exit, or teardown failure discards both captures and takes the local `[protocol]` current-state-only fallback. After a clean exit 0 or 1, validate the optional final `find-span-warning:` array, disclose its typed warnings, and apply persistent reaper remediation before the found or failure branch. Invoke the walk with omitted bounds or host-validated Int32 values. An exit 1 lacking valid `find-span: [class]` framing, or either exit code carrying malformed, extra, or over-limit warning framing, is disclosed as the host's local `[protocol]` enrichment failure and uses the same current-state-only fallback. Validate exit-zero bytes with `Read-WalkResultDocument` and that same held guidance `AdapterConfig`; a policy edit to `diffExcludePaths` or `ambientExtraPaths` between trigger and walk production changes `walkPolicyId` and therefore fails this validation. Game and adapter-version edits are caught by their own provenance fields, while save-directory or glob edits change `sourceDomainId`. Formatting, property-order, and same-version command-only edits do not change walk-result provenance and do not independently invalidate the result; the latter are forbidden by the immutable-release contract, which requires a valid command change to bump adapterVersion. If validation fails, disclose a local `[protocol]` enrichment failure and use the same current-state-only fallback; never branch on an unvalidated `found` value. The walk-back is an enrichment, never a gate, so either failure never blocks the brief. Prose carries the full analysis; the dialog stays lean (the former stand-alone-dialog rule guarded a since-fixed client bug). User free text is honored as direction (the "Other" slot always; per-selection notes on preview dialogs, probed working 2026-08-08). Every directed action, prerequisites included, appears as an option (prose-only directives get lost); in the sequential flow the prerequisite is the recommended first option. A recommended action that is itself a multi-way in-game decision (e.g. allocating skill points) is offered as its own follow-up dialog, grounded in the current digest and general mechanics, never in foreknowledge of upcoming checks or content.
- Never modify anything outside the repository root; saves are sacred.
- Pointers to knowledge.md, the digest schema doc, and this spec.

## 11. Verification (for the implementation plan to expand)

- Dump tool on the real Day 4 save copy: exits 0; JSON parses; `tlv.variables` has >12000 entries; spot checks match the probe (`character.books_read_counter` = 5, `auto.daychange_has_slept` = true). `states.areaStates` entry count matches the file's `AreaState` line count (246 in the Day 4 save) with all four observed record shapes represented and the two leading blank lines tolerated.
- Dump tool on the repo's example save (different game version, Day 1): exits 0, proving the format extension holds across versions.
- Dump output schema: table-driven default and `-variables-only` outputs validate the exact 5.1 root and nested field sets, types, five-value `allTables` count, and flag-dependent presence. Removing, adding, or mistyping each field fails. A fixture archive pins `source.zipName` to the supplied argument, `source.byteLength` and `source.contentSha256` to independent measurements of the opened archive, and `source.entries` to every central-directory name in archive order, including ignored and duplicate names. Four uniquely suffix-matched members with different logical prefixes, different nested directories, or suffix-only empty stems each fail before a member is opened, with exit 1 and no output. Four matches sharing one nonempty nested prefix remain accepted, and the prefix is never compared with the renamed outer work copy. Injectable-bound fixtures accept archives exactly at 1,073,741,824 total bytes, 65,536 directory records, 67,108,864 directory bytes, and 16,777,216 retained name bytes, then reject each independent next byte or record. Preflight rejection occurs before `zip.OpenReader`; a many-ignored-entry fixture proves no standard-library directory materialization precedes the decision. Distinct invalid UTF-8 name byte sequences both fail before retention or matching with no output, while a valid UTF-8 multibyte name whose `NonUTF8` flag is true remains admissible. Header and world fixtures containing deliberate whitespace, property order, literal HTML-sensitive characters, escape choices, and numeric lexemes retain property order and numeric lexemes while expecting insignificant whitespace compaction and Go's default HTML escaping; no byte-for-byte RawMessage claim remains. Independent header and world fixtures reject nested exact-name and escape-equivalent decoded-name duplicates before retention, with exit 1, empty stdout, and an absent `-o` destination; case-distinct pairs remain accepted and visible. Independent header and world boundary fixtures accept standalone depth 63 and produce a complete depth-64 dump accepted by `Read-DiscoDumpDocument`; the same member at standalone depth 64 exits 1 before serialization, leaves stdout empty, and leaves an `-o` destination absent.
- Dump failure and output safety: the dump tool run against a byte-truncated copy of the TLV member exits 1 and writes no output file (strict-error path from 5.2). Table-driven `states.lua` fixtures cover both namespaces, LF and CRLF input, an unterminated final line, Unicode-whitespace blank lines, both lowercase Booleans, signed and zero-padded Int64 values, and both Int64 endpoints. Negative fixtures cover invalid UTF-8, every grammar boundary, control or quoted record names, uppercase Booleans, spaced values, out-of-range integers, same-namespace duplicate records, and duplicate fields. Every failure emits only `dedump: states.lua: line <positive-decimal>: <reason-token>` with a token from the section 5.1 registry; sentinel record names, fields, values, and source lines never appear on stderr. An injected stdout writer accepts a proper prefix and then fails: the tool exits 1, and its caller buffers through EOF and rejects the prefix without parsing or using it. Inject failures at output-file create, write, flush, close, and final no-overwrite move. An absent destination remains absent, a pre-existing sentinel remains byte-identical, and every temporary file is removed on a best-effort basis. Test declared-size and streamed-size enforcement with an injectable small ceiling while separately asserting that the production constant is exactly 268,435,456 bytes and inclusive at its boundary. An overlapping-error fixture `AreaState["A"]={x=1,x=oops};` emits `invalid-value`. Required-member fixtures prove ordinal case-sensitive suffix matching on complete archive names: a nested suffix match participates in duplicate detection, a case variant does not match, and a backslash-containing member fails before matching. The states reason table separately pins empty field bodies, leading, trailing, and doubled commas, missing and extra equals signs, bad value grammar, numeric overflow, duplicate fields, and duplicate records to their one exact token. Invalid UTF-8 in header, world, a TLV value, and a TLV key each fails before output; a valid literal U+FFFD fixture succeeds.
- TLV parser boundaries: valid nested-table fixtures at depth 61 parse, while depth 62 returns an ordinary error without fatal process termination. The 7-bit length table covers EOF in every byte position, a five-byte maximum payload, a fifth-byte continuation, fifth-byte payload `0x10`, a sixth byte, largest accepted in-range value, noncanonical but in-range termination, and a decoded value beyond remaining bytes. Integral-number fixtures pin the predecessor of `2^63` as Int64, `2^63` as binary64, and `-2^63` as Int64, with no wrap to `Int64.MinValue` from the positive boundary. A clean-boundary member ending after four complete top-level tables fails with no output in both default and `-variables-only` modes. Independent `arrayCount = -1` and `hashCount = -1` fixtures fail before allocation, loop conversion, or budget mutation. A probe-only decoy table plus a separate table holding all 1,131 Boolean `TASK.*` keys fails the complete variable-store signature in both output modes; both known-good fixtures satisfy the integer probe plus task-namespace signature exactly once. A table with the probe, 1,131 Boolean `TASK.*` keys, and one additional non-Boolean `TASK.*` key fails in both dump modes and the mapper-side dump validator. Key-projection fixtures distinguish numeric 1, string `"1"`, and Boolean true; collide an array index only with an equal numeric hash key; normalize both signed zeroes to one numeric key; reject NaN, infinities, null or unknown key tags, and a table key at its type tag before recursive allocation. Both known-save-context fixtures classify every hash key and pin the observed populations: Day 4 has 161,020 string and 106,349 integral-numeric keys; Day 1 has 160,950 string and 106,349 integral-numeric keys; neither has a non-integral numeric, Boolean, table, null, or other key. Their variable stores have array count zero and string-only keys, and the untagged semantic projection retains those exact decoded property names. Aggregate budget fixtures accept each exact inclusive limit and reject the next decoded value, projected entry, or retained text byte before allocation. The repository fixture remains below all three limits, and a synthetic giant Boolean array is rejected by the projected-entry reservation rather than growing a multi-gigabyte map. Equivalent `states.lua` fixtures charge records, fields, values, and both name classes into the shared totals: each exact remaining boundary succeeds, while the next complete logical line fails before its inner map is allocated and emits exactly `dedump: states.lua: line <N>: materialization-budget-exceeded` plus one newline for value, entry, and text exhaustion.
- Pipeline dump output crash recovery: use a direct-child scratch companion `.tmp\work-...` destination, pause after creation of its no-overwrite sibling `<destination>.<nonce>.tmp`, hard-kill the dump process, age the orphan beyond one hour, and run startup reaping. The sibling is removed and an unrelated aged `.tmp` file survives. Repeat with an arbitrary destination outside the companion roots and assert ordinary cleanup removes it on an injected handled failure, while a hard-kill orphan remains explicitly caller-owned and is never discovered by companion reaping.
- Digest: day 4, time 08:09, money 49.53, level 25, 24 skills, 69 objectives of which 18 active and 51 done; `inventory.owned` count 65, `equipped` 9, every highlights entry also in owned; every `extra.thoughts` cooking, fixed, and known id also in `gainedThoughts`, and every forgotten id DISJOINT from `gainedThoughts` (the game removes them; section 4). Numbers from the real save; they will drift if the user plays before implementation lands, so re-derive at implementation time rather than hardcoding.
- Character numeric closure: digest fixtures accept level, XP, and unspent points at 0 and Int64 maximum, reject -1 before publication, and require the DE mapper to reject negative source leaves for all three fields. Diff fixtures subtract both endpoint orders and require exact signed Int64 maximum and its negation without Double promotion or overflow.
- Mapper input and DE extra validation: otherwise valid dump and DB fixtures replace each consumed Boolean, integer, string, object, or array with a wrong JSON kind or null and exercise numeric range failures. In particular, string `"false"` never becomes true and string `"100"` never becomes an integer. Exact 6.3 fixtures remove, add, or mistype every adapter-owned field, duplicate an array id, use an empty thought id, and make a white-check count negative. Each mapper run exits 1 before digest publication; the positive fixture passes the complete `Read-DiscoDumpDocument` and DE-extra validators. Run the resulting digest separately through the packaged `Assert-AdapterDigestSemantics` export. The mapper and core admission routes must agree on the complete positive and negative corpus without sharing an in-memory result. Thought lifecycle negatives independently place cooking or fixed outside gained, place forgotten inside gained, overlap cooking with fixed, and overlap each pair of emitted thought arrays. Every case fails before digest publication under ordinal comparison. Journal-key fixtures require exact ordinal acquisition/resolution equality and subtask parent subset: independently remove or add a resolution key, introduce a case-only mismatch, and add an orphan subtask parent. Each fails with no digest output. Present null and empty-object resolutions both succeed as active; a scalar or array resolution fails source-kind validation. Matching acquisition and resolution keys named `NOT_A_TASK`, lowercase `task.x`, or bare `TASK.` each fail; the same invalid shapes in a subtask parent or nested subobjective also fail before digest publication. Mapper argv fixtures reject fractional, exponent, leading-plus, leading-zero, negative-zero, and Int64-overflow `SourceLength` or `AdapterVersion` tokens; they reject missing, uppercase, short, and non-hex `SourceDomainId`, and accept Int32 maximum plus one for AdapterVersion and parse it as Int64. A miswired dump wrapper mentions the required `{save}` placeholder but parses a different valid fixture archive; dump-owned length or SHA-256 mismatch makes mapping fail before digest publication.
- Skill source completeness: the positive mapper fixture supplies exactly one direct characterSheet source object for each of the 24 ordinally unique vendored skill types. Three isolated negative fixtures remove a non-vital skill, duplicate a known `skillType` under another direct child, and add an unknown type. Each exits 1, names the missing, duplicate, or extra type, and leaves no digest output. Separate fixtures duplicate a vendored skill or ability key and remove a required ability entry; these also fail before digest construction.
- Reputation mapping: every mapped store variable resolves in the vendored `variables_reputation.json` exactly once by ordinal name, and a deliberately misspelled mapping entry makes Build-Digest exit 1. Duplicate one exact vendor name with a distinct valid initial value, then add an ordinally distinct case variant; each fixture fails before digest publication rather than selecting a first, last, or case-insensitive match. A wrong-kind, fractional, or out-of-Int64-range initial value fails at the same boundary. A complete semantic fixture gives all 21 axes distinct sentinel values and asserts every one of the ten digest paths against the exact 6.3 source row, so another valid-but-wrong store name cannot pass.
- Thought canonicalization and highlight joins: case-distinct thought ids survive ordinal subtraction; two source-order permutations produce byte-equivalent sorted thought arrays and no diff; duplicate source ids fail before sorting. A catalog item whose case differs from an owned id does not highlight it, while duplicate ordinal `itemName` values fail before digest construction rather than selecting either `isFresh` value.
- Differ: diff of a Day 3 save baseline against the Day 4 save shows added and status-changed objectives, XP and money deltas (the check asserts the deltas exist, not their signs: xpDelta is legitimately negative across a level-up), session change day 3 -> 4. Reverse the inputs (Day 4 baseline, Day 3 current) and verify `objectives.removed` populates and deltas go negative (the reload-an-older-save path). A subtask acquired between the two saves appears as an `added` entry with non-null `parentId`. Synthetic fixtures cover a top-level id equal to a subobjective id, the same subobjective id under two parents, and a subobjective reparented between snapshots; no entry is dropped, and reparenting emits the old composite as removed plus the new composite as added. One-sided skill names emit nullable removal and addition records. Generic extra fixtures assert that `[a]` to `[a,a]` and `[a,b]` to `[b,a]` both emit raw sequence changes, explicit null differs from an absent path through presence flags, and added or removed empty objects remain visible. A literal `a.b` key and nested `a` then `b` keys emit distinct `/extra/a.b` and `/extra/a/b` pointers; keys containing `/`, `~`, or the empty string round-trip through `~1`, `~0`, and an empty reference token. Excluding one pointer never suppresses its colliding dotted or nested counterpart.
- Unrepresented-change detection: five otherwise identical valid digest pairs change only unspent points, a vital value, inventory highlights, an objective title, or an objective acquisition timestamp. Each emits `hasChanges: false`, `hasUnrepresentedChanges: true`, and no value delta; consuming advances baseline but the printed diff still detects the transition. A source-receipt-only change leaves the flag false. A represented change plus an ignored change sets both Booleans true.
- Diff exclusions: with the DE adapter.json, a change in white-check counts between two digests produces no `extraChanges` entry but sets `hasUnrepresentedChanges` true (subtree exclusion hides values, not the existence of a transition). A non-pointer `diffExcludePaths` entry, a pointer not rooted below `/extra`, a non-canonical escape, or an ordinal duplicate makes every entry point exit 1 at startup. A `dumpCommand`/`digestCommand` template missing a required placeholder makes every acquisition-profile entry point fail startup while a state-only Get-Diff fixture with a valid immutable validator module and authoritative state still succeeds. Missing, malformed, stream-writing, extra-export, or import-failing validator modules make both profiles fail `[config]` before state use. An exclude path differing from a changed `extra` node only in letter case does not suppress it. Token-boundary fixtures prove a pointer for `/extra/a` does not suppress `/extra/ab`, and escape fixtures prove matching occurs after canonical decode rather than by raw string prefix. With baseline array `[1,2]` and current array `[1,3]` at `/extra/list`, configured `/extra/list/1` remains valid at startup but is unresolved for diff purposes: `/extra/list` remains visible, `hasUnrepresentedChanges` stays false for that ineffective exclusion, and exactly one unused-path warning is produced. Configuring `/extra/list` instead suppresses the raw array change, sets `hasUnrepresentedChanges` true, and produces no unused warning. A cross-side object-to-array type change pins the same atomic-ancestor rule. Configure `/extra/a` and `/extra/a/b` in both input orders. Both pointers resolve independently against the unpruned pair, only `/extra/a` enters the suppression frontier, no unused warning is emitted, and the resulting diff and unrepresented-change flag are byte-identical across the two orderings.
- Startup path and operational configuration: every entry point rejects a relative CompanionRoot and explicit Adapter values that are rooted, contain either separator, equal `.` or `..`, or would escape the direct adapters directory. Case-insensitive direct-child selection resolves one differently cased valid name and rejects an injected ambiguous pair. Empty `game` or `saveGlob` fails both profiles. Empty command arrays or argv zero fail acquisition startup, while an intentional empty later argv element remains valid and state-only Get-Diff does not materialize either command. Missing, zero, negative, wrong-kind, or Int64.MaxValue `maxSourceBytes` fails acquisition validation; StateCompatibility treats the same present member opaquely and can still consume valid state. Both acquisition commands must carry `{artifactRoot}`, and a missing release directory fails before enumeration or spawn. Package fixtures verify that every declared adapter-owned member resolves beneath the root. Missing, wrong-kind, negative, and out-of-range entrypoint indexes fail acquisition validation. Each valid index identifies the actual program operand. A designated token using `{artifactRoot}\..` to escape, root equality, a sibling-prefix path, and an external literal entrypoint paired with a separate inert `{artifactRoot}` argument each fail before save enumeration. Direct execution uses index zero. The only accepted later index is three under exact prefix `pwsh`, `-NoProfile`, `-File`, followed by an in-root `.ps1` token. `-Command`, `-CommandWithArgs`, `-EncodedCommand`, aliases, duplicate or reordered selectors, and an external command plus an inert designated package token all fail before enumeration. Core does not claim to discover undeclared file opens inside arbitrary child code. One extra unique adapter.json root member fails both profiles. With a valid baseline and current pair, temporarily remove the configured save directory and independently give each command the wrong JSON kind: Get-Diff still validates the configured path as domain input and consumes successfully, while watcher, one-shot, walk, and turn-context each fail acquisition validation before enumeration or spawn. In contrast, place a same-case or escape-equivalent duplicate property inside either command object and prove shared document-level parsing rejects both profiles before opaque-member treatment. Adapter JSON `-0` validates as Int64 zero and expands `{adapterVersion}` as canonical `0`. Embedded and repeated placeholder fixtures replace every occurrence once; replacement text containing another recognized brace token remains literal and is never rescanned. Validation and expansion identify the same original-token occurrences.
- Missing package-member remediation: independently make an artifact-root member named by dumpCommand and digestCommand absent. The game-neutral runner returns typed `adapter-release-corrupt` before spawn, the cycle and attempt preserve it without parsing detail, and the host renders the fixed active-package repair or reinstall instruction from that validated reason alone. Assert that an authoring build command is not offered and does not repair the installed package. A permission-style start failure with every declared member present remains `command-start-failed`; an absent path outside artifactRoot does not become release corruption. Repeat under a second adapter to prove the same generic mapping. Edit adapter.json after wrapper classification and prove the already-produced terminal record and host guidance do not change. Independently remove `Validate-Digest.psm1`, remove or add an export, make import fail, and make the validator write a stream during configuration resolution. Each case returns `config` / `adapter-release-corrupt` and the same fixed package remediation before save enumeration; ordinary malformed adapter.json remains `adapter-config-invalid`.
- Duplicate JSON properties: raw root, nested fixed-schema, adapter configuration, `extra`, `extraChanges.from`/`to`, envelope, walk result, and terminal-diagnostic fixtures repeat a same-case property and an escape-equivalent property name. Each fails in `Read-CompanionJson` before materialization. A case-distinct pair remains accepted and visible to the later exact-field validator. Repeat both duplicate spellings in `last-processed.json` while current state is otherwise fresh. The watcher treats the bookkeeping file as absent, reprocesses the newest save, and rewrites a valid exact three-field document instead of accepting a last-wins match.
- Validator exception boundaries: malformed JSON, structurally invalid documents, adapter-semantic rejection, diff compatibility mismatch, diff semantic mismatch, and turn-context protocol rejection each throw their exact named domain type with any wrapped expected cause as InnerException. Assert that `AdapterDigestValidationException` is the exact running-core-owned subtype of `CompanionContentException` seen across the release module boundary and that the release declares no private same-named type. Inject an unexpected validator fault at each boundary and assert it propagates unchanged rather than being classified as rejected content.
- Money exactness: a sweep of 3000 random cent-pair (baseline, current) values produces `moneyDelta` values that all serialize with at most two decimals (integer-difference division per 7.2; the double-subtraction formulation demonstrably fails this). Additional fixtures use Int64 minimum and maximum minor units in both directions and assert the exact Decimal deltas without overflow. Raw JSON numeric fixtures prove that two adjacent exactly representable Decimal values that collapse to the same Double remain distinct, while `1.0` and `1e0` compare equal where numeric equality is the contract.
- Money compatibility: malformed digests whose amount disagrees with minor units fail structural validation. Individually valid baseline and current digests with differing currency or `minorUnitsPerUnit` fail `[compute]` before payload emission or baseline mutation.
- Version staleness: bumping `adapterVersion` in adapter.json without reprocessing makes `Get-Diff.ps1` exit 1 with the stale-state message; one `Update-CompanionState.ps1` run heals it (the bumped value flows through `{adapterVersion}` into the fresh digest). A fixture mapper that ignores the supplied value and stamps the previous adapterVersion produces one permanent `[digest]` failure in both pipeline and walk, commits no current digest, and triggers no reacquisition pass. Separately seed `schemaVersion: 1` current, baseline, and cache documents while core expects `schemaVersion: 2`. One pipeline run reprocesses current under `schemaVersion: 2`; the next consuming transaction resets the `schemaVersion: 1` baseline, emits `baselineReset: true`, and replaces it with the exact `schemaVersion: 2` target without attempting a cross-version diff.
- Get-Diff first-run classification: with the scratch companion root containing neither `state\` nor `current.json`, both peek and consuming entry points exit 1 with exact `[missing-current]` remediation and do not create `state\` or `consume.lock`. With `state\` present but current absent they produce the same class. Delete `state\` only after a successful consuming preflight and require the resulting lock-path race to map `[lock]`, preserving the deterministic-first-run versus concurrent-race distinction.
- Digest structure and provenance: table-driven malformed fixtures independently remove or mistype every required 6.1 field, add an unexpected non-`extra` field, violate each money, non-empty-id, uniqueness, and subset invariant, and supply non-canonical timestamps. Null leaves and arrays beneath `extra` remain valid. Assert the exact caller mapping from 7.1. Separate fixture mappers mis-stamp `source.saveFile` and `source.savedAt`, and independently mis-stamp `source.byteLength` and `source.contentSha256`, while producing otherwise valid digests; pipeline and walk both fail `[digest]` before commit or comparison. The successful helper result returns the observed source identity, and commit guard plus bookkeeping use that returned value. Then use core-valid but DE-invalid digests: an empty skill array, an integer thought id, a wrong skill group, a non-humanized task title, and a failed task status. Each passes `Read-DigestDocument` but fails `Assert-AdapterDigestDocument`. Exercise every trust boundary: fresh mapper output and historical walk input fail `[digest]`; current target reads take their documented missing, self-heal, or freshness route; baseline becomes content-invalid; producer envelope validation fails `[protocol]`; and host guidance rejects transport-valid nested state before injection. Unexpected validator invocation failure follows each boundary's validator-infrastructure route rather than being misclassified as invalid content.
- Diff structure and pairing: table-driven fixtures independently remove or mistype every required 7.2 field, add every possible unexpected field level, violate each null, status, ordering, uniqueness, overlap, presence, canonical-empty, and structurally recomputable `hasChanges` invariant, and exercise all three legal `extraChanges` presence cases. A non-reset standalone diff whose only mutation flips `hasUnrepresentedChanges` remains structurally valid by design; the same mutation is rejected by `Assert-DiffSemantics` when supplied its baseline, target, and adapter configuration. Producer-side fixtures cover every omitted-field projection member, matched and one-sided skill/objective keys, vital presence, highlights set order, effective and ineffective exclusions, and both change Booleans together. Every generated invalid document maps through its producer-specific failure class. Envelope fixtures pair a valid diff with a different state sharing the same `generatedAt` but a different source receipt; `Read-TurnContextEnvelope` rejects it `[protocol]`.
- Watcher quiescence: with no new save between polls, the watcher runs zero cycles, observed as `last-processed.json`'s own LastWriteTime being unchanged (an observable section 8 commit guarantee; a successful cycle always rewrites it), proving the last-processed round-trip compares equal. Probed 2026-08-08 across 14 seconds at a two-second interval: cycle count 0, state fingerprint unchanged, and SaveGames fingerprint unchanged. The pre-fix probe recommitted the same save 17 times because implicit JSON date conversion plus string parameter binding discarded fractional ticks; the exact-string reader and provenance regression close that failure mode.
- JSON callable, exactness, and depth: exercise both `Read-CompanionJson -Path -Budget` and `-Json -Budget` with object, array, scalar, and null roots. Each returns exactly one `CompanionJsonValue`. Fixed-schema fields are validated independently against their declared kind and range in each view that consumes them, with `RootElement` authoritative for numeric validation. For arbitrary numeric leaves and opaque subtrees, `RootElement` is authoritative and `Materialized` need not preserve the exact numeric value. Syntax, duplicate, depth, encoding, and materialization failures throw `CompanionJsonParseException` with the original parse exception retained; missing, denied, and injected file reads propagate their original I/O exception unchanged. A generic value at depth 64 succeeds and depth 65 fails; a duplicate property at depth 64 still fails before materialization. A maximum-depth-62 digest embeds into a maximum-depth-63 diff and then a maximum-depth-64 envelope without loss. A digest at depth 63 is rejected at its own boundary. The committed digest and diff round-trip, and a deepest known leaf such as `extra.reputation.copStyle.sorry` remains a number, never a stringified collection. Production-source inspection asserts that no Companion JSON writer uses `ConvertTo-Json` and that `ConvertTo-CompanionJson` emits through `Utf8JsonWriter`. For every budget profile, accept each exact UTF-8-byte, token, and decoded-text limit and reject cap plus one before `JsonDocument` or PowerShell materialization. Use both a depth-2 giant string and a wide shallow array. `-Path` never reads beyond byte cap plus one; `-Json` rejects from the throwing encoder's `GetByteCount` before encoded allocation. Supply isolated high and low UTF-16 surrogates separately; each throws `CompanionJsonParseException` with `EncoderFallbackException` preserved and never materializes U+FFFD. Every bounded producer accepts its cap and fails closed at cap plus one through its named serialization route. Pin the 2026-08-09 retained high-water observations used for tuning: 3,059,759-byte complete dump, 55,242-byte digest, and 2,735-byte diff; the current complete dump remains below 16 MiB.
- Schema mirror: extract sections 6.1 and 7.2 from this file and their counterparts in `core/digest-schema.md`, normalize only the files' final newline, and compare the resulting strings case-sensitively. The check fails on any content, order, case, or internal whitespace difference.
- Case-colliding read: the mapper successfully reads a real dump (whose variable store contains the known case-colliding key pair) end to end; a plain `ConvertFrom-Json` without `-AsHashtable` demonstrably throws on the same document.
- Commit-guard robustness: with `current.json` replaced by garbage bytes, one `Update-CompanionState.ps1` run commits a fresh digest, exits 0, and notes the self-heal on stderr. Separately retain valid newer current S2, delete its save so older S1 is selected, and inject a commit-guard open or read infrastructure failure. The cycle returns `failurePhase: commit`, leaves current and bookkeeping byte-identical, and a successful retry guard-discards S1 rather than regressing current. In a separate rotation fixture, give current S2 a valid version, game, domain, and source identity but remove required `character`; then delete the S2 save so S1 is the newest remaining candidate. The guard's prefilter sees S2 as newer, complete admission rejects it, and S1 commits with `pipeline-current-self-healed`. Repeat with a core-valid but DE-invalid S2. Neither case may write guard-discard bookkeeping or become watcher quiescent. Validator infrastructure failure instead preserves S2 and fails closed at commit.
- Stale-baseline recovery: consume S2 into a valid baseline, corrupt current, remove the S2 save, and let a cycle self-heal current from remaining predecessor S1. Consumption exhausts as `[stale-target]` with baseline and every other authoritative state file unchanged, and the host directs a fresh in-game save rather than offering an automatic reset. Publish a new save S3 whose content represents the reloaded state; the next transaction consumes the ordinary reverse content diff from baseline S2 to S3 and advances baseline normally.
- Reset-on-read: after consuming `Get-Diff.ps1`, the diff file contains the canonical empty diff (both change Booleans false) and an immediate second consuming run prints the canonical empty diff. A `-Peek` run between two saves prints the same diff as a consuming run would, and a subsequent consuming run still shows those changes.
- Baseline read classification: run the complete three-outcome by three-caller matrix. For the pipeline cache writer, consuming operation, and peek, test absent, bytes-read content-invalid, and infrastructure-failure baselines. Absent and content-invalid reset to the target for comparison; pipeline writes canonical empty without changing baseline, consuming emits canonical empty then advances baseline, and peek emits canonical empty without mutation. Infrastructure failure makes pipeline write the cautionary canonical-empty cache while retaining its committed cycle outcome, but consuming and peek return `[compute]` with no usable stdout and byte-identical state files. Cover no-share, access-denied, injected stream failure, and a typed file-not-found race, and assert the exact stdout, baseline, cache, warning, and class for every matrix cell. The access-denied case begins at the first open decision and proves that no Boolean existence probe can reclassify it as absent.
- Diagnostic cache interleaving: pause a consuming refresh after it samples current A, let a pipeline commit B and write cache A to B, then let the consumer replace the cache with its sampled empty A to A result. Assert that the cache may finish falsely empty, while baseline A and current B remain authoritative and the next consuming or peek computation still reports A to B. No runtime, watcher, or guidance decision may read the cache in this fixture.
- Source identity: create two fixture saves with identical LastWriteTimeUtc and names that sort differently by ordinal comparison. The watcher trigger, commit guard, and turn-context floor all select and retain the ordinal-descending winner; a digest for the other filename is not accepted as fresh merely because its timestamp ties. In a two-process consuming fixture, let baseline advance to the ordinal-higher identity while a lower equal-timestamp target waits for the lock; the waiting caller exits `[stale-target]` without stdout or baseline regression. Invoke `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek` against the same newer baseline and older target; it also exits `[stale-target]` without stdout or mutation. Two barrier fixtures then pause peek immediately after its baseline snapshot: one advances baseline before the stale comparison and proves the comparison uses the frozen snapshot without a second read; the other advances it just after the comparison and permits advisory output from that snapshot. Both assert zero state mutation and no dependency on acquiring `consume.lock`.
- Turn-context pairing: a successful bridge run emits exactly the 7.5 field set; its top-level adapter and game fields equal both nested objects, its diff target generation equals the state generation, all diff source-receipt fields equal the state receipt, its baselineReset boolean matches the diff null test, its cache-degraded field matches the selected structured result, its warnings satisfy the exact enum/detail/order contract, and its target source identity meets the emitted floor. Every exit-0 fixture has empty stderr, including those with non-empty warnings. Begin one successful run with invalid `current.json`, let a concurrent writer repair it before the cycle commit guard, and assert the envelope still contains exactly one deduplicated `pipeline-current-self-healed` warning from the initial gate. Mutate only `refresh.attempts` to 4 with coherent attempted and commitObserved Booleans; `Read-TurnContextEnvelope` rejects it against the three-pass ceiling.
- Serialized cycle publication: watcher W acquires `cycle.lock`, accepts receipt H1 for identity I, and pauses before publication. Replace the source bytes with H2 while preserving name and LastWriteTimeUtc, then start explicit one-shot U. U remains blocked before source acceptance; W publishes H1 and releases; U re-enumerates, accepts H2, publishes it, and returns. Assert H2 remains current and no H1 cycle can publish afterward. Separate fixtures prove a second watcher skips rather than waits, one-shot and bridge return `[busy]` after the 300-second wait under injected live contention without mutation, lock-path errors map `[lock]`, and no path holds `cycle.lock` while acquiring `consume.lock`. In a separate barrier schedule, publisher A resolves adapter bytes C1 and pauses before the lock; publisher B resolves changed bytes C2, publishes under C2, and releases; A then acquires the lock. A's post-lock receipt confirmation returns `adapter-config-changed`, publishes and bookkeeps nothing, and leaves B's current byte-identical. Repeat with invalid or unreadable confirmation bytes for `adapter-config-confirmation-failed`, and cover watcher skip plus one-shot and turn-context `[config]` routing. A whitespace-only edit also changes the conservative exact-byte receipt. For both late confirmation reasons, the turn-context diagnostic clears the previously selected source identity and emits null lock stage, source, and generation provenance as the explicit `config` exception. Build trusted C1 and C2 fixture packages under separate immutable `releases\<canonical adapterVersion>\schema-<schemaVersion>` roots, each with the companion-shaped mapper and validator subtree plus release-local core module. When adapter.json switches after C1 passed confirmation, C1 finishes only from its held C1 root; when it switches before confirmation, C1 stops and C2 alone may publish. Repeat for the dump executable, mapper script, digest-validator module, each vendored DB, and mapper-imported module. Mutating the authoring tree or sibling package does not affect C1, and a complete walk uses one held release root for every pair. Separately select a new schema-qualified child under the same adapterVersion and prove a core schema upgrade can reprocess without an adapter token change. Runtime tests consume prebuilt packages; online installer publication, recovery, and arbitration are explicit anti-goals of this design. After one-shot lock acquisition, independently make the second enumeration empty or fail, make each gate sample vanish or become unreadable, and exhaust stability. Assert the exact closed pre-cycle class and reason, exit 1, no cycle invocation, byte-identical pipeline and baseline state, applicable cleanup only, and lock release in every case.
- Turn-context freshness race: establish floor S1, freeze target S1, create newer save S2 before the post-target enumeration, and keep the watcher idle. The raised floor rejects S1, reacquires S2, and only S2 can reach consumption. Use a controlled during-enumeration insertion twice: when the enumeration stub returns S2, the raised floor requires S2; when it omits S2, S1 may be consumed and the next transaction must acquire S2 when no cycle publishes it before the target freeze. Add two schedules for a save created after the enumeration call returns: if no pipeline publishes it before stage 6, S1 is consumed and S2 remains for the next turn; if a serialized cycle publishes S2 before stage 6 reads current under `consume.lock`, S2 qualifies above the floor and is consumed in this turn. A publication after the locked read always remains pending for the next turn.
- Consumption lock: hold `consume.lock` from one fixture process while invoking each consuming entry script from another. Both second callers exit `[busy]` after the bounded wait with no stdout and unchanged baseline; after release, each entry point can acquire the same lock and consume normally. Deny creation or open access without holding a competing handle and verify `[lock]`, not `[busy]`, with the same no-mutation result.
- Equal-identity target freeze: seed current receipt H1 at source identity I and pause consumer A before `consume.lock`. Force-refresh receipt H2 at the same identity, then let consumer B acquire the lock and publish baseline H2. Resume A. A must freeze H2 after acquiring the lock and may emit an empty diff, but baseline never transitions from H2 back to H1. The converse ordering, with H2 landing after A's locked H1 freeze, leaves H2 pending for B and also never loses the transition.
- Turn-context failure injection: fail private candidate construction before baseline mutation and assert `consumptionState: none`, empty process stdout, and unchanged baseline. Fail baseline serialization, temp write, and replacement separately; each returns a private valid candidate with `notConsumed`, and the wrapper alone starts one new attempt. Fail every post-baseline cache stage separately: fresh validation machinery, comparison, serialization, temp write, and replacement. Each returns a private valid candidate with `consumed` and `cacheDegraded: true`; fresh validation machinery uses exact reason `cache-target-invalid`, never pre-baseline `compute`. The wrapper selects it without a second consuming attempt. Fail attempt-level envelope serialization once after notConsumed and once after consumed; both return `[protocol]`, preserve the structured consumption state, emit no stdout, and do not retry. Finally fail the wrapper's one stdout write: it returns `[delivery]`, the host ignores any partial bytes, baseline remains advanced, and no retry occurs. A later ordinary acquisition repairs the cache. A fresh cache sample with mismatched schema, adapter, or game takes the cautionary canonical-empty fallback and succeeds when that fallback write succeeds. At every private failure boundary, inspect `ConsumeResult.detail` before terminal framing and require a valid class-owned registry value of at most 512 Unicode scalars. No exception message, path, command line, child stream, or arbitrary JSON value may enter it. Inject adapter-validator infrastructure failure at fresh mapper output, initial target, preflight target, locked-final target, baseline, pipeline cache, post-baseline fresh cache target, commit guard, and producer envelope. Require the exact registry owner and reason defined for each in-process boundary. Initial and failed-preflight `compute` diagnostics carry the selected save identity with null generation; a locked-final failure carries the most recently admitted preflight provenance; baseline failure carries the final target provenance. The pipeline cache case returns `failurePhase: cache` with internal `cycle-cache` reason `adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed`, does not take the content-invalid fallback, and leaves baseline untouched. The post-baseline cache target retains `reset-write:consumed` / `cache-target-invalid`; commit guard retains `refresh` / `current-commit-failed`; producer envelope uses `protocol` / `adapter-validator-infrastructure-failed`. Separately fail host guidance adapter admission after transport-valid exit-0 envelope selection and require local `[protocol]`, host-only `consumptionState: consumed`, detail `envelope-invalid`, null diagnostic provenance, no state or diff injection, no retry, and immediate stop. Assert that raw payloads are immutable `CompanionJsonValue` objects and private payloads are `TurnContextPayloadCandidate` objects whose nested diff and state retain exact RootElement numbers. Attempts may change only CacheDegraded and the final warning collection before one serialization; string, hashtable, caller mutation, and post-serialization mutation probes are rejected. Raw mode succeeds with only Diff and rejects State or Context. Private mode requires non-null Diff, State, and an exact `TurnContextPayloadContext`; missing, wrong-kind, out-of-range, or cross-inconsistent context fails `[compute]` before baseline mutation. Empty warnings and false CacheDegraded are the candidate's initial values. Separately inject raw payload serialization and raw stdout-write failure in `Get-Diff.ps1`; both preserve state, while their exact emit reasons are respectively `payload-serialize-failed` and `stdout-write-failed`. Force the shared locked target read to return every freshness reason and assert the legal ConsumeResult tuple is `freshness`, `none`, null payload, false cacheDegraded, and matching detail.
- Acquisition retry taxonomy: fixtures exercise every 7.5 failure class. Only typed stability, typed gate disappearance, vanished-or-changed copy source, below-floor freshness, and stale-target failures trigger another pass within the three-pass acquisition budget; permanent failures make one initial pass. No internal attempt writes process stdout. Only wrapper exit 0 emits a complete envelope; `[delivery]` may leave partial bytes that are ignored. Remove the candidate during each of `Test-FileWriteStable`'s two metadata samples and assert retryable `vanished`; access denial at either sample is non-retryable `unreadable`. A child flood fixture writes at least 8 MiB concurrently to each redirected stream; the fixed pumps retain no child text and memory remains within their two fixed buffers. Inject a read fault independently in each pump, require process-tree termination and exact `command-transport-failed`, and prove no promised output is accepted afterward. A parent exits after spawning a descendant that retains both pipe writers; the command deadline remains active after parent exit, then bounded teardown closes the readers and returns without awaiting EOF forever. Independently inject tree-kill failure, stream-close failure, and a pump that ignores cancellation through the drain grace; each returns `command-transport-failed` within the composite deadline plus five seconds. Interrupt the watcher during an active command and assert child cancellation, bounded cleanup, cycle-lock release, exit 0, and no adapter-failure diagnostic. In combined races, cancellation latched before an injected tree-kill, stream-close, or incomplete-drain defect returns internal `command-cancelled` and still exits 0 without an adapter diagnostic; timeout or pump fault latched first retains its ordinary result, and a later cancellation is handled only at the next watcher checkpoint.
- Host transaction selection: make private attempt A return `[reset-write:not-consumed]`, then land a newer save before the permitted new attempt. Pin the host deadline at 3,600 seconds and derive the six-pass explicit-wait subtotal as `6 * (300 + 2 * 120 + 10) = 3,300` seconds, plus 60 seconds of requested stability sleeps and 240 seconds of nominal headroom. The assertion also records that synchronous I/O and scheduling remain subject to the outer cutoff rather than a strict inner bound. Successful B consumes the newer target; only B reaches process stdout, and A is reduced and discarded before return. Make B instead return `[reset-write:consumed]`; B still reaches stdout with `cacheDegraded: true` and no third attempt. Make B also return notConsumed; stdout stays empty and the terminal summary is bounded. For success and both reset-write outcomes, inject missing JSON, malformed JSON, every missing or extra field, the wrong envelope version, and each broken cross-field invariant. All map `[protocol]`, emit no stdout, and perform no new consuming attempt. Include the composed case where valid A is notConsumed, B is consumed, and B then fails envelope validation: neither envelope is returned, the summary reports consumed, and no third attempt runs. In producer mode, omit mandatory `ExpectedCacheDegraded` and mismatch only that value in each direction against an otherwise valid structured attempt result. Each case is `[protocol]`, preserves the structured consumption state, selects no envelope, and starts no further attempt. Every fixture asserts process stdout, baseline and cache mutation, selected envelope, internal attempt count, cleanup warning, final class, and the exact seven-field bounded terminal record. Assert flat unbracketed tokens such as `[turn-context:protocol]` with JSON `outcomeClass: "protocol"`; nested forms such as `[turn-context:[protocol]]` are invalid. For every class, assert the exact `lockStage`, source-identity, and generation-timestamp presence permitted by its acquisition stage. Cycle-lock failures carry `lockStage: cycle`, the selected identity, and null generation; consume-lock failures carry `lockStage: consume` plus both validated provenance fields. Commit failure and an invalid cycle tuple produce `[refresh]` with the selected identity and null generation. A freshness failure may carry generation only after the target has passed `Read-AdapterDigestDocument` and the other target checks. Wrapper validation with `-ExpectedLockStage` and host validation without it accept and reject the same correctly framed corpus. Coherently wrong envelopes whose top level, diff, and state agree with one another but disagree with `AdapterConfig.adapterVersion`, `AdapterConfig.game`, or `Get-CoreSchemaVersion` fail `[protocol]` independently.

  Pin the wrapper retry floor reset in both directions. After A observes S2 and returns notConsumed, remove S2 and make current S2 missing, invalid, or version-stale; B may establish floor S1 and consume S1; B emits S1 as its selected-attempt sourceFloor and carries none of A's refresh counters or non-operational warnings. Transaction cleanup and reaper warnings remain the bounded exception. With the S2 save removed but current S2 still valid, B consumes S2 instead and cannot downgrade.

  Exercise every class and reason in the complete detail registry. Legal atom sets succeed only under their owning reason and in registry order. Unknown or cross-class reasons, extra or misordered atoms, noncanonical integers, unsupported atom types, and arbitrary string atoms fail. Paired same-class fixtures retain distinguishable reason tokens for unreadable versus version-stale freshness, cycle-lock versus consume-lock failure, dump nonzero exit versus promised-output omission, and each reset-write stage. Adapter-version mismatch fixtures carry `-1`, `2147483648`, and both signed Int64 endpoints through expected and actual atoms without truncation; schema-version fixtures remain within their narrower valid domain. Sentinel paths, save values, command text, exception text, and child-stream content never appear in emitted details. Producers never create a detail over 512 scalars; manually constructed 513-scalar detail and 4097-byte terminal records are rejected. When optional atoms make the complete record exceed 4096 UTF-8 bytes, final budget reduction removes complete trailing atom clauses and leaves valid JSON with a valid reason token. Instrument terminal serialization and `Read-TurnContextDiagnostic` to require the `config` writer and reader profile respectively; no unbudgeted `Read-CompanionJson` call is permitted.

  Corrupt an in-memory attempt result after independently arranging no mutation and an actual baseline mutation. In both cases the wrapper emits one `[protocol]` `attempt-result-invalid` diagnostic with `consumptionState: unknown`, null provenance, no envelope, and no retry; it never guesses from the malformed fields. For invalid exit-1 framing, independently arrange underlying `none`, `notConsumed`, and `consumed` outcomes. Each becomes a host-only `[protocol]` result with `consumptionState: unknown`, no envelope injection, and no retry. Malformed exit-0 output becomes host-only `[protocol]` with `consumptionState: consumed`. Independently inject process-start failure before creation and assert host-only `consumptionState: none`. After creation, inject non-0-or-1 exit, composite deadline, wait failure, one capture-pump failure, process-tree termination failure, and incomplete drain. Each completes within the deadline plus grace, injects nothing, never retries, and reports host-only `consumptionState: unknown`, including variants terminated before and after an instrumented baseline advancement. Independently emit exactly 33,554,432 stdout bytes and exactly 65,536 stderr bytes, then one byte more on each stream. Exact caps reach ordinary protocol validation; cap-plus-one latches overflow, retains no later bytes, terminates within the grace, and reports host-only `[protocol]` with unknown consumption. Inject a final validated-entry stdout failure after a nonempty prefix. The entry emits one `[delivery]` `stdout-write-failed` diagnostic with consumed state; the calling host rejects the partial stdout, injects nothing, and never reruns the transaction. `Read-TurnContextDiagnostic` accepts wrapper-emitted `unknown` only for the exact attempt-result-invalid tuple and rejects it everywhere else. The host does not trust provenance from rejected bytes. Instrument the tool transcript and filesystem to prove rejected and superseded full envelopes are never logged or persisted and are unreachable after return.
- Source-copy outcomes: mutate the selected save's timestamp or length between the before-copy and after-copy observations and verify disposition `changed`; remove it and verify `vanished`; deny read access without changing it and verify `unreadable`. With an injected small `maxSourceBytes`, accept an exact-cap source, reject metadata cap plus one before destination creation, and independently grow or misreport the file past the cap during the initial source hash, streaming copy, work-copy hash, and final source hash. Each excess stops at cap plus one, never writes the extra copy byte, invokes no adapter command, cleans partial work, and maps to nonretryable `unreadable` with source-owned reason `source-size-limit-exceeded`; the DE production value is exactly 1,073,741,824. Mutate bytes without changing length and restore the timestamp; the source or work-copy SHA-256 mismatch still reports `changed`. Independently inject a truncated work copy while the original metadata remains unchanged and verify that length mismatch reports `changed`, sets retryable true, and never invokes an adapter command. Each helper failure is a `SaveDigestStageException` whose `Stage`, `SourceDisposition`, `Reason`, typed `Atoms`, and inner-error presence match section 7.4; neither consumer parses or renders its internal message. Every copy-stage reason validates once under canonical detail owner `source`; the walk uses that typed reason only for classification and emits exactly the bare `find-span: [copy]` primary line, while the cycle maps it to `failurePhase: source`. No duplicate `copy` registry owner exists. Exact-line failure fixtures reject every primary-frame suffix before considering the optional warning line. Only `vanished` and `changed` set retryable true. Successful copy preserves `stable`, returns identical source and work hashes, passes the complete receipt to the mapper, and rejects any digest that alters one receipt field. Instrument comparison to prove file name, canonical savedAt, and content hash use ordinal equality, byte length uses numeric equality, and domainId is checked separately against held configuration rather than the four-field receipt. For each of the three source passes, a held share-friendly reader permits a concurrent writer open and a move; the metadata and hash checks still map resulting disagreement to `changed`. A restrictive `FileShare.Read` control proves the same operations would be blocked and is not used by the helper. Prime the actual enumerated `FileInfo` metadata cache, replace the scratch source before the first hash, and prove independent refreshed observations detect the replacement rather than pairing its bytes with the cached identity.
- Work-file allocation and reaping: freeze wall-clock time and invoke `Invoke-SaveDigest` twice in one process against the same source and role stem. The operations receive disjoint 32-lowercase-hex nonce path sets and lease files despite sharing a time and process. Through an injected nonce source, pre-create one selected destination with sentinel bytes and force that nonce; `FileMode.CreateNew` fails with the typed stage result, every sentinel remains byte-identical, and no destination is overwritten. Separately give a scratch source an mtime older than the reaper cutoff, pause the streaming copy immediately after its destination becomes visible, run the work-file reaper concurrently, and assert that the fresh destination survives. Resume the copy and verify its bytes and hash, verify the digest receipt retains the source's original old `savedAt`, and verify each successful result still owns an existing digest path and an exclusively held lease until disposal. Disposal is idempotent and leaves no paths from either operation. A failure before result construction performs the same cleanup inside the helper. Age an active canonical group's lease and every member beyond one hour while its exclusive lease handle remains held. Run full and `-TmpOnly` reapers from every startup context and prove the complete group survives without a warning. Release the lease without cleanup and prove the same aged group, including a mapper-style private sibling nonce, is removed on the next reaper run. Hard-kill the mapper after its sibling temp appears: parent cleanup removes only known outer paths and its lease, the private sibling remains as an unleased `work-` orphan, and startup reaping removes it after that file becomes age-eligible. Without restarting the watcher, repeat a precommit hard-kill cycle until the first private orphan becomes age-eligible; the next serialized cycle's steady-state reaper removes it and carries any cleanup degradation through the cycle warning channel. Give a detached descendant one real group output path, let command teardown return, and write both before and after an aged-group reaper pass. No promised output is accepted from the failed command, no state is published, unrelated files survive, and reaping stays bounded. A file recreated after enumeration is treated as an unleased orphan and survives until its own mtime crosses the one-hour threshold. Reaper boundary fixtures age lowercase unleased `work-owned`, uppercase `WORK-preserve`, one exact state temp for each allowed target, and near-matches with uppercase suffix, short, uppercase, or non-hex nonce, unknown target, and directory shape. Only lowercase work files and exact canonical state-temp files are removed; every near-match and directory survives in both full and `-TmpOnly` modes as applicable. Independently fail `.tmp` enumeration, `state` enumeration, one candidate metadata read, and one non-sharing lease probe. Each run returns exactly one deduplicated cleanup warning, skips only the affected root, entry, or group, and continues processing every other candidate. A sharing-violation lease probe skips the live group without warning. `.tmp\` and `state\` scans at exactly 65,536 entries and 16,777,216 UTF-8 filename bytes, then at each cap plus one. An overflowed root stops immediately, receives no deletion or lease probe from its retained prefix, returns exactly one `pipeline-reaper-discovery-limit-exceeded` warning naming `tmp` or `state`, and leaves every file untouched because a lease may lie beyond the cap; the other root still completes with independent counters. A second run repeats the same persistent warning until a stopped-process fixture reduces the named root below both limits, after which ordinary lease-aware reaping resumes. `-TmpOnly` never begins the state scan. Route enumeration and metadata failures through watcher, one-shot, walk, and turn-context startup, asserting each caller's documented rendering or envelope channel.
- State publication temp lifecycle: inject write, flush, close, and final-move failures independently. Each ordinary failure best-effort removes its exact nonce temp, and an immediate same-process retry uses a different nonce and publishes successfully. A forced cleanup failure leaves only that owned orphan; a retry and two concurrent same-process writes use disjoint CreateNew temps, never truncate one another, and publish only complete documents. In a forced nonce-collision fixture, writer A creates, closes, and pauses with temp T before publication; writer B selects T, fails `CreateNew`, and leaves T byte-identical because it never acquired ownership.
