A then publishes successfully. Repeat with a pre-existing sentinel T and prove the failed writer neither deletes nor changes it. A recently created orphan whose nonce happens to coexist with a later process is irrelevant because a non-owner never cleans it.
- Acquisition-domain isolation: build two scratch adapter directories with equal `game` and `adapterVersion`, and two save directories whose newest files share name, timestamp, and size but differ in bytes. Switching adapter or save selector changes the derived domain, invalidates old current and bookkeeping state, clears a watcher sample, requires two new-domain observations, and extracts the new bytes in watcher hot reload, watcher restart, and turn acquisition. Identical normalized ordinal path spellings derive the same domain; case-only spellings conservatively derive different domains; and sibling paths differing as ordinary `s` versus U+017F derive different domains, while a changed exact saveGlob also derives a different one. No persisted document contains either absolute path.
- Post-acceptance equal-identity limitation: accept receipt H1, replace source bytes with H2 while preserving file name and LastWriteTimeUtc, and assert watcher and ordinary turn acquisition retain H1 without hashing the equal-identity candidate. An explicit `Update-CompanionState.ps1` run reprocesses H2, and the equal-identity commit guard permits that forced replacement. This fixture pins the anti-goal rather than claiming automatic discovery.
- Post-commit pipeline failure: inject failures in the pipeline cache and `last-processed.json` writes after a qualifying current commit. The bridge revalidates and consumes that target with the matching structured warning and empty successful stderr; a pre-commit dump or digest failure emits no envelope and does not consume.
- Guard-discard bookkeeping failure: arrange for a newer qualifying current digest to make the cycle discard its candidate, then fail `last-processed.json`. The legal bookkeeping outcome revalidates and consumes that newer current target with a structured bookkeeping warning; `refresh.attempts` increments, `refresh.commitObserved` remains false, and the outcome does not map to `[refresh]` merely because `completed` is false.
- Cycle outcome closure: exercise every terminal row in section 8 and assert all ten fields exactly, including null `detail` on every success and a registry-valid `detail` on every failure. Cache and bookkeeping rows use only their named internal registry owners, never an attempt class; qualifying bridge revalidation reduces each to the matching null-detail warning, while failed revalidation emits only freshness detail. Exercise each legal warning producer alone and in reachable combinations. Feed representative unlisted tuples to the bridge boundary, including an unknown null-detail `pipeline-typo` warning; validation rejects it before any consuming call or baseline read. Other representative unlisted tuples include completed commit without cache, cache failure with `cacheWritten: true`, and dump failure with a non-stable source disposition; each maps to non-retryable `[refresh]`. Exercise absent and content-invalid pipeline baselines as the two cycle-only notes: watcher and one-shot render the exact note, while turn context sets `baselineReset`, omits both notes before envelope warning validation, and retains every other warning. Configure one diff-unaddressable exclusion and require pipeline step 5 to return its `diff-exclude-path-unused` record with pointer detail through the cycle warning sink. When turn-context consumption independently produces the same record, composite `(code, detail)` deduplication leaves exactly one envelope warning. Produce reaper overflow followed by `no-saves`, and a dump failure followed by denied helper cleanup. Each terminal diagnostic preserves its primary class and carries the exact bounded operational warning subset after disposal. Force notConsumed A to record cleanup degradation, then let B succeed and independently fail; the same transaction warning appears once in selected B's envelope or the final diagnostic. Unknown codes, pointer warnings, duplicate or unsorted records, and wrong detail shapes fail terminal diagnostic validation. Traverse 65,536 uneventful walk candidates with the same unresolved maximum-size exclusion on every pair. The walk retains and renders exactly one warning after all results are disposed; stderr never scales with pair count. On successful and failed walks, inject persistent reaper overflow and require the optional final `find-span-warning:` array to validate and reach host remediation before result or failure branching. The walk accepts reaper detail `tmp` and rejects otherwise identical `state`, while pipeline and turn-context fixtures retain `state` as valid. Unknown codes, wrong details, duplicate or unsorted records, malformed JSON, extra stderr lines, invalid UTF-8, and exact 16,777,216-byte stderr cap plus one all map to local `[protocol]`; exact-cap valid framing reaches ordinary validation. The outer supervisor independently accepts exact-cap valid stdout, rejects stdout cap plus one, bounds simultaneous stdout and stderr floods, and discards both captures on process-start, pump, wait, abnormal-exit, or teardown failure. An injectable short composite deadline covers a stalled walk and a parent exit whose descendant retains stdout; process-tree termination, stream closure, cancellation, and drain finish within deadline plus five seconds, no partial result is used, and guidance takes the current-state-only fallback. At each watcher cancellation checkpoint, assert the typed cancellation unwind returns no cycle outcome or failure diagnostic, including after current or a derived replacement commits. Inject cleanup deletion failure during that unwind: the internal cancellation signal freezes exactly one cleanup warning after disposal and lock release, and the watcher renders it before graceful exit 0. Deny deletion of one work path after committed success and after guard-discard success; each run retains its original successful outcome and exit code, logs one cleanup caution, and leaves the orphan for the startup reaper. Repeat cleanup denial after an earlier source, dump, and digest failure; each retains its original failure phase, disposition, retryability, bridge mapping, and exit code while adding only the cleanup caution. Separately deny deletion of an aged startup orphan. The reaper returns one typed cleanup warning and writes no stream; turn context still returns a valid envelope with empty stderr and exactly one `pipeline-cleanup-degraded` entry, while watcher, one-shot, and walk entry scripts each render the warning and continue their ordinary startup path. Instrument the caller-owned sink directly: helper cleanup before result construction preserves the primary exception while appending one record, and `SaveDigestResult.Dispose` appends the same record without throwing. Cycle and walk freeze or render warnings only after disposal, including walk failure and watcher cancellation paths. Instrument every failure mapping to prove it consumes typed `Reason` and `Atoms` and never reads an exception message.
- Configuration snapshot: pause after the wrapper resolves adapter configuration, edit adapter.json, and resume. Attempt A, a forced notConsumed retry B, candidate-envelope validation, and selection all use the same original object; a digest from the changed configuration cannot be accepted under it. The held snapshot is neither serialized nor logged and is unreachable after return. Carry the selected C1 envelope through host `-TransportOnly` validation after adapter.json becomes C2: the host accepts the structurally and internally valid C1 bytes without rereading config, while the next wrapper invocation observes C2 and follows the new configuration. In the guidance phase, independently change game, adapterVersion, and source domain after C1 delivery; each identity mismatch skips trigger evaluation and walk startup, discloses local configuration drift, injects or uses no C1 state or diff, produces no brief or answer, and stops. A control edit changing only `ambientExtraPaths` while the identity trio stays equal remains eligible and uses the fresh presentation policy. Changing `diffExcludePaths` also bumps adapterVersion; the resulting mismatch follows the same disclosure, no-injection, and stop path, and the next acquisition reprocesses under the new version rather than reinterpreting C1's diff. Seed baseline B and deliver consumed C1 target S. For transient fresh-config resolution or host adapter-admission failure that leaves C1 active, require host-only `consumptionState: consumed`, detail `envelope-invalid`, null diagnostic provenance, no injection or retry, then require the next transaction to compare S to S without replaying the B-to-S notice. For a retained game, adapterVersion, or source-domain mismatch, require the same consumed disclosure and no injection, then reprocess under C2 and take the documented baseline-reset path rather than a literal C1 S-to-S comparison. That reset also does not replay the B-to-S notice. Exercise validator-module ownership in a clean process. Fail every selected-profile non-module check and prove no import occurred. Then fail after first import through stream output, missing or extra export, and injected snapshot-construction failure; each removes the exact provisional instance or poisons its path and a retry never reuses it. During provisional cleanup and final release, make `OnRemove` independently emit output, emit a warning, throw, and leave the instance loaded. Every stream is captured and discarded; each defect leaves a poisoned tombstone, never changes the established primary result, and makes later acquisition fail `adapter-release-corrupt`. Hold two successful snapshots concurrently, prove they share one approved module with reference count two, dispose them in both orders, and require one clean unload only after the final idempotent release. Repeat for watcher failure and retry, the post-lock confirmation snapshot, and a second immutable release path; no disposed callable remains usable and process exit is not needed for cleanup.
- First-run: with `state\` empty, `Get-Diff.ps1` exits 1; after one `Update-CompanionState.ps1`, consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` prints the canonical empty diff.
- Commit guard: feeding the pipeline a save older than `current.json`'s source (simulated by processing the Day 3 save after the Day 4 digest is current) leaves `current.json` untouched and exits 0 with the guard's stderr note.
- Atomic replace: a reader loop (using the pipeline's own share-friendly read) parsing a state file continuously while 1000 write-temp-then-`[IO.File]::Move(overwrite)` cycles rewrite it observes zero read failures, and the writer completes all 1000 moves (live-claim: probed 2026-08-07; result written=1000, ok=205577, bad=0. The original formulation with a default-sharing reader CONTRADICTED the claim - the writer, not the reader, failed on sharing violations - which produced the 7.1 sharing/retry rules). Unit fixtures inject each replace failure category. Only an `IOException` carrying Win32 sharing violation 32 retries, using exactly five total attempts with delays 40, 80, 120, and 160 milliseconds; the fifth failure is rethrown. Other I/O failures and `UnauthorizedAccessException` fail after one attempt. A sharing handle released before a scheduled retry permits publication, and a handle retained through the fifth attempt leaves the original destination byte-identical.
- Save-write stability: with the game running, observe at least one autosave being written and confirm the two-sample size-stability gate never dispatches a copy that then fails to unzip; pass condition is 0 unzip failures across a play session that produces at least 5 saves (live-claim: probed 2026-08-17; result save events=5, stable archives=5, live autosaves=1, unzip failures=0; the monitor began at 23:42:08 UTC and the retained autosave was written at 23:55:22 UTC).
- Watcher sample identity: after poll P0 records `(N,T1,L)`, rewrite fixture N to different bytes with equal size L and timestamp T2. Poll P1 replaces the sample without dispatch; only an unchanged P2 dispatches. A control preserving all three metadata fields pins the documented heuristic limitation and still exercises receipt checks during any explicit refresh. In two additional sequences, P0 records one tuple, P1 respectively returns zero matches or throws during enumeration or metadata read, and P2 sees the original tuple. P2 is a new first observation in both cases; only an unchanged P3 may dispatch. Independently keep name and timestamp equal while changing the resolved domain; the full bookkeeping tuple mismatch triggers a new-domain cycle. Inject a `current.json` infrastructure-read failure with otherwise matching bookkeeping: the watcher emits a caution, clears its sample, skips that poll, and retries the read later, while missing or content-invalid current still authorizes the documented self-healing cycle.
- Watcher graceful interruption: cancel independently during the idle interval, configuration read, enumeration, stability wait, cycle-lock wait, each source copy or hash pass, an active adapter command, immediately before current commit, immediately after current commit, and during cache and bookkeeping writes. Every case bypasses the poll-failure logger, releases lock and lease ownership, completes within the command teardown bound when a child exists, and exits 0. Configuration-read and enumeration fixtures return their injected synchronous call before asserting the post-call cancellation checkpoint; they prove cooperative observation, not hard cancellation of a blocked operating-system call. Pre-commit cases publish no current; post-commit cases preserve the complete current and leave only derived state for the next startup to repair. No case renders an adapter-failure diagnostic or leaks an owned temp.
- Historical-save immutability: while exercising every supported manual, autosave, quicksave, overwrite or rotation mode and enabled cloud synchronization, retain hashes, sizes, and timestamps for older matching candidates across later saves. Confirm none changes and separately confirm every artifact that is rewritten in place remains outside the configured saveGlob. The live session covered three manual events, one quicksave, one autosave, overwrite or rotation behavior, and operator-confirmed cloud synchronization; older matching candidate mutations=0 and observed non-save in-place rewrites=0. The retained manual archive at 23:45:48 UTC survived the later quicksave and autosave, and the retained quicksave at 23:47:20 UTC survived the later autosave, establishing at least three older-candidate survivor comparisons against the autosave at 23:55:22 UTC (live-claim: probed 2026-08-17).
- Read-only guarantee: after a full watch cycle, every file under `SaveGames\` has an unchanged LastWriteTime and hash sample.
- Test-UneventfulDiff (7.4): canonical empty diff is uneventful; sessionChange-only is uneventful; `hasUnrepresentedChanges: true` by itself and combined with other otherwise-uneventful surfaces is uneventful; extraChanges confined to an ambient prefix (both the exact prefix and a nested leaf under it) is uneventful; decoded reference-token boundaries hold, and escaped slash, tilde, dot-containing, and empty tokens compare without collision; any objectives bucket entry, any nonzero character delta or skill change, any inventory change, and any non-ambient extraChange are each eventful; with an empty ambient list every extraChange is eventful; ambient extraChanges COMBINED with an eventful section are eventful; a path differing from an ambient prefix only in letter case is eventful (the match is case-sensitive, 7.4); and the production idle shape, sessionChange POPULATED TOGETHER WITH ambient-prefixed extraChanges (a sleep flag flipping alongside the clock), is uneventful. The host trigger resolves one guidance-phase config and uses its ambient set rather than a DE literal. Change ambientExtraPaths after envelope creation but before this resolution and assert trigger behavior follows the new held snapshot. Change it again after trigger evaluation but before walk production; the held snapshot remains the validator input and `walkPolicyId` mismatch produces the local protocol fallback.
- Get-SaveCandidates ordering (7.4): the same-timestamp ordinal fixture pair asserts the full sorted order (ordinal name descending on ties), Get-NewestSave still returns its head (regression for the extraction), and on zero glob matches Get-SaveCandidates returns an empty array (safe to enumerate under strict mode) while Get-NewestSave returns `$null` (its documented contract). Startup rejects a relative `saveDirectory`. A scratch directory with a case-variant direct-child match, a nested would-be match, and a bracket-expression pattern proves case-insensitive PowerShell `WildcardPattern` matching against direct-child `FileInfo.Name` only. Instrumented `Get-Diff.ps1` startup validates and hashes the configured directory path without observing its existence, never enumerates or opens a save file, and never touches `.tmp`.
- Get-SaveCandidates enumeration failure: against a fixture save directory that exists (startup validation passes) but whose ACL denies directory listing (a deny-ACE applied for the test and removed afterwards), the exported helper THROWS rather than returning an empty set, and the walk exits 1 with a `find-span: [config]` stderr line and NO stdout JSON (an errored read is not an empty history; regression for the removal of the shipped enumeration's silencing error action). With injectable low ceilings, accept the exact direct-entry and cumulative-name-byte limits and throw `SaveCandidateLimitException` on the next entry before glob filtering or metadata retention. Nonmatching entries consume both budgets. Assert watcher skip, one-shot and turn-context `[source]` / `candidate-limit-exceeded`, and walk `[config]`, with no path treating the excess as an empty history. The production constants are exactly 65,536 entries and 16,777,216 UTF-8 name bytes.
- Ambient walk-past (integration): the walk is adapter-agnostic, so a FIXTURE ADAPTER whose digestCommand is a stub script emitting synthetic schema-conformant digests keyed by save name constructs pair content to order. Three fixture saves whose synthetic digests make the FIRST pair differ in BOTH an ambient-prefixed extra leaf AND a leaf under a path listed in the fixture adapter's diffExcludePaths, with the SECOND pair eventful, discriminate each flow separately: with the fixture adapter.json as configured the walk returns the second pair (found:true, walkedSaves 3; both mechanisms flow into the per-pair comparison); with ambientExtraPaths emptied it stops at the first pair (the ambient leaf is now progress); with diffExcludePaths emptied instead it also stops at the first pair (the formerly excluded leaf is now visible). The stub also prints a junk line to ITS stdout, and every variant's walk stdout still parses as exactly one JSON object (the section 8 child-stdout redirect holds).
- ambientExtraPaths validation: a non-array value, an invalid or non-canonical JSON Pointer, a pointer not rooted below `/extra`, and an ordinal duplicate each make every acquisition-profile entry point exit 1 at startup, matching the diffExcludePaths rules there. State-only Get-Diff treats the known acquisition-only member as opaque and still consumes a compatible state pair. The DE adapter.json is asserted to declare `ambientExtraPaths: ["/extra/dayPhase"]` (a config-read check, so the live trigger actually tolerates dayPhase flips rather than defaulting to `[]`).
- Relative executable resolution: with `WorkingDirectory` set to the adapter directory, separator-containing relative `dump-tool\dedump.exe` fails process start, while the normalized absolute path starts the same executable and reaches its ordinary missing-input exit. Both contexts use `UseShellExecute = false`. (live-claim: probed 2026-08-09)
- Find-LastEventfulSpan boundHit fixtures (all fixture runs supply `-CompanionRoot` pointing at a SCRATCH COMPANION TREE built so the adapter commands can actually execute there: `adapters\<name>\adapter.json` names a scratch save directory and its held schema-qualified artifact root contains the complete companion-shaped package, with the mapper under `adapters\<name>`, its vendored data beside it, the dump executable beneath that adapter subtree, and `core\DigestDiff.psm1` at the package root. The packaged mapper's `..\..\core\DigestDiff.psm1` import therefore remains inside the held release. Core resolves relative argv[0] and the child working directory against the outer adapter directory; the scratch `.tmp\` receives the work files. This is the parameter's stated purpose and its verification; fixture saves are byte-copies of a real save under distinct names with controlled mtimes, so identical-content pairs diff canonical-empty): empty directory and a single save each produce exit 0, found:false, boundHit "exhausted", walkedSaves 0; two identical-content saves (the whole directory) with `-MaxSaves 2` produce boundHit "exhausted" with walkedSaves 2 (the candidate set was consumed); THREE identical-content saves with `-MaxSaves 2` produce boundHit "saves" with walkedSaves 2 (one candidate remains, so the wider re-run offer is honest); two saves whose mtimes sit further apart than `-MaxAgeHours` (relative to the newest, per 7.4's anchor) produce boundHit "age" with walkedSaves 0 and NO work files created (the metadata-decided fast path); THREE saves with cumulative spacing (t0, t0 minus 1.5 h, t0 minus 3 h) at `-MaxAgeHours 2` produce boundHit "age" with walkedSaves 2, discriminating the NEWEST-candidate age anchor from a previous-candidate anchor (the third save is 3 h from the newest but only 1.5 h from its predecessor; an anchor-to-predecessor implementation would keep walking) and exercising a mid-walk age stop after work files were created and cleaned; `-MaxSaves 1` emits exactly the null-detail `walk-max-saves-clamped` record through the final warning array and behaves as 2, and `-MaxAgeHours 0` analogously emits exactly `walk-max-age-hours-clamped` and behaves as 1. Both records compose with other walk warnings in the one sorted, deduplicated warning line. CLI binding fixtures prove `1.5` becomes Int32 2 before clamp, while `NaN`, `2147483648`, and `-2147483649` fail parameter binding before startup or clamp diagnostics and emit no script-owned `find-span:` frame. The host classifies that unframed exit 1 as local `[protocol]` and falls back to current state alone. Producer traversal and semantic replay both accept `Int32.MaxValue` without exception and make identical decisions for exactly one hour, one hour plus one tick, and the full synthetic `DateTime.MinValue` to `DateTime.MaxValue` span. The same cases apply to watcher `-IntervalSeconds`. The Int32.MaxValue fixture enters the real `Wait-WatcherInterval`, observes its first bounded chunk, cancels, and exits gracefully with no poll-failure diagnostic; a single-poll binding-only harness is insufficient. Boundary instrumentation proves the helper never passes more than 86,400 seconds to its wait primitive and never converts the complete interval to Int32 milliseconds. The never-touches-`state\` invariant is verified by TWO discriminating checks (a single absence check cannot see the reaper half, whose `state\` sweep is a silent no-op when the directory is missing): after every ordinary fixture run, NO `state\` directory exists under the scratch root (catches a walk calling the two-directory `Initialize-CompanionDirs`); and one dedicated run against a scratch root whose `state\` is PRE-SEEDED with an over-an-hour-old `<target>.<32-lowercase-hex>.tmp` orphan asserts the orphan SURVIVES the walk untouched (a reaper run without `-TmpOnly` would delete exactly that file); the same run pre-seeds an over-an-hour-old `work-stale-probe.json` in the scratch `.tmp\` and asserts it is GONE afterwards (the walk actually calls the reaper; an implementation skipping the call entirely would pass every other check). The mirror half guards the pipeline without touching live companion state: after resetting the scratch save directory, stage one valid save, pre-seed the scratch `state\` directory with the same aged orphan, snapshot the real project `state\` tree, and invoke the real `Update-CompanionState.ps1` entry point with `-CompanionRoot` set to the scratch root. The run must remove the scratch orphan, create scratch `state\current.json`, and leave the real-state snapshot byte-for-byte and metadata-for-metadata unchanged (the pipeline reaper must keep sweeping `state\` orphans; a default-on `-TmpOnly` or a dropped state-sweep branch would silently disable killed-writer recovery). The scratch tree and all pipeline state it contains remain scoped to the suite's outer `finally` cleanup.
- Find-LastEventfulSpan found:true fixture (the primary success path): fixture setup selects two real saves O and N whose current-mapper digests differ eventfully, with O older and N newer by fixture mtime. Setup asserts the pair is eventful and fails loudly if it is not, so rotated save history cannot turn a sourcing mistake into a walk failure. A directory holding O, N, and a byte-copy of N as the newest candidate, all three stamped with fixture mtimes minutes apart (well inside the default `-MaxAgeHours`, so the age bound cannot fire), produces exit 0, `found` true, `boundHit` null, `walkedSaves` 3, `span.from` naming O and `span.to` naming N with `savedAt` values equal to their fixture mtimes in 'o' format (orientation pinned: baseline is the older save), and an eventful `diff`. The newest pair (N copy vs N) is canonical-empty and is walked PAST. Because this fixture's only eventful pair is also its last, it cannot discriminate the stop rule; a FOUR-file variant does: O, N, an O byte-copy, and an N byte-copy stamped in that mtime order (minutes apart) put an eventful pair (O copy to N copy) FIRST in the walk, and the asserted result is found:true with walkedSaves 2 and the span naming the two NEWEST files; an implementation reporting the last eventful pair or an aggregate would walk on to the older eventful pair and emit walkedSaves 4 with a different span.
- Find-LastEventfulSpan fail-closed: in a fixture directory whose SECOND-newest zip is byte-truncated, the walk exits 1 with a `find-span: [dump]` stderr line, prints NOTHING on stdout because the failure occurs before emission, and leaves no `work-walk-*` files in `.tmp\` (finally-scoped cleanup on the failure path). An injected output-stream failure after accepting a valid prefix exits 1 with `[emit]`; the caller buffers through EOF and discards that prefix because exit 0, complete JSON, and output validation were not all observed.
- Walk-result protocol: pass every producer result through `Assert-WalkResultSemantics`, serialize it once, pass those exact bytes through `Read-WalkResultDocument`, and emit those bytes without reserialization; every captured exit-zero document passes the document validator again with one held validated `AdapterConfig`. Inject serialization failure and require `[emit]` with no stdout. Mutation fixtures add and remove each root, provenance, or span field, change every JSON kind, use walkedSaves 1 and values above EffectiveMaxSaves, violate each found/boundHit/null cross-state, use age at EffectiveMaxSaves, reverse the span identities, supply a reset or uneventful diff under the supplied ambient set, put each directory separator in `span.from.saveFile` and `span.to.saveFile` independently, and make span.to disagree with diff.currentSource. Put a valid B-to-T selected diff beside a different older A and a final receipt naming B; producer semantics reject the selected-pair mismatch. The same dayPhase-only found result is rejected as uneventful when that path is ambient and accepted when the held ambient list is empty. Change only held `diffExcludePaths` and prove found-mode semantic acceptance follows the recomputed suppression and `hasUnrepresentedChanges`, not a separately supplied ambient list. Both found and dry results carry exact root provenance. Replace every serialized domain consistently with a different valid hash, change only game, adapterVersion, or walkPolicyId while keeping the other provenance stable; each is rejected against the held adapter snapshot. In found mode, independently make root provenance disagree with the nested diff or span and require rejection. Empty-directory, lone-save, and immediate-age results use dry mode with empty receipt evidence and no separately supplied Baseline or Target parameters. In found mode, independently mutate a legal `walkedSaves` value, substitute a non-adjacent selected pair, omit a preceding receipt, make a preceding receipt eventful, and cross each saves or age stop; replay rejects each before serialization. A three-candidate dry traversal whose result is relabeled from `saves` to `exhausted`, an altered age stop, an omitted evaluated eventful receipt, and any non-adjacent or identity-mismatched receipt each fail producer semantics before serialization. The private receipt factory refuses to mint until the pair's full diff has passed semantic and eventfulness validation. Instrument a 65,536-candidate dry traversal with small valid documents: at no point are more than two full digests and one full diff retained, no full document remains at dry completion, and only compact immutable receipts scale with pair count. Resolve producer and consumer snapshots around an intentionally mis-versioned same-version edit to `diffExcludePaths` and, independently, a conforming same-version edit to `ambientExtraPaths`; the changed derived `walkPolicyId` makes the downstream validator reject both found and dry results even when the selected diff remains eventful under both snapshots. With identical arrays, replace the walk-policy tag with its next literal: the derived id changes, and both found and dry documents stamped with the prior tag fail validation. Producer mutations exit `[protocol]` with no stdout; captured invalid exit-zero bytes produce the host's local `[protocol]` enrichment disclosure and current-state-only brief rather than a dry-result branch.
- Find-LastEventfulSpan stability gate: with a background writer appending bytes to the NEWEST fixture save every second for the duration of the run, the walk exits 1 with a `find-span: [unstable]` stderr line and NO stdout (the gate really runs and really gates: an implementation that skips `Test-FileWriteStable` copies the changing file and fails elsewhere or not at all, never with `[unstable]`); the helper's own two-sample protocol is additionally pinned by a unit check: a stable file returns `$true` within one sample interval (an elapsed-time bound pins the first-attempt pass), and a file whose writer keeps GROWING it across the WHOLE 5-attempt window returns `$false` (a single rewrite between the first two samples would legitimately pass on a later attempt; only sustained instability may assert the false outcome).
- Find-LastEventfulSpan live runs against the real SaveGames, one with defaults and one with substantially wider bounds intended to probe more of the idle stack and gap (e.g. `-MaxSaves 12 -MaxAgeHours 9000`): each run exits 0 and its stdout parses as one JSON object with `provenance`, `found`, `walkedSaves`, `boundHit`, `span`, and `diff` all present; when `found` is true the two `span` `savedAt` values equal the LastWriteTimeUtc ('o' format) of two ADJACENT enumeration candidates on disk; the two runs' found/boundHit/walkedSaves AND elapsed wall-clock time are recorded side by side (the pair measures whether wider bounds change the observed dry result on this machine's idle cadence and the per-candidate cost figure, feeding the default-sizing and latency rationale in 7.4) (live-claim: probed 2026-08-08; defaults: found=false, boundHit=age, walkedSaves=7, elapsedSeconds=12.1; wide: found=false, boundHit=age, walkedSaves=8, elapsedSeconds=11.3, so the wider run remained age-bound and did not establish full-gap coverage); no `work-walk-*` files remain in `.tmp\` afterwards; and every file under `SaveGames\` keeps its LastWriteTime (the read-only guarantee extends to the walk).
- Consumer ownership matrix (7.6): in a scratch companion root, each marker fixture (absent, valid `bridge`, valid `runtime`, unparseable, extra field, wrong version, unknown owner, and a file held open without read sharing) is crossed with each consuming caller (consuming `Get-Diff.ps1`, `Get-TurnContext.ps1`, `Publish-StateEvent.ps1`) and with and without one record file in the outbox. Every result equals its ownership-table row, and every failing row reports `consumptionState: none` and leaves `baseline.json`, `current.json`, the cache, and the outbox byte-identical. `Get-Diff.ps1 -Peek`, the watcher, the one-shot, and the walk succeed under every marker fixture. With the marker absent and no outbox directory, the complete existing Consumption and TurnContext groups pass unchanged, which is the bridge-parity proof.
- Consumer switch (7.6): each row of the transition table from each reachable root state, including rerun idempotence and the `previousOwner` each result reports, and `release-switch-invalid` for both switches together and for either switch with `-Owner runtime`. The abandon release from a root holding one valid and one invalid pending record leaves one `abandoned` tombstone, one `quarantined` tombstone, one quarantine entry with the invalid record's exact bytes, no record file, no baseline, and marker `bridge`; the next `Get-TurnContext.ps1` then reports `baselineReset: true`. Terminating the process after each write of the abandon row, from roots whose marker is `runtime`, absent, and `bridge` in turn, and then rerunning the same command, always ends with no pending record and no baseline, and the next bridge read reports `baselineReset: true`. The same holds for a root whose pending records are all invalid, under an absent and under a `bridge` marker, killed after the step-6 quarantine ledger write. From an absent and from a `bridge` marker with an empty outbox, `-AbandonUndelivered` with the baseline present deletes it, writes no ledger entry and no marker, and reports `changed: false`, `baselineRemoved: true` and `abandonedCount: 0`; with the baseline already absent it reports `baselineRemoved: false`; and `-DeliveryConfirmed` or neither switch on such a root changes nothing.
- Outbox commit and crash states (7.6): fault injection at record serialization, temp write, commit move, baseline apply, cache comparison, and cache write produces exactly the durable-state table's row and the documented outcome. A commit whose target name already exists fails `record-commit-failed` and leaves the existing file byte-identical. After an injected apply failure, a second `Publish-StateEvent.ps1` first rewrites the baseline from the pending record and then either reports `unchanged` or commits only a genuinely newer transition, never a second record for the first. Terminating the child at an injected checkpoint between commit and apply yields the same recovery. One `outbox-write:not-consumed` triggers exactly one in-process retry, and a second ends exit 1 with no record.
- Record kinds (7.6): a changed target commits `consumed`; an unchanged target returns `unchanged` with no file written and, with `-RequireRecord`, commits `snapshot` while `baseline.json` keeps its bytes and `LastWriteTimeUtc`; an absent and a content-invalid baseline each commit `consumed` with `baselineReset: true` and null `baseline`; `-Resync` commits `resync`, and a second `-Resync` with the same `-RepairOperationId` returns `replayed` both while the record is pending and after it is tombstoned `delivered`, while a resync whose bytes became producer-invalid is quarantined by the second `-Resync`, which then commits a fresh `resync` under the same identifier, and a second `-Resync` whose reconciliation quarantines another pending record also commits a fresh `resync` rather than replaying; and a target older than the baseline is `stale-target` in every mode. Every committed record passes `Read-StateEventRecord` in both modes, and table-driven negative fixtures remove, add, or mistype each root field and break each kind invariant. Every receipt reports the running `schemaVersion` and the held `adapterVersion` and `game`.
- Settlement (7.6): each row of the settlement table, including `settlement-out-of-order` for a later record while an earlier one is pending and its success after the earlier one settles, and `settlement-quarantined` for a record quarantined by a `-Resync` both with a trusted identity (an `adapterVersion` the held configuration no longer has) and with an untrusted one (nested content the core rejects), while the same two tombstones under another `-RecordSha256` answer `settlement-conflict` and `settlement-unknown` respectively. A crash injected between the ledger write and the file delete, followed by any outbox operation, removes the file and leaves exactly one tombstone. Settling the newest record after an injected apply failure first applies the baseline.
- Reconciliation guards (7.6): a settled record whose file delete is made to fail on every attempt is never pending while its tombstone exists, and its tombstone survives age and count pruning under small injectable bounds. After the ledger is then deleted, the rebuilt ledger carries a fresh incarnation, the revived record is superseded and never written over the newer baseline, its repeated settlement succeeds, and the next commit's identifier differs from every identifier issued before. Terminating a repair between its fencing ledger write and the quarantine move leaves the sequence fenced: the next reconciliation completes the move and the next commit never reuses that sequence. In the equal-source variant, a settled record and a later settled replacement of the same source identity, the earlier file's delete failing and the ledger then deleted, leave the later baseline in place, while an equal-source record whose apply failed is still applied over its earlier baseline, including when its `state.generatedAt` precedes the baseline's because the clock stepped backwards.
- Version change (7.6): with one pending record written under core schema version N, a core reporting N+1 fails every outbox operation as `outbox-record-invalid` with no mutation, until one `-Resync` quarantines that record's exact bytes and commits a `resync`. With one pending record whose `adapterVersion` differs from the held configuration, ordinary publishes and settlements proceed, and one `-Resync` quarantines it the same way.
- Outbox constants (7.6): the production constants are asserted as exactly 180 days, 512 tombstones, 16 pending records, 64 quarantine entries, and 1,024 enumerated children, and the seventeenth ordinary pending publish is `outbox-full` with no mutation, while a `-Resync` publish at 16 pending records commits its record.
- Receipt independence (7.6): with stdout closed, and with the child killed immediately after the commit move, the record file is present and passes structural validation, so a consumer that only lists `state\outbox\` still observes the transition.
- State-event acquisition cost (7.6): three consecutive `Publish-StateEvent.ps1 -RequireRecord` runs against a scratch companion root seeded from the live `state\`, with current state already fresh, record wall-clock time per run. The figure sizes the runtime's per-interaction acquisition and decides whether skipping unchanged acquisitions or a persistent core host is worth designing (live-claim: not yet probed; 7.6 is unimplemented).

## 12. Anti-goals (current design)

- No FOW parsing, no `containerSourceState` or `kubujussState` mapping, no conversation table consumption (spoiler surface, no guidance value).
- No white-check detail beyond counts.
- No multi-game orchestration or long-running companion app in this layer; the user has flagged a future "single companion app with per-game adapters" direction, so the core/adapter seam (adapter.json + command templates) is kept clean, but no further packaging work now. The long-running process belongs to the [companion execution runtime](companion-execution-runtime.md), and 7.6 is this layer's only runtime-facing surface.
- No delivery to multiple logical Companion consumers: consuming `Get-Diff.ps1` and `Get-TurnContext.ps1` share one exclusive lock and one baseline writer, but a diff consumed by one is not replayed to the other. The durable runtime journal is the multi-client delivery mechanism: under runtime ownership (7.6) the core hands each transition to exactly one runtime journal and still delivers to no second consumer itself.
- No new-playthrough detection: starting a fresh game produces mass removals, negative deltas, and a backwards clock in one diff, all governed by the "loaded timeline wins" rule; distinguishing "new playthrough" from "loaded an old save" is deliberately not attempted in the current design.
- Saves are assumed to be single files (the glob matches files, `{save}` is one path); folder-shaped saves in some future game would extend the seam additively and are out of scope until such a game exists.
- No automatic discovery, within one unchanged acquisition domain, of a post-acceptance source rewrite that preserves both file name and LastWriteTimeUtc. The accepted receipt still proves which bytes were mapped; an explicit one-shot refresh can discover replacement bytes, but every-poll hashing of equal identities is outside this version.
- No writing to saves, ever, in any future iteration of this layer.

## Hardening
